Sources: The Australian Museum, "Interactive: Frog life cycle, from egg to adult", <https://australian.museum/learn/teachers/learning/frog-life-cycle/> ("Most tadpoles hatch from eggs as fully aquatic larvae"; "They breathe through gills and use a long tail to swim"; "Hind legs emerge first, followed by front legs"), for the first two sentences; and National Geographic Kids, "Axolotl", <https://kids.nationalgeographic.com/animals/amphibians/facts/axolotl> ("Axolotls eat whatever they can get their jaws on, including mollusks, worms, insect larvae, crustaceans, tadpoles"), and Animal Diversity Web, Ambystoma mexicanum (Amy Majchrzak), <https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Ambystoma_mexicanum/> ("axolotls will eat anything that they can catch"; its animal foods list includes amphibians), for the third.

    The freshwater shrimp fact card (H4; open question 276) has the title "Freshwater shrimp" and the body "Freshwater shrimp are tiny animals with many legs. When something comes near, they flick their tails and zip backward. Axolotls hunt small water animals like these." Sources: The Australian Museum, "Freshwater Shrimp" (Paratya australiensis), <https://australian.museum/learn/animals/crustaceans/freshwater-shrimp/> ("five pairs of long thin legs"; it "uses a flick of the tail to move quickly and escape from predators"); Derwent Estuary Program, "Species of the Derwent", <https://www.derwentestuary.org.au/species/shrimp> (the glass shrimp Paratya australiensis has "a vigorous backward-flicking escape response"; "Freshwater shrimp live in freshwater creeks and streams"), for "zip backward"; and San Diego Zoo Wildlife Alliance Library, "Diet & Feeding - Axolotl (Ambystoma mexicanum) Fact Sheet", <https://ielc.libguides.com/sdzg/factsheets/axolotl/diet> (wild adults eat "Small crustaceans"), and National Geographic Kids, "Axolotl", <https://kids.nationalgeographic.com/animals/amphibians/facts/axolotl> (axolotls eat "crustaceans"), for the third sentence. Owner to verify: "tiny" (the Australian Museum lists a size of 7 cm); and the shrimp sources are about Australian species, not species of Xochimilco.
14. **C14** The Collection Book also holds fact cards, in this order:
    - One fact card for each hazard and predator in the area data, then one for each timing element type that carries a fact card, then one for each mobile snack type, area by area in area-table order (N2): within an area its hazard and predator cards come first (a moving hazard's after the still hazards'), then its element cards, then its snack type cards, the tadpole's before the shrimp's (issue #117; open question 276). Each is found once the player has met that hazard, predator, element, or snack type (A21, N3, H4; open questions 119 and 229). A new card joins the book only when some area in the area table has its hazard, predator, element, or snack type, so the common carp, punting pole, tadpole, and freshwater shrimp cards (C13) join it with the first phase 2 issue that adds each one to an area: the tadpole and freshwater shrimp cards joined with Area 2's mobile snacks (issue #121; open question 299), the punting pole card with Area 5's punting poles (issue #118; open question 317), and the carp card with Area 4's carp (issue #119; open question 340); since then the carp card with Area 2's carp (issue #132), so it moved to Area 2's cards, after the tilapia card (open question 380). Today that is the tilapia fact card, then the carp fact card, then the plastic worm fact card, then the tadpole fact card, then the freshwater shrimp fact card, then the heron fact card, then the ghost net fact card, then the punting pole fact card, then the garter snake fact card, then the water hyacinth fact card (C13; issues #103, #118, #119, and #132). A found card shows a picture only when it has Book art; today only the tilapia's does, and the tadpole, freshwater shrimp, heron, carp, ghost net, punting pole, garter snake, and water hyacinth cards have none (open question 159). An unfound card shows "???" and a hint that names the habitat of the first area, in area order, where its hazard or predator appears: "Explore the canal to find this card." for the tilapia, "Explore the lily pad pond to find this card." for the plastic worm, for the carp, whose first area is Area 2 since issue #132 (it was Area 4, with "Explore the chinampa gardens to find this card.", from issue #119), for the tadpole and the freshwater shrimp, whose first area is Area 2 (issue #121), and for the heron, whose first area, Area 3, is in the lily pad pond habitat, and "Explore the chinampa gardens to find this card." for the ghost net (issue #76), for the punting pole, whose first area is Area 4 (issue #119), and for the garter snake and the water hyacinth, whose first area, Area 5, is in the chinampa gardens habitat (issue #103). The ghost net card is found the first time the axolotl comes within 100 pixels of a ghost net's edge, outside the Home Spot (N3). The carp card is found the first time the axolotl comes within 180 pixels of a carp, outside the Home Spot (N3). The garter snake card is found the first time the axolotl comes within 180 pixels of a garter snake's head, the punting pole card the first time it comes within 180 pixels of a pole's sweep, and the water hyacinth card the first time it comes within 180 pixels of a mat pair's sweep, each outside the Home Spot (N3). The tadpole and freshwater shrimp cards are each found the first time the axolotl's centre comes within 120 pixels of an uncollected snack of that type, outside the safe area (H4).
    - The "Boy or girl?" fact card, found from the start, with no picture. Its text: "Grown-up boy axolotls are longer and slimmer, with a bigger bump by the back legs. Girls are rounder and plumper." Source: Animal Diversity Web (University of Michigan), <https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Ambystoma_mexicanum/>. Supporting: the Royal Veterinary College axolotl care sheet. The boy's longer tail fin (F2) is a drawn cue only, not a fact on the card.
    - Two refuge fact cards (N2, issue #74), each found in the refuge, with its own unfound hint and no Book art (open questions 182 and 183):
        - "Axolotl refuges", found on the first visit to the refuge. Its body: "In Xochimilco, people make safe canals for axolotls next to chinampa gardens. Filters of volcanic rock let water in but keep big fish like tilapia out." Unfound hint: "Visit the refuge to find this card." Sources: Tec Science (Tec de Monterrey), "How to Save the mexican Axolotl? Start by Restoring Their Home", 2025-01-31, <https://tecscience.tec.mx/en/biotechnology/how-to-save-the-axolotl>; and The Wildlife Society, "Creating refuges for axolotls in Mexico City", 2021-12-06, <https://wildlife.org/creating-refuges-for-axolotls-in-mexico-city/> (Zambrano et al., Aquatic Conservation, doi:10.1002/aqc.3520).
        - "Canal clean-ups", found the first time cleared trash is on screen while it is not found: after a clean-up plays, or on opening a refuge that already has a cleared batch, so older saves find it too. Its body: "People in Xochimilco pull rubbish, like plastic bottles, out of the canals. Keeping the water clean helps keep axolotls safe." Unfound hint: "Beat an area, then visit the refuge to find this card." Sources: EU Delegation to Mexico, "Clean-up activity in Xochimilco, Mexico City, as part of the #EUBeachCleanup campaign", 2021-09-27, <https://www.eeas.europa.eu/delegations/mexico/clean-activity-xochimilco-mexico-city-part-eubeachcleanup-campaign_en>; and AFP via The Jakarta Post, "Mexican kayaker on mission to clean up floating gardens", 2022-02-04, <https://www.thejakartapost.com/culture/2022/02/04/mexican-kayaker-on-mission-to-clean-up-floating-gardens>.
        - On a first visit both may show, "Axolotl refuges" first. The owner is to verify both cards' sources before release (open question 187). Their ids are saved with the hazards met (D2).
    - So there are 13 fact cards today, in this order: the tilapia, the carp, the plastic worm, the tadpole, the freshwater shrimp, the heron, the ghost net, the punting pole, the garter snake, the water hyacinth, "Axolotl refuges", "Canal clean-ups", and "Boy or girl?" (issues #103, #121, #118, #119, and #132). The section heading reads "Facts: N of 13 found". It read "Fact cards: N of M found" until issue #124 shortened it, so a two-digit count stays on one line at 320 x 440, where text never shrinks (X5); with 13 cards it still takes the room the 9-card heading took on every supported canvas (owner decision to fold issue #124 into issue #121's PR, 2026-10-24; open question 301). The tadpole and shrimp cards' ids are saved with the hazards met (D2), with no save change, like the others. The carp, ghost net, garter snake, punting pole, and water hyacinth cards' ids are saved with the hazards met (D2), with no save change (ADR 0012's additive rule).
    - Fit (issues #75 and #98; open questions 151 and 173): on a trip, a fact card's title wraps at a tight-padding width, the canvas width less twice the margin and the tight padding, from 120 to 420 pixels (404 pixels at 460 wide), so the title always shows. The body first wraps at the body width. Only when the body does not fit the canvas height there does it wrap at the title's tight-padding width instead, which takes fewer lines on canvases under 484 pixels wide. Today only the freshwater shrimp card (issue #121), the heron card, and the water hyacinth card (issue #103) at 320 x 440 do so. The heron body takes 9 lines at 240 pixels, which does not fit, and 8 lines at 264 pixels, which fits, using 397 of the 408 pixels of height. In the Collection Book, the "Collection Book" title hides only on a featured page whose entry does not fit under it; the heading then moves up and the list gains 47 pixels. Measured, it hides only on the found plastic worm card's page, at 320 x 440, 460 x 320, and 460 x 340, on the found heron card's page, at 320 x 440, 340 x 440, 460 x 320, 460 x 340, 480 x 320, and 480 x 340, and, since issue #103, on the found water hyacinth card's page, at 320 x 440, 460 x 320, 460 x 340, 480 x 320, and 480 x 340, and, since issue #121, on the found freshwater shrimp card's page, because of its body's length, at 320 x 440, 340 x 440, 460 x 320, 460 x 340, 480 x 320, and 480 x 340, and, since issue #118, on the found punting pole card's page, at 320 x 440, 460 x 320, and 460 x 340.
    - Fit of the refuge cards (issue #74): they add 2 pages to the Collection Book, so it has 28 pages at 1 row per page and 17 at 5 rows per page. The list of pages where the title hides does not change: the refuge cards keep the "Collection Book" title on every supported canvas. On their fact card, the closest fits are: "Axolotl refuges" 0.5 pixels to spare at 460 x 360 and 480 x 360 (padding 24), and 11 pixels at 320 x 440 (padding 12); "Canal clean-ups" 5.5 pixels at 460 x 340, 480 x 340, and 520 x 340.
    - Fit of the ghost net card (issue #76): on a trip, its title and body fit every supported canvas, the body at the body width; the heron at 320 x 440 is still the only card whose body wraps at the tight-padding width. In the Collection Book, the ghost net card, found or not, keeps the "Collection Book" title at 460 x 320 and 320 x 440, and the list of pages where the title hides does not change. With Poppy red and the ghost net card, the Collection Book has 30 pages at 1 row per page and 18 at 5 rows per page.
    - Fit of the garter snake and water hyacinth cards (issue #103): on a trip, both cards fit every supported canvas, as every area fact card does; the owner shortened the garter snake title to "Meet garter snakes" so it stays on one line and the card fits 320-tall landscape screens (C13). The water hyacinth body, like the heron's, wraps at the tight-padding width at 320 x 440 (above), and the garter snake body wraps at the body width. In the Collection Book, the title hides on the found water hyacinth card's page (above); the garter snake card's page keeps it on every supported canvas. With Plum and the two cards, the Collection Book has 33 pages at 1 row per page and 21 at 5 rows per page.
    - Fit of the tadpole and freshwater shrimp cards (issue #121; open question 299): in the Collection Book, the title hides on the found freshwater shrimp card's page (above); the tadpole card's page, and the unfound shrimp card's, keep it on every supported canvas, and the other pages where the title hides do not change. On a trip, the shrimp body wraps at the tight-padding width at 320 x 440 (above). With the two cards, the Collection Book has 35 pages at 1 row per page and 23 at 5 rows per page.
    - Fit of the punting pole card (issue #118; open question 317): on a trip, it fits every supported canvas, as every area fact card does, and its body wraps at the body width. In the Collection Book, the title hides on the found punting pole card's page (above); the unfound punting pole card's page keeps it on every supported canvas, and the other pages where the title hides do not change. The longest heading, "Facts: 12 of 12 found", takes the room the 9-card heading took on every supported canvas, so no other page hides its title (open question 301). With the card, the Collection Book has 36 pages at 1 row per page and 24 at 5 rows per page.
    - Fit of the carp card (issue #119; open question 340): in the Collection Book, the carp card's page, found or not, keeps the "Collection Book" title on every supported canvas, and the other pages where the title hides do not change. On a trip, its body wraps at the body width on every supported canvas. With the card, the Collection Book has 37 pages at 1 row per page and 25 at 5 rows per page.
15. **C15** First-find cards explain what each kind of treasure is for. There is one card each for the first Decoration, the first Wearable, and the first Paint shell, and each shows once per save (open question 94).
    - A kind's card is due when the player has banked at least one treasure of that kind and its card has not been seen. This is checked in the Home Tank right after the tank arrival card's OK, before the hub takes input (open question 93). So a save that already owns one sees the card once, after its next trip. Cards never show mid-trip, or when the Home Tank opens from the title screen.
    - Due cards show one at a time, in this order: Decoration, Wearable, Paint shell (open question 95).
    - Each card has a bold title, a short body, and the 32 pixel icon of the first banked treasure of its kind beside the title, with its rarity glow (B2; open question 98). The wording (open question 96):
        - Decoration: "You found a Decoration!", with the Decorations section line (C11) as its body.
        - Wearable: "You found a Wearable!", with the Wearables section line as its body.
        - Paint shell: "You found a Paint shell!", with the body "It unlocks a Dream colour: a magic paint for your axolotl. Try it in Restyle." On smaller canvases the body shortens to "It unlocks a Dream colour. Try it in Restyle.", and on the smallest the title shortens to "New Paint shell!".
    - The Decoration and Wearable cards have one "OK" button. The Paint shell card has "Try it now!" (primary, focused first) and "Maybe later", which closes the card (open question 97). "Try it now!" opens restyle on the Dream colour tab (F6, F14) for the explorer, or for the first owned axolotl when there is no explorer. Done or Cancel there go back to the Home Tank with that axolotl focused.
    - Every card button ignores presses for the first 0.8 seconds the card shows, so the press that closed the tank arrival card cannot press it. Tab, Shift+Tab, Left, and Right move the focus. Enter and Space press the focused button, ignoring key repeat. Escape closes the card. Touch works too. The cards appear and leave at once, with no motion, and fit 460 x 320 and 320 x 440.
    - A card is saved as seen as soon as it closes, by either button (D1). "Start over" erases the save, so the cards show again in the new game. With device storage off, the seen cards last only for the page session.
16. **C16** The Home Tank's Menu button folds and opens the hub's menu (C7), so the player can just look at the tank (issue #148; open questions 502 and 503).
    - Look: a secondary square tile, 65 x 64, with the menu icon, three bars, over its label: "Menu" while the menu is folded and "Hide" while it is open. It is one size for both labels, and it never becomes an icon-only tile, at any size, so it is always clearly labelled (X5).
    - Focus order: it is the last hub button, after Sound, and the axolotls come after it.
    - Spot: the bottom-left corner, left of the tank, inside the 16 pixel margins. Where the button there would overlap the tank box (C7), it goes in the top-left corner, above the lid. It is never in the top band. While the menu is folded, the Refuge nudge sits on the Menu button and reaches 3 pixels past its top-right corner, and the nudge is kept off the tank box too. The spot never depends on the focus, or on whether the menu is folded or open. Where neither corner is off the tank box at full size, the hub's tank shrinks just enough to free one (C7). The opened menu keeps a 12 pixel gap from the button and its nudge.
    - Folding: it hides the six actions, the name panel, the best-stars badge, and the focus caption. The Menu button keeps its place and the focus, nothing else moves, and the tank keeps its size. While the menu is folded, Tab and the arrows move the focus over the Menu button and the axolotls only, and tapping an axolotl still opens its choices card. Opening brings everything back, with the focus still on the Menu button.
    - Open and fold rules: when the Home Tank opens from the title screen or after a trip, the menu is open where it opens by default (C7) and folded elsewhere. A press folds an open menu and opens a folded one. Coming back from a screen a menu action opened (the explorer card, decorate mode, the Treasure Chest, the Collection Book, the refuge, or the Sound card) opens the menu with the focus on that action. Coming back from a screen an axolotl opened (its choices card, and Restyle from it) keeps the menu as it was. A resize or a rotation that changes the default takes the new default; one that keeps it changes nothing. The state is never saved (D2).
    - It shows wherever the hub shows, under the tank arrival card, the milestone cards, and the Sound card too, and hides with the hub. Its press, by touch or by Enter or Space, plays the button tap (S15) and nothing else.

### D. Save and load

1. **D1** Progress saves on the device by itself after banking, going home, being carried home, beating an area, finishing the character creator, any tank change, any outfit change, any rename or restyle, any Dream colour change, choosing the explorer, choosing an area on the area map (N2), any Collection Book unlock, and closing a first-find card (C15). There is no save button. Treasures banked at the Home Spot mid-trip are saved at once. A rescue banked mid-trip is saved when the trip ends (open question 57).
2. **D2** Saved: banked treasures (including owned Dream colours), the starter and rescued axolotls, each axolotl's name and look (real morph, applied Dream colour, boy or girl, face, gill style, spots, tail, body, age, and size), each axolotl's outfit, the chosen explorer, decoration placements, Collection Book unlocks, which first-find cards were seen (C15), whether the Dash pot tip was seen (B11), the stars earned for each area, the last area chosen on the area map (Area 1 by default; open question 112), the fact cards found, which are the hazards and predators met and the refuge fact cards found, in one list (C14; open question 119), and settings (none yet; reduced motion follows the device). Not saved: the Trip Bag, the position mid-trip, leg state, Shell blocks used this trip, the refuge's pending clean-ups and milestones (N2), and each area's trip memory: its trip seed, its eaten snacks, its stranded axolotl left waiting, and its lost treasures. Each area keeps that memory by area id, for this play session only (open questions 56 and 117). Which areas are unlocked, and how far the refuge is cleaned up, are not saved either: both come from the saved stars (N2). The look is plain data, with each option held by its stable id.
3. **D3** Closing or reloading and then reopening restores saved progress exactly. The game opens in the Home Tank, never mid-trip. Unbanked finds from an unfinished trip go back into their area and can be picked again (B4). They are not sure to be on the next trip after a reload.
4. **D4** Saves hold no personal data, never leave the device, and need no account or network, per [ADR 0007](../adr/0007-kid-safety-and-privacy.md).
5. **D5** If there is no save, the game starts fresh. An unreadable save never crashes the game, and the player can still play. The game starts fresh with no message. A damaged save is kept on the device until the next "Start over". A save from a newer version is left untouched and is never overwritten (open question 54).
6. **D6** Saves from an earlier version still load after an update.
7. **D7** "Start over" erases saved progress only after a two-step confirmation. The wording works for kids and for parents. It is a button on the title screen, shown only once an axolotl is owned (open questions 51 and 52).
8. **D8** A note that parents can read says progress is kept only in this browser on this device, and that clearing browser data erases it. It is on the first "Start over" card and in the README (open question 53).

### E. Updates

1. **E1** After a new version is deployed, a player who reloads or reopens the game gets it within one reload. Or they see a friendly "A new version is ready" prompt that loads it in one tap.
2. **E2** An update is never forced mid-trip. The prompt only appears on the title screen or in the Home Tank.
3. **E3** Updating never loses saved progress.
4. **E4** The version number is shown in small text on the title screen or in settings.
5. **E5** Any update check contacts only the game's own website, sends no player data, and fails silently when offline.

### F. Character creator and outfits

1. **F1** On first launch, and after "Start over", the character creator appears before the Home Tank. It goes step by step, one choice per screen, in 11 steps in this order: Colour, Boy or girl, Eyes, Mouth, Gills, Spots, Tail, Body, Age, Size, and Name. Each step screen has:
    - A heading "Step N of 11: ..." (for example "Step 3 of 11: Pick your eyes!").
    - Progress dots that show done, current, and to-come steps by shape and fill, not by colour alone.
    - A short helper line.
    - Every option of the step, all visible at once, as big picture buttons (up to 8 per step). Each picture button shows a small preview of that choice on the axolotl so far. The chosen one is marked by a thick ring and a tick. There are no arrows to flip through options. On the Boy or girl step, each picture button also shows a boy or girl sign as well as the axolotl and its label (F16), so Boy and Girl are never told apart by colour alone.
    - Back and Next buttons. The last step has Done instead of Next.
    - A "Surprise me" button.

    "Surprise me" on the first step, Colour, picks a whole new axolotl at random: Colour, Boy or girl, every look option, and a suggested name that fits the boy or girl (F17). It is never exactly the current axolotl. On every other step, "Surprise me" changes only that step's choice:
    - On a look step (Eyes to Size), it picks a different option of that step, each other option equally likely.
    - On Boy or girl, it always switches to the other one. If the name is a suggested name that does not fit the new boy or girl, it becomes a random suggested name that fits. A suggested name that fits, or a typed name, stays (F17; open question 109).
    - On Name, it picks a different suggested name that fits the boy or girl, ignoring capital letters. A typed name can be replaced by a suggested one.

    "Surprise me" always gives a different choice, because every step has at least two options. Nothing else changes: no other look option, colour, Dream colour, or outfit, and the step and the Outfit view stay. The focus is on "Surprise me" afterwards, because pressing any button focuses it. The helper line ends with one sentence that says what "Surprise me" picks on that step, for example "Surprise me picks new eyes." (open question 110). The player can then accept it or keep changing options. The creator starts with a random boy or girl, a random suggested name that fits it, the default look (open question 22), and the Leucistic colour. The creator ends in the Home Tank with the new starter axolotl. It still opens from one place (`openCreator` in `src/scenes/sceneFlow.ts`).

    Text and touch targets never shrink to fit (X5): targets stay at least 48 by 48 CSS pixels, and primary actions at least 64 pixels tall. When a step does not fit, it makes room in this order:
    1. The helper line hides.
    2. The picture button labels hide, and one caption names the focused or chosen option.
    3. The preview shrinks to 0.6 of its size.
    4. The heading drops its "Step N of 11:" start.
    5. The preview hides, and the chosen picture button is the preview (F3).
    6. (restyle only) The heading hides; the bold current tab names the step (F6).

    Every step fits the smallest canvases, 460 x 320 and 320 x 440.
2. **F2** The character creator offers:
    - Name: free text typed in the name box, or a pick from a list of suggested names (F17).
    - Boy or girl: exactly two choices, Boy or Girl. It is always set, and there is no way to skip it. A boy has a clearly taller, longer tail fin. A girl has a clearly rounder body and belly, and bigger eyelashes. The eyelashes are white-backed, so they read on dark morphs. A girl also always has a small pink heart on her near cheek, below and behind the eye. The heart is part of her look, never a Wearable, and takes no slot. Its ink outline is white-backed, so it reads by its shape in greyscale. A boy has no extra cue (issue #87). The boy and girl cues read at every size, on all eight real morphs, in colour and in greyscale. This replaces issue #52's rule that the cues are kept subtle (issue #58). The heart shows on every Girl picture, the Boy or girl step's picture buttons included. Collection Book portraits and silhouettes are boys, so they do not show it ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 6u; open question 128).
    - Eyes: happy, sleepy, determined, curious, or grumpy. Grumpy has a lid that slopes down toward the snout, with only the lower half of the eye showing, and a short, gentle brow angled down toward the snout. It looks sulky and cute, never angry. Its brow and its half-shown eye tell it apart from determined (issue #87; open question 129).
    - Mouth: smile, open "D", tongue-out, small "o", big grin, cat smile, smirk, or blep. A big grin is a wide, filled grin, wider than open "D". A cat smile is a "w". A smirk is a one-sided smile. A blep is a closed smile with a small tongue tip.
    - Gill style: fluffy, long, or short.
    - Spots: none, freckles, dots, or patches. Freckles are a cluster of many tiny specks on the shoulders. Dots are a few even, medium, round dots along the back and tail. Patches are two or three big, soft blotches over the back and tail. Spots stay off the head, and never look like morph marks (the Wild flecks, the Copper speckles, or the Piebald patches).
    - Tail: Short, Medium, or Long tail length.
    - Body: Slim, Regular, or Chubby. Each has a clearly different outline at every size and age.
    - Age: Baby, Young, or Grown-up. Age changes proportions only, never size. A Baby has a bigger-looking head, from a shorter body and tail, shorter gills, and stubby legs. A Grown-up has longer gills. The head and face anchors never move.
    - Size: Small, Medium, or Large (F4).
    - Colour: one of the eight real morphs: Leucistic, Wild, Golden albino, White albino, Copper, Piebald, Axanthic or Melanoid.

    Every combination of options reads in greyscale and keeps the eyes, the gill roots, and the Wearable anchors and keep-clear zones ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 6i) clear. Text about one axolotl says "he" or "she" to match its boy or girl, never "they" or "it".
3. **F3** A preview of the axolotl updates straight away whenever an option changes. Under reduced motion, the preview is a still pose. When a step has no room for the preview (F1), the chosen picture button is the preview. The options on a step can be told apart at a glance, in colour and in greyscale. The Spots step's picture buttons show the body and tail up close.
4. **F4** Size is for looks only. Large is the old Medium (scale 1.00), Medium is the old Small (0.85), and Small is new (0.72). The default size is Medium. Small, Medium, and Large axolotls have exactly the same hit area and the same speed, on trips and everywhere else. The tilapia hit area matches Medium for every size (see Tuning values). Boy or girl, tail, body, and age are for looks only too. The leg-loss puff, the regrowth twinkles, and the snack speed-up star sit on the leg root at every size, age, and body. The protected ring and the carry bubble scale with the size and keep their clearances. These are visual only (issue #49).
5. **F5** The starter's morph decides which morphs can be rescued: the other seven real morphs become the stranded axolotls (C2). The starter's morph cannot be rescued again.
6. **F6** In the Home Tank, the player can rename any owned axolotl and restyle its face (eyes and mouth), gill style, spots, tail, body, age, and size, by touch and by keyboard. Restyling uses the same step screens, picture buttons, options, and live preview as the character creator (F1 to F3), with these changes:
    - A row of tabs, one per step, replaces the progress dots and the Back and Next buttons. Each tab is at least 48 pixels tall. When the tabs do not all fit, 48 by 48 pixel arrow buttons page through them. The current tab is marked by a bar and bold text, not by colour alone.
    - Choosing an axolotl in the Home Tank, by a press or by Enter or Space on the focused axolotl, opens its axolotl choices card (issue #82; owner decisions 2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21). The Home Tank dims under ink at 0.45, and the dim takes every press. A centred white panel is titled with the axolotl's name. Its buttons come in this order: "Restyle" (pencil icon), which opens restyling on the Eyes step; "Outfit" (shirt icon), which opens the Outfit tab on its slots view; "Dream colour" (paint drop icon), shown only when at least one Dream colour is owned, which opens the Dream colour tab; and "Back" (left chevron). The buttons are square tiles (open question 134). Focus starts on Restyle. The arrow keys, Tab, and Shift+Tab move the focus, wrapping. Enter or Space presses the focused button, ignoring key repeat. Escape is Back. Every button ignores presses for the first 0.8 seconds the card shows, so the press that opened it cannot choose; Escape works at once (open question 135). The card appears and leaves at once, with no motion. Text and buttons never shrink, and the card fits 460 x 320 and 320 x 440 with its title. Back and Escape return to the Home Tank with that axolotl focused.
    - Restyling starts on the Eyes step. From the Paint shell first-find card's "Try it now!" (C15), it starts on the Dream colour tab.
    - The Colour and Boy or girl tabs open read-only screens. The colour and the boy or girl are fixed after creation and never change.
    - A restyle-only "Dream colour" tab comes right after Colour (F14). Its picture buttons are "Real colour" first, then each owned Dream colour in catalogue order. Each picture button shows that colour on the whole axolotl, its swatch, and its name label. The helper line says Dream colours are not found in nature. With no Dream colour owned, it shows only "Real colour", and the helper line says "Find Paint shells in the canal to get Dream colours!". It shows at most 8 picture buttons per page, with a "More colours" button that shows the next page, like "More names" on the Name step; "Real colour" is first on page 1. Its labels never hide (fit step 2 does not apply). When it still does not fit after the other fit steps, it shows fewer picture buttons per page. Done keeps the Dream colour picked. Cancel leaves without keeping it.
    - A restyle-only "Outfit" tab comes right after the Dream colour tab (C10, F8; open question 68). It opens on its slots view: one picture button for each of the eight slots, in F8 order, each with the slot name as its label. A worn slot shows the Wearable on the axolotl and its perk icon. An empty slot shows a dashed outline of the slot and a plus badge. Under them, the axolotl's outfit summary (F10), or, when that does not fit, one caption for the focused slot ("Head: Sun hat, Speed +4%" or "Head: empty"). Picking a slot opens its choices view: "None" first, then every banked Wearable for that slot in catalogue order. Each choice shows its name and its perk icon and text, and "On <name>" when another axolotl wears it. Picking a choice puts it on at once, and the view stays open. "Back to slots" returns to the slots view. A slot with no banked Wearable shows only "None" and the helper "No <slot> items yet. Find some on a trip!". It shows at most 8 picture buttons per page, with a "More slots" or "More wearables" button for the next page. With Area 5's Wearables (B3), a slot has up to 7 choices (Head and Back: None and six Wearables; issue #103). Its labels, perk icons, and perk text never hide (fit step 2 does not apply). When the choices view still does not fit after the other fit steps, more steps follow: 7. the tab row hides, and "Back to slots" moves above the picture buttons; 8. each label sits beside its picture instead of under it; 9. only when nothing before it fits, one choice a page, with "More wearables" beside the picture and the label block under them, wrapped at the band width (owner decision on issue #103, comment 6374010919). A label block under its picture wraps at 168 pixels. A label block beside its picture wraps at 168 pixels where that fits, else wider, up to the band width less 60 pixels (204 pixels at 320 x 440; open question 152). At 460 x 320 and 320 x 440 the choices view shows 1 choice a page, with "More wearables". At 320 x 440, with 2 to 7 choices, it takes fit step 9, the only canvas that does; with 1 choice ("None" alone) it keeps step 8. Every layout that fit before step 9 was added is unchanged. Done keeps the outfit, and takes each Wearable in it off any other axolotl that wore it. Cancel leaves without keeping it.
    - Every restyle step shows the axolotl in the outfit being chosen, in its preview and on its picture buttons. The character creator shows no outfit.
    - Done and Cancel are on every step. Done keeps the changes. Cancel and Escape leave without keeping any change. On the Outfit tab's choices view, Escape goes back to the slots view instead. From the axolotl choices card, Done or Cancel return to the Home Tank with that axolotl focused, not to the card.
    - "Surprise me" follows the F1 rule on the current tab: on a look tab it picks a different option of that tab, and on Name a different suggested name that fits the axolotl's boy or girl (F17). Nothing else changes. On the Colour, Boy or girl, Dream colour, and Outfit tabs (both Outfit views), "Surprise me" is hidden, with its place kept so Cancel and Done never move, and Tab does not reach it (open question 108). "Surprise me" never changes the colour, the boy or girl, the Dream colour, or the outfit (open question 77).

    An axolotl's real morph never changes. Only a Dream colour can be put on top of it (F14).
7. **F7** The Go exploring flow asks which owned axolotl goes exploring: the explorer. Pressing "Go exploring" in the Home Tank opens the explorer card, "Who's going exploring?" (open question 69). It shows each owned axolotl as a picture button, in its own look and outfit, with its name as the label, up to 8 to a page, with a "More axolotls" button for the next page. The chosen axolotl is marked by a ring and a tick, not by colour alone. Under the pictures is the chosen axolotl's outfit summary (F10). Three buttons follow:
    - "Go!" opens the area map (N2) with the chosen axolotl as the explorer. The trip starts when the player picks an unlocked area there, and Back on the map returns to this card with the same axolotl chosen and Go! focused (open question 116). Go! ignores presses for the first 0.8 seconds the card shows, so the press that opened the card cannot press it (open question 75).
    - "Change outfit" opens the restyle screen on the Outfit tab for the chosen axolotl (F6). Done or Cancel there returns to the card, with the same axolotl chosen.
    - "Back" closes the card and goes back to the Home Tank.

    With one owned axolotl, it is chosen already. The last explorer is remembered: choosing an axolotl saves it as the explorer (D1). When no explorer is remembered, or the remembered one is not owned, the first owned axolotl is chosen. The explorer is the axolotl the player swims as on the trip, shown with its own look and outfit.

    Text and touch targets never shrink. When the card does not fit, it makes room in this order: less padding, smaller pictures, the outfit summary rows side by side, fewer pictures per page, the title hides, the outfit summary hides, and then "More axolotls" moves beside the pictures, so the title and the summary come back if they fit. The names and the buttons never hide. The card fits the smallest canvases, 460 x 320 and 320 x 440. There, with long names and many perks, it shows 1 picture per page, with no title and no outfit summary (open question 76). "Change outfit" still shows the summary on the Outfit tab.
8. **F8** Each axolotl has eight Wearable slots:
    - Head: for example hats, flowers, and crowns.
    - Neck: for example necklaces and scarves.
    - Face: for example glasses. The eyes always stay visible.
    - Shirt: for example tank tops, T-shirts, sweaters, hoodies, and wetsuit tops. Torso only, with no sleeves on the legs, so leg loss never changes it.
    - Back: for example backpacks, bags, diving tanks, and shell packs.
    - Tail: for example bows and ribbons.
    - Socks: worn under footwear.
    - Feet: footwear, for example shoes, sneakers, boots, and flippers. One footwear item covers all four feet.

    Each Wearable fits exactly one slot. An axolotl wears at most one Wearable per slot, so items from different slots mix and match. Putting a Wearable into a filled slot takes the old one off and returns it to the Treasure Chest. Putting a Wearable on and taking it off work by touch and by keyboard, on the Outfit tab (F6). The axolotl choices card's "Outfit" opens it straight from the Home Tank (F6), and the explorer card's "Change outfit" still opens it too (F7).

    Layering goes from the body out: shirt, then neck item, then back item. An axolotl can wear socks alone, footwear alone, or both. With both, the sock shows above the shoe. When a leg is lost, its sock and shoe disappear with it. Both come back when the leg has fully regrown, never on a part-grown leg. A Socks or Feet Wearable's perk keeps working while at least one leg is left (open question 73). (Issue #16.)
9. **F9** Each Wearable has exactly one perk. The perk types are:
    - Speed: faster swimming. It raises the top speed by its percent.
    - Regrowth: lost legs grow back faster. It cuts each leg's regrowth time by its percent.
    - Dash: a longer Tail Dash. It makes each dash last longer by its percent. The recharge stays the same (open question 70).
    - Shell: blocks one hit per trip (A22).
    - Treasure sense: treasure hints appear from further away (B7).
    - Snack magnet: pulls snacks within its radius toward the explorer's head (A19; open question 71).

    A rarer Wearable has a slightly stronger perk and a fancier look. Shell perks appear only on Epic and Legendary Wearables.
10. **F10** A perk is shown as its perk icon and text, with its value, on the Wearable wherever the Wearable is listed (open question 74). Each perk type has its own icon shape. Each axolotl also has an outfit summary that shows the combined value for each perk type it wears, one row per perk type in F9 order. With no perk worn, it says "No perks yet". The outfit summary shows on the Outfit tab and on the explorer card. Perks are never shown by colour alone.
11. **F11** Each perk type has a hard total cap. When an outfit adds up past a cap, only the capped value applies, and the outfit summary shows that the cap is reached with a "max" mark after the value. Shell perks block at most one hit per trip in total, however many Shell perks the outfit has. Even a full Legendary outfit still loses a leg to every hit it does not block, and is still carried home after losing the fourth leg.
12. **F12** Perks apply only during a trip, and only from the explorer's outfit. Outfits on other axolotls have no effect. Perks never change the hit area, the telegraph, protected time, the snack eat reach, or the rule that one hit removes exactly one leg.
13. **F13** Wearables never cover the eyes, the gill roots, or a girl's cheek heart, in any slot, at any size, boy or girl, tail, body, or age, per the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 6i. A girl's eyelashes count as part of the eyes. Back items (for example the Diving tank) and Head items (for example the Sun hat) may reach past the body outline, so they show as shapes against the water. Every Wearable, now and every one added later, is big and bold enough to read at a glance as what it is. In the swim view, each worn item shows at least 64 square CSS pixels of its own visible fill, at every look and every swim phase, for a Small axolotl at 320 x 440 (0.684 CSS pixels per local unit). Visible fill is the item's fill not covered by its own ink or by a later layer. Each item also meets its slot's minimum sizes (6i). No item is excepted. When an item misses a minimum, its shape, zone, or layer changes, never the minimum. Worn items use the item ink, 2.0. Shirt and Tail items draw over the near gills. The Shell necklace is a collar: a thick cord with shells along the chin, inside the body outline (issue #88; open questions 130 to 133).
14. **F14** In the Home Tank, on the restyle screen's Dream colour tab (F6), which the axolotl choices card's "Dream colour" opens directly, the player can put any owned Dream colour on any owned axolotl, and take it off at any time, by touch and by keyboard. An axolotl shows at most one Dream colour at a time. One owned Dream colour can be on any number of owned axolotls at the same time, because it is a paint, not a single item. Putting it on one axolotl never takes it off another. A Dream colour combines with spots, and the spots draw on top of it. Taking it off returns the axolotl to its real morph. A Dream colour never changes perks, the real morph, or Collection Book unlocks. Dream colours are explained by the Treasure Chest's Dream colours section line (C11) and by the first Paint shell's first-find card (C15), whose "Try it now!" opens the Dream colour tab.
15. **F15** The 16 Dream colours are told apart from each other and from the real morphs in greyscale. Each patterned or sheen Dream colour (Hearts, Tie-dye, Iridescent, Rainbow, Galaxy, and Tie-dye galaxy) has its own distinct pattern cue (for example hearts, swirls, a sheen, or stars). Solid Dream colours (Mint, Sky blue, Lavender, Sunset orange, Bubblegum, Midnight blue, Lily green, Firefly gold, Poppy red, and Plum) have no pattern cue, so they are always shown with their name label next to the swatch. On the Dream colour tab's picture buttons (F6), every label always shows and each picture button shows its swatch. Wherever a Dream colour is named in the Treasure Chest or the Collection Book, it carries the label "Dream colour — not found in nature".
16. **F16** All new screens and controls (the character creator's step screens, picture buttons, and progress dots, renaming and restyling with its tabs, the Outfit tab, the Go exploring flow's explorer card, the axolotl choices card, slots, perks, the outfit summary, the Shell sparkle, and Dream colours) work under reduced motion, can be read in greyscale, and work by touch and by keyboard, per X2, X3, and X5. On the step screens, picture buttons, and tabs:
    - The focused control has a visible focus ring.
    - The arrow keys move between picture buttons. Enter or Space picks one.
    - Tab reaches Back, Next, Surprise me (only where it shows), and Done. In restyling, Tab also reaches the tabs and Cancel, on the Dream colour tab More colours, and on the Outfit tab More slots or More wearables and, on the choices view, Back to slots. On the Outfit tab's choices view, Escape is Back to slots.
    - On the explorer card, focus starts on Go!. The arrow keys move between the axolotl pictures. Enter or Space chooses the focused axolotl or presses the focused button, ignoring key repeat. Tab reaches the pictures, More axolotls, Go!, Change outfit, and Back. Escape is Back. The card appears and leaves at once, with no motion.
    - On the axolotl choices card, focus starts on Restyle. The arrow keys, Tab, and Shift+Tab move through Restyle, Outfit, Dream colour (only where it shows), and Back, wrapping. Enter or Space presses the focused button, ignoring key repeat. Escape is Back. The card appears and leaves at once, with no motion.
    - The name box's text cursor blinks while the name box is focused. Under reduced motion it stays steady (F17).
    - The Iridescent Dream colour's sheen glides along the axolotl. Under reduced motion the sheen stays still, and it still shows.
    - Each Boy or girl picture button, in the character creator and on restyling's read-only Boy or girl screen, shows the usual boy or girl sign: a circle with an arrow for a boy, and a circle with a cross for a girl. The sign is ink on a white disc, 24 pixels across, in the lower-left corner, where the Colour step shows the morph badge. The labels "Boy" and "Girl" stay. Boy and Girl are never told apart by colour alone.
17. **F17** Names stay on this device only ([ADR 0011](../adr/0011-name-entry-stays-on-device.md)). They are never sent anywhere, and the game has no way to share them. The character creator and renaming both offer a list of suggested names, so a player never has to type a name. Suggested names fit the axolotl's boy or girl: in the character creator, in "Surprise me", and for rescued axolotls. A boy gets boy names and names for anyone. A girl gets girl names and names for anyone. "Surprise me" on the Name step, and on the Boy or girl step when the name no longer fits, picks a suggested name that fits. "Surprise me" on the Boy or girl step never changes a typed name. The lists:
    - Boy names: Axel, Finn, Ollie, Milo, and Gus.
    - Girl names: Lily, Blossom, Daisy, Rosie, and Luna.
    - Names for anyone: Bubbles, Gilly, Pebble, Noodle, Sprinkles, Mochi, Ripple, Puddle, Sunny, Peanut, Wiggles, Splash, Pickle, and Xochi.

    The name box is where the player types a name on the Name step. It looks like a place to type: a light field with an inset top and left edge, corners less rounded than the buttons, and a small pencil icon at its right end. It shows "Type a name" whenever it is empty. While it is focused, a text cursor blinks, 0.5 seconds on and 0.5 seconds off. Under reduced motion the cursor stays steady. The real text field matches the drawn name box. The name box works by touch and by keyboard.

### H. Areas, exit and stars

1. **H1** Areas are the levels of the game. The canal is Area 1, and it is the only area in the MVP (X9). After the MVP, the lily pad pond is Area 2 (built, issue #75; N2), the pond at dusk is Area 3 (built, issue #98), and the chinampa gardens are Area 4 (built, issue #76) and Area 5 (built, issue #103). Reaching an area's open exit beats it. Each area is built from its area data (Implementation Decision 8). One screen is 480 x 360 world pixels, and an area is a whole number of screens wide and tall. In an area taller than one screen, the camera scrolls up and down as well as sideways, and the zoom still fits one screen's height. The canal is 4 x 1 screens, never scrolls up or down, and plays exactly as before. The pond is 5 x 1 screens and never scrolls up or down either. It is harder than the canal: wider, with more tilapia, more snacks, faster snack drift, and a hazard (N2, N3). A one-leg axolotl can swim it to the exit and home in under 180 seconds. Area 3 is 6 x 1 screens and never scrolls up or down either. It is a little harder than Area 2: wider, with more snacks, faster snack drift, more and quicker mobile snacks (its freshwater shrimp flick away in place of the darting snacks it had until issue #120; H4), a stronger stream, more treasure slots, two herons, and, since issue #133, two carp (N2, N3). A one-leg axolotl can swim it to the exit and home in under 180 seconds too. Area 4 is 6 x 2 screens, the first area taller than one screen, so the camera follows the axolotl up and down as well as sideways, by touch (the swim pad) and by keyboard (the arrow keys and WASD). It is harder than Area 3: twice as tall, with more snacks, faster snack drift, more treasure slots, a tighter tilapia (the predator ladder), and the ghost nets (N2, N3). Since its danger pass (issue #119), its 4 tilapia and its 4 carp chase, at every depth since issue #134, two punting poles jab, two ghost nets drift, and half its snacks are tadpoles and shrimp (N2, N3, H4). A one-leg axolotl can swim it to the exit and home, each way in under 180 seconds. Area 5 is 7 x 2 screens and scrolls up and down as Area 4 does. It is the first U-turn area: the way runs right along the top row, down in the rightmost screen column, and back left along the bottom row to the exit under the Home Spot (N2). It is harder than Area 4: wider, with more snacks, faster snack drift, more treasure slots, a tighter tilapia (the predator ladder), five carp, three garter snakes, five punting poles, and the water hyacinth mats (N2, N3). A one-leg axolotl can swim it to the exit, waiting at each mat channel while it is closed, and home, each way in under 180 seconds: measured along the main path, 82.9 seconds to the exit when it sets off at the mats' time 0, and 80.9 to 88.4 seconds over 16 start times through the mat cycle (open question 234).
2. **H2** Each area has one clear, reachable exit: the canal's at the far end of its main swim path, the pond's at its right end, past the stream, Area 3's at its right end, past the stream and the root arch, out of every heron's reach, Area 4's deep in the bottom right, past the gap current, out of every predator's strike and clear of every ghost net's drift path and every punting pole's danger zone (issue #119), and Area 5's in the bottom left, straight under the Home Spot, at the end of the way back along the bottom row, out of every predator's reach and clear of every ghost net, mat sweep, and hiding spot (issue #103; open question 222). When the exit is open, its sign's arrow points toward the nearer side edge of the area: right in Areas 1 to 4, and left in Area 5, whose exit is in the left half (open question 224). It stays closed until every snack in the area has been eaten. Closed and open differ by shape or icon, not by colour alone, including under reduced motion.
3. **H3** A snack counter (an icon and a number) shows the snacks left. When only 3 are left, each gets the same hint sparkle that hidden treasures use (B7; see open question 16). The sparkle is static under reduced motion.
4. **H4** Snacks move. In Area 1 they drift slowly. In Area 2 they drift a little faster, in Area 3 a little faster again, about 15.2 pixels per second at most, in Area 4 a little faster still, about 15.6 pixels per second at most (issue #76), and in Area 5 a little faster again, about 15.8 pixels per second at most (issue #103). In every area the drift stays slower than a one-leg axolotl swims against the area's strongest stream and at most half the Snack magnet pull (see Tuning values). A snack is eaten where it is drawn, drift and bob included, by the axolotl's head (A19). In some areas 3 snacks also dart away a little, once each, by the same shared rule, the one-time darters (built, issues #98, #76, and #103; N2 growth). In an area with freshwater shrimp, the shrimp replace the one-time darters; each area's N2 paragraph says which it has (open question 274). The one-time darter rule:
    - Which: the darters are set by the area data, spread evenly through the area's worms and bugs in list order, with no random draw, so every trip has the same darters (open question 163). A tadpole or a shrimp is never a one-time darter (open question 274).
    - When: a darter darts when the head reach centre comes within 60 pixels of it, where it is drawn, drift and bob included. That is outside the 36 pixel eat reach, so it darts before it can be eaten.
    - The hop: one short hop of 40 pixels, straight away from the head, gliding at 120 pixels per second, slower than the axolotl swims. Under reduced motion it lands at once.
    - Once: each darter darts at most once per trip, then drifts and bobs from where it landed and stays catchable, so the exit can always open (H2). The Snack magnet still pulls it.
    - Where not: a darter never hops where its drift and bob could carry it out of the swim bounds, up to the surface, into the Home Spot ring or the safe area, or into a current, or within 80 pixels of a plastic worm, or, in an area with water hyacinth mats, where it could overlap a mat pair's sweep (open question 228). Then it does not dart at that moment, and it can dart later.

    Mobile snacks (owner decision on issue #117, 2026-10-23; built as shared parts in issue #117, opt-in per area; [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md); open questions 269 to 278; their movement: owner decisions 1 to 7 on issue #131, 2026-10-24, built as shared parts in issue #131, [ADR 0024](../adr/0024-mobile-snack-wander-loops-and-shrimp-hops.md), open questions 354 to 371): two snack types move on their own, so snacks get harder to catch area by area (the snack ramp below; N2, the danger pass). Tadpoles never stop: each one wanders its own home area, about one screen wide and from near the surface to near the bed, and changes direction every few seconds (owner decision 1). Shrimp are always busy: each one hops around its own small home area every 1 to 2 seconds, and still flicks away when the head comes close (owner decision 3). The snack counts do not change; only the movement does (owner decision 4). These rules live here, so every later area gets them (owner decision 5). Worms and bugs keep drifting and bobbing as before. The shared rules:
    - Tadpole, a swimmer (open questions 271 and 354 to 357): it never stops. It swims its own closed **wander loop**, a list of turn points written as numbers in its area's layout, the first its start: straight from each turn point to the next and from the last back to the first, at its area's steady speed, forever, turning at once at each point. Where it is comes from the snack drift clock alone: at time t it is ((t + offset) x speed) mod lap pixels along its loop from its start, so it adds no random draw. Its speed is set per area (the snack ramp below; owner decision 2 on issue #131, 2026-10-24), above 0 and at most 72 pixels per second, 0.8 times a one-leg axolotl's 90, so a one-leg axolotl always swims faster than any tadpole. Its **home area** is the box around its loop. A wander loop meets the snack motion rules when it has 4 to 8 turn points; its home area is 320 to 480 pixels wide, about one screen, and at least 150 pixels tall, so it reaches from near the surface to near the bed (a taller area aims for about 200), inside one 480 x 360 box; every turn point is inside the swim bounds and below the highest wave; each leg, turn point to turn point, takes 1.5 to 5 seconds at its speed, so it changes direction every few seconds; and a lap takes at most 40 seconds, so waiting in one place on its loop always catches it within a lap. An area's tadpoles start out of step, set by their offsets. It has no drift and no bob, and its place is the same under full and reduced motion. Its turns are drawing only, so its place stays exactly on its loop: at each corner it flips to face the new leg's sideways way and eases, over 0.25 seconds, to the new leg's tilt, the leg's angle from level held to 40 degrees each way, so it never swims sideways; a near-vertical leg, whose sideways part is under a quarter of its length, keeps the facing it had. Its wiggle is drawing only too: 4 tail frames at 8 frames a second and a body sway of up to 8 degrees each way, added to its tilt. Under reduced motion it still swims its loop, shows its first frame with no sway, and takes each new tilt at once (open questions 363 and 364). The catch proof: a one-leg axolotl steering straight at where it is drawn eats it within 20 seconds from each of 8 starts round its home area, 100 pixels outside it (open question 361).
    - Freshwater shrimp, a repeat darter (open questions 272, 358, and 359): it is always busy. Its **home area** is the box around its hop spots, a short list written as numbers in its area's layout, the first its start. A home meets the snack motion rules when it has 3 to 6 hop spots in a box at most 200 x 160 pixels. At rest it drifts and bobs like a worm or a bug. It is ready at the trip start, except that an area's hopping shrimp start out of step: shrimp n of N, in data order, first rests (n − 1) / N of its rest after a hop. A ready shrimp, drawn within its trigger radius of the head reach centre (inclusive, but not exactly on it), flicks straight away from the head, backward, by its flick distance, gliding at its flick speed. It flicks only where its drift and bob at the landing pass the hard rules (below), and its whole way there passes them too (below), and only inside its home box grown by its flick distance, so a player cannot herd it across the area; otherwise it stays ready and does not flick that moment. A ready shrimp drawn farther than 120 pixels from the head reach centre, twice its trigger radius, hops toward its next hop spot: at most 2/3 of its flick distance, gliding at 2/3 of its flick speed, landing on the spot once it is that close, then aiming at the one after, round its home. A hop lands only where its drift and bob pass the hard rules, with each plastic worm where it is now, and only when its whole way there passes them too: when its next spot's hop is not clear it aims at the one after that, and when none is clear it rests again. While the head is within 120 pixels it does not hop: it waits, ready to flick. After a flick it rests for its rest time, and after a hop for 3/4 of it, so it hops about every 1 to 2 seconds; each rest is counted only on swimming steps, with no limit on flicks or hops. After a flick it hops back toward its next hop spot. A hop is always slower than its flick, so its top speed for the Snack magnet does not change. Under reduced motion a flick or a hop lands at once. A collected shrimp never moves, and a flick or a hop whose shrimp is eaten stops. The Snack magnet may pull a gliding shrimp, and the two moves add up. It faces the head while the head is within 120 pixels, during a flick, and during the rest after a flick, and otherwise the way it last hopped; it shows its flick frame only while flicking (open question 363). A home of 1 hop spot, the plain form of an earlier shrimp spot (open question 367), never hops and has no home box limit: it flicks only where the whole earlier clear check of open question 273 holds at the landing and its whole way there passes the hard rules. Limits: a trigger radius above the 36 pixel eat reach; a rest of at least 1 second; a flick of 24 to 72 pixels; a flick speed above 0 and at most 150 pixels per second, a four-leg axolotl's top speed; and the catch limit, rest x 70 ≥ trigger radius + flick distance − 36, so a one-leg axolotl that swims straight at a shrimp that has just landed reaches it within one rest (70 pixels per second is a one-leg axolotl's 90 less 20 for drift). No hop starts within 120 pixels of the head, so the catch limit still holds.
    - Where (open questions 273 and 360; owner decision 6 on issue #131, 2026-10-24): the keep-out rule is relaxed for tadpoles and shrimp alike. A threat may reach part of a home, but every snack keeps coming back to safe water. The hard rules hold for every tadpole turn point and leg box, and for every shrimp hop spot, hop leg box, and flick or hop landing, each shrimp place by its drift and bob box: inside the swim bounds and below the highest wave; outside the Home Spot ring, the safe area, every current, and every mat pair's whole sweep, edges counting as inside; and at least 80 pixels from every plastic worm where it is now and every hazard's shape, a drifting ghost net by its whole drift path. A shrimp's whole way on every flick and hop, its drift and bob box at every point on the way, passes the hard rules too, except the plastic worms where they are now, which count only at the landing, so a shrimp resting beside a spat worm can still get away; two clear ends do not make a clear way. The threat rule for a tadpole: a predator or a moving hazard may reach part of its loop, but the longest stretch of the loop out of every threat's reach is at least half the loop, so every tadpole spends at least half of each lap, in one stretch, where it can be caught without danger. The threat rule for a shrimp: at least half of its hop spots, each by its drift and bob box, are out of every threat's reach. Reach for both is the strike range S of every threat for the risk ladder (B10): a predator's lunge range from its patrol loop, with no leash added, and a moving hazard's touch reach from its sweep. The threat rules are there because the exit needs every snack eaten (H2), so a snack that lived inside a threat's reach would force a risk. A spat plastic worm never lands within 80 pixels of the box of any tadpole leg or shrimp hop leg: it stays where it was, as when its landing is in a current. The Snack magnet may still pull any snack toward the head (A19).
    - Data (open question 270): mobile snacks are fixed area data, each tadpole's wander loop and each shrimp's hop spots written out as numbers in the area's own layout, never seeded, so they add no random draw (open questions 354 and 366). The data shape also takes a wander loop of 2 turn points and a home of 1 hop spot, the plain form of an earlier lane or shrimp spot; neither meets the snack motion rules (open question 367). They come after the seeded worms and bugs, tadpoles first, then shrimp. An area keeps its total snack count: its worms and bugs go down by its mobile count, so lowering them re-draws that area's seeded snacks, and its phase 2 issue re-records only its own golden, on purpose ([ADR 0022](../adr/0022-danger-pass-phase-2-seam-amendments.md); open question 297). An area's snacks in the current are a floor: that many are placed in its first current on purpose, and seeded stripe snacks may also start there (Glossary). The snacks that start in a current are always worms or bugs, because a mobile snack never enters a current.
    - Counting (open question 274): a tadpole and a shrimp each count as one snack, like a worm or a bug, for the counter, the last-3 sparkle (H3), the exit (H2), the snack speed-up (A19), and the reset when carried home (A12), so the exit can always open. Mobile snacks are snacks, not hazards, predators, or elements, so neither the one-new-thing rule nor the danger bar counts them (N2).
    - Fact cards (open question 276): "Meet the tadpole" and "Freshwater shrimp" (C13), each found the first time the axolotl's centre comes within 120 pixels of an uncollected snack of that type, where it is drawn, outside the safe area, and shown with play paused, like A21.
    - Proof (open questions 366 to 368): every area from Area 2 on proves, in its own layout test, that its tadpoles and shrimp meet the snack motion rules above (`meetsSnackMotion`), that every wander loop and home meets the hard rules and its threat rule, and that a one-leg axolotl catches every tadpole by the catch proof. An area adds these proofs in the issue that places its wander loops and homes.

    Snack ramp (owner decision on issue #117, 2026-10-23; open question 278): from Area 2 on, every area has both mobile snack types, and by area number the mobile share, the tadpole speed, the shrimp flick distance and flick speed never go down, and the shrimp rest never goes up. Area 1 has no mobile snacks. Each area's issue proves its own row in its own layout test, by the check range in the share and tadpole speed columns. The values are starting points for the phase 2 issues (N2, the danger pass), to tune in play-testing. Every shrimp's trigger radius is 60 pixels. A phase 2 issue that changes a value records it in its own N2 paragraph and a new open question, never in this table.

    | Area | Mobile share (check range) | Snacks: tadpoles + shrimp + worms + bugs (worms + bugs today) | Tadpole speed, pixels per second (check range) | Shrimp rest, flick distance, flick speed | Snack magnet pull |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | 1 | 0%, none | 16: 0 + 0 + 10 + 6 (10 + 6) | None | None | 40 |
    | 2 | About 20%: 20% (15 to 25%) | 20: 2 + 2 + 10 + 6 (12 + 8) | 40 (35 to 45) | 2.0 seconds, 36 pixels, 100 pixels per second | 48 |
    | 3 | About 35%: 33% (30 to 40%) | 24: 4 + 4 + 10 + 6 (14 + 10) | 50 (45 to 55) | 1.7 seconds, 44 pixels, 115 pixels per second | 60 |
    | 4 | About 50%: 50% (45 to 55%) | 28: 7 + 7 + 8 + 6 (16 + 12) | 60 (55 to 65) | 1.4 seconds, 52 pixels, 130 pixels per second | 72 |
    | 5 | About 60%: 59% (55 to 65%) | 32: 10 + 9 + 7 + 6 (18 + 14) | 70 (65 to 72) | 1.2 seconds, 60 pixels, 145 pixels per second | 84 |
    | 6 on | Rising to a cap of about 70%: at least the area before's (55 to 70%) | Its own | At least the area before's (70 to 72) | Area 5's or harder, inside the catch limit, with a flick speed of 145 to 150 pixels per second | max(40, 1.2 x its fastest mobile snack) |

5. **H5** Reaching the open exit banks every trip find at once, as at the Home Spot (A14). Then a kind "Area beaten" screen shows the stars earned (see open question 17).
6. **H6** Each area has up to 3 stars:
    - Star 1: reach the exit.
    - Star 2: find the area's special treasure (see open question 13).
    - Star 3: rescue the stranded axolotl, or finish the trip without losing a leg. A hit blocked by a Shell perk costs no leg (A22), so it does not lose this star.

    Stars are awarded when the area is beaten. An area's special treasure is always Common or Rare, never Epic or Legendary. The canal's is the Toy boat (Rare), the pond's is the Frog statue (Rare; issue #75), Area 3's is the Wooden duck decoy (Rare; issue #98), Area 4's is the Scarecrow (Rare; issue #76), and Area 5's is the Piñata (Rare; issue #103). The Castle tower, the canal's special treasure before, stays owned by everyone who banked it, and is now an ordinary Epic that turns up by the normal chance (B4) (issue #93; open question 136).
7. **H7** A star, once earned, is kept forever. When an area's special treasure changes, stars already earned are kept: a player who earned Star 2 with the Castle tower keeps it. A player who already banked the Toy boat earns Star 2 the next time they beat Area 1. From now on, Star 2 in Area 1 needs the Toy boat (issue #93). Any unlocked area can be replayed from the area map (N2). Replaying an area can earn the stars still missing, and hiding spots shuffle on replays too (B4). Stars per area are saved (D2) and shown, including each area's best stars on the area map.
8. **H8** The exit, the snack counter, the last-snack sparkle, and the "Area beaten" screen work by touch and by keyboard, under reduced motion, and in greyscale, per X2, X3, and X5.

### X. Cross-cutting

1. **X1** Nothing is scary or gory. The tilapia is round and cartoony with no frightening face, per the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md). UI text never uses "die", "dead", "killed", or "game over".
2. **X2** Rarity, legs, the telegraph, the protected state, and locked or unfound items can all be read in greyscale.
3. **X3** Every new animation (poof, regrowth, telegraph, carried home, tank swimming, and celebrations) has a still or minimal version under reduced motion. Gameplay still works.
4. **X4** There is no flashing beyond the art style guide limits, and no screen shake.
5. **X5** New touch targets are at least 48 by 48 CSS pixels. Primary actions are at least 64 pixels tall. The keyboard can do everything touch can.
6. **X6** Text sizes follow the art style guide. Wording is readable by an 8-year-old.
7. **X7** There are no ads, chat, purchases, analytics, accounts, or third-party requests, per [ADR 0007](../adr/0007-kid-safety-and-privacy.md).
8. **X8** Swim-test features keep working: the swim pad, the Dash button, keyboard controls, snacks, the current, reeds, bubbles, and pause.
9. **X9** The world is made of named areas. A habitat is a group of one or more areas with its own look, and each area is in one habitat (N2). The MVP ships one area: the canal, which is Area 1 (H1), in the canal habitat. After the MVP, the lily pad pond habitat adds Area 2 (built, issue #75) and Area 3, the pond at dusk (built, issue #98; N2), and the chinampa gardens habitat adds Area 4 (built, issue #76) and Area 5 (built, issue #103), so five areas ship in three habitats. An area may use its own variant of its habitat's look, such as Area 3's dusk look (open question 168). The game must allow more areas to be added later and joined, so a player can swim from one to another within a single trip. Adding areas must not change the rules for legs, banking, treasures, or saves. Saved progress identifies treasures and hiding spots per area, so new areas never break old saves.
10. **X10** In-trip messages (the find and rescue toasts, the snack banner, the first-trip hint, the Dash pot tip, which uses the first-trip hint's banner and placement (B11), the plastic worm message "Bleh! That was plastic, not food.", and the ghost net message "Snagged on an old net! Wriggle free.", both of which use the snack banner's placement, N3) never cover the controls or open water in the middle of the play area. They show at the top or bottom edge of the screen, else over the Trip Bag panel, else over the status panels. The middle is the full width, from 30% to 70% of the way down the water as drawn on screen (from the water line to the sea bed, not the screen height). Inside it a message may cover only the status panels themselves (the snack counter, legs, and Trip Bag), never the water beside or between them. Outside it a message may go anywhere except over a control. When the snack banner or the first-trip hint has no such place, it tries every place again with smaller text, 20 pixels (still at least the 18 pixel minimum, X6), before it waits; the find toast never shrinks (open question 58). A message with no such place waits, hidden, until one opens up. At 460 x 320 and 460 x 340 with Go home showing, only the "Tie-dye galaxy Paint shell" and "Rainbow dragonfly wings" find toasts wait this way (open questions 38 and 154). The canal, the pond, and Area 3 never scroll up or down, so each screen size has fixed water rows. An area taller than one screen, such as Area 4, scrolls up and down (H1). There the middle is measured on screen, from the water line or the screen top, whichever is lower, to the sea bed or the screen bottom, whichever is higher (open question 121). The messages move again when that water moves by 8 CSS pixels or more, or 0.1 seconds after a smaller move.
11. **X11** Frame time stays within the budget in [ADR 0013](../adr/0013-axolotl-rig-and-frame-budget.md) in the four measured cases: the canal with the explorer and a rescue, the Home Tank with four axolotls, the title screen, and the dev test area (taller than one screen, with the explorer and a rescue, via `?area=dev-test-area` in a dev build), each on WebGL and on Canvas (open question 106).


### M. Music

Background music is N6 (epic issue #150; [ADR 0025](../adr/0025-procedural-background-music.md)). The mood rules, the instruments, and each cue's key, tempo and chords are in the [music guide](../music-guide.md).

1. **M1** The Home cue, Sunny Tank, plays on the title screen, in the Home Tank, in the character creator and restyle screens, on the area map, and in the refuge. It is cozy but happy. Moving between these screens never restarts it.
2. **M2** On a trip, the music is the cue of the area's habitat: Canal Paddle for the canal (Area 1), Lily Pad Hop for the lily pad pond (Areas 2 and 3), and Garden Boats for the chinampa gardens (Areas 4 and 5). Cards and popups shown during a trip, and the pause screen, keep the trip's cue playing.
3. **M3** Every habitat has its own cue, and no two habitats share one. A new habitat is not done until it has its cue. A rule test checks that every habitat in the habitat list has a cue.
4. **M4** The dusk rule. An area whose look is dusk or night plays the dusk version of its habitat's cue, never a cue of its own. The dusk version keeps the cue's tune and chords and changes only how it is played: slower, the tune on a music box, soft held chords, no drums, and fireflies (soft, high chimes at random). One shared rule makes every habitat cue's dusk version, so every habitat cue has one. Area 3, the pond at dusk, plays Lily Pad Hop (dusk).
5. **M5** Music never reacts to danger. A warning, a strike, a lost leg, and being carried home bring no sting, no change of cue, and no change of tempo or volume (X1, [ADR 0008](../adr/0008-gentle-fail-state.md)).
6. **M6** Music is made live in code with Web Audio. There are no audio files, no network requests, and no new dependencies (X7, [ADR 0007](../adr/0007-kid-safety-and-privacy.md)).
7. **M7** No sound plays before the player's first tap, click, or key press. The music starts on that first input, with a short fade in.
8. **M8** A music switch is on the title screen, in the Home Tank (in the Sound card since sound effects, S14), and on the trip pause screen. It shows an icon and the words "Music on" or "Music off", is at least 48 by 48 CSS pixels, works by touch and by keyboard (X5), and can be read in greyscale (X2). Music is on at first launch. The choice is kept on the device across reloads and updates, and "Start over" (D7) keeps it, because it is a device setting, not progress.
9. **M9** A change of cue cross-fades over about 1 second. When the page is hidden (another tab, or the phone locked), the music stops, and it carries on when the page shows again. While a trip is paused, the music plays softer.
10. **M10** Each cue is an 8-bar loop that changes a little each time round: a new instrument for the tune, a harmony, a made-up phrase, or a quiet pass. The changes come from a seed, so the same seed always plays the same notes.
11. **M11** The volume is gentle, with no sudden loud sounds: a limiter on the output keeps the loudest moment of any cue close to its usual level.
12. **M12** Frame time stays within the [ADR 0013](../adr/0013-axolotl-rig-and-frame-budget.md) budget with music on, in the X11 cases.

### S. Sound effects

Sound effects are N7 (epic issue #162; [ADR 0026](../adr/0026-procedural-sound-effects.md)). The mood rules, every sound's recipe, the moments kept silent, the key rule, and the loudness levels are in the [sound guide](../sound-guide.md). The approved sketches are `prototypes/sound-effects/sketches.html`.

1. **S1** Sound effects are made live in code with Web Audio, like the music (M6): no audio files, no network requests, and no new dependencies (X7). The music and the sounds share one audio engine: one audio context, one unlock on the first input, one reverb, and one limiter, with a music bus and a sounds bus. No sound plays before the first tap, click, or key press (M7). When the page is hidden, sounds stop with the music (M9).
2. **S2** The sound guide lists every moment that has a sound and every moment kept silent. Swimming, steering, the current, predators patrolling or chasing, a lunge that misses, the snack magnet, the hint sparkle, find toasts, pausing, keyboard focus moves, and typing a name are silent.
3. **S3** Eating a snack plays a soft gulp and one note. Snacks eaten within about 2 seconds of each other climb the scale one note each (the snack ladder), up to 10 notes, and a pause starts it again from the bottom. Each of the last 3 snacks (H3) plays the gulp on one high note with a twinkle in place of its ladder note, and the ladder does not climb for it (open question 470). A tadpole or shrimp climbs the snack ladder like any snack and adds a small boing to its gulp and note; one of the last 3 plays the twinkle with no boing (open question 481).
4. **S4** Finding a treasure plays a sound that grows with its rarity: more notes, higher, and longer from Common to Legendary, so the rarity can be heard without seeing its colour (X2). Even the Legendary sound is under 2.5 seconds, and every rarity is about as loud as the others (S13). Only one find sound plays at a time; a second waits for the first to end.
5. **S5** Losing a leg plays the "uh-oh": a soft puff with two low notes falling a small step, left unfinished. A leg growing back plays three rising notes that end on the home note, which answers it. Neither has a crunch, a hit, a sad slide, or a minor-key sigh (X1, [ADR 0008](../adr/0008-gentle-fail-state.md)).
6. **S6** A Tail Dash plays a watery whoosh that rises, with a trail of bubbles.
7. **S7** Banking plays one bubble and note per banked find, a rescued axolotl counting as one, climbing, up to 5, then a ukulele strum of the home chord.
8. **S8** The exit opening (H2) plays a tiny "ta-da". Beating an area (H5, H6) plays a strum, one chime per star earned, each higher, then the home chord, so one star still ends happily.
9. **S9** A predator's warning plays the ripple: a soft water swish with two low notes, once when its telegraph starts (A1), never repeating or building. The music does not change (M5). A punting pole's warning plays the same ripple, once when it starts on screen, and a frame plays at most one ripple, a predator's and a pole's warning starting together included (open question 483).
10. **S10** Being carried home (A6) plays the lullaby: a soft held home chord, a slow music box line falling gently to the home note, and bubbles floating up. It starts when the carry starts, once the last leg's "uh-oh" has rung out, and a trip that ends before it starts drops it (open question 490).
11. **S11** No sound is scary, harsh, gross, or sad: no alarms, drones, rising tension, crunches, buzzers, or gagging, and no sound repeats or builds while danger lasts. Every sound has an on-screen partner, so the game plays fully with sounds off. Reduced motion (X3) changes no sound.
12. **S12** Pitched sounds use the five notes of the major pentatonic scale of the cue playing, or of the cue that would play when music is off: G for Sunny Tank and Canal Paddle, F for Lily Pad Hop, and D for Garden Boats, dusk versions included. Every cue names its key, and a rule test checks it.
