13. **S13** Every sound has a level in its data, set so that every sound is about as loud as the others: event sounds at about −18 and touch sounds (button taps, close or back, picking up a Decoration, taking off a Wearable, page turns, the shrimp flick) about 3 dB under, measured K-weighted over the sound's loudest 0.2 seconds. The sounds bus and the music bus start at the same volume, and the music never dips for a sound. The same sound starts at most once every 60 ms; one asked for sooner waits its turn instead of being dropped (open question 479). A rule test checks that every sound has a level.
14. **S14** A sounds switch shows an icon and the words "Sounds on" or "Sounds off", is at least 48 by 48 CSS pixels, works by touch and by keyboard (X5), and can be read in greyscale (X2). It is beside the music switch on the title screen and on the trip pause screen. In the Home Tank, one "Sound" hub tile, in place of the music tile, opens a small card with both switches. Sounds are on at first launch. The choice is kept on the device across reloads and updates, and "Start over" (D7) keeps it, because it is a device setting, not progress.
15. **S15** Every button press, by touch or by keyboard, plays the same soft tap. A Close or Back button, and Cancel in Restyle, plays the tap lower: the close or back tap. A press that plays its own sound plays it in place of the tap: the Collection Book's and the Treasure Chest's Previous and Next (a page turn), Surprise me, and Restyle's Done (the restyle's sound, when it has one). The Dash button does not tap: a press that starts a Tail Dash plays its whoosh (S6), and a press while the Dash recharges plays nothing (open question 466). Turning sounds on plays a short sample; turning them off plays nothing.
16. **S16** Frame time stays within the [ADR 0013](../adr/0013-axolotl-rig-and-frame-budget.md) budget with music and sounds on, in the X11 cases.

## Implementation Decisions

1. **Two-tier ownership.** Finds live in one of two tiers. The Trip Bag holds unbanked finds for this trip only. The banked tier holds the Treasure Chest and the axolotls in the Home Tank. Only banking moves finds from the Trip Bag to the banked tier. Being carried home returns Trip Bag finds to the world. No gameplay event removes anything banked. Only "Start over" does.
2. **Stable identities.** The treasure catalogue and hiding spots are fixed per area. Every catalogue item, hiding spot, and morph has a stable, unique identity. Saves therefore survive updates and additions to the catalogue or to areas. Which spot holds which treasure is picked fresh each trip and is never saved.
3. **Persistent save contents.** The save is one versioned JSON save of the hub only, kept in `localStorage` under the key `glam-gills:save` ([ADR 0012](../adr/0012-save-on-device.md)). The hub is banked state, the tank layout, each axolotl's look and outfit, the chosen explorer, Collection Book unlocks, stars earned per area, and settings. Nothing from a trip is saved. It stays on the device.
    - Upgrade chain: older saves are upgraded forward, one upgrade per version step, then loaded.
    - Additive rule: a new hub field loads with its default when an older save lacks it, with no version bump. Only a change an old save cannot load by default (a rename, a new meaning) adds an upgrade and bumps the save version.
    - Repair rule: a save that is structurally damaged (not JSON, not an object, no valid version, no hub, or an upgrade that fails) or from a newer version is unreadable. Anything else loads, repaired field by field: unknown ids are dropped and bad fields fall back to their defaults, so one bad field never wipes the rest.
4. **Deterministic rules.** The rules for legs, regrowth, protected time, the tilapia telegraph, lunge and cooldown, the heron's wading, warning, stab, and cooldown, cover from a heron, darting snacks, banking, hiding difficulty by rarity, the snack regrowth speed-up, the exit rule, star rules, save upgrades, and the update decision are deterministic. They can be tested without rendering, per [ADR 0006](../adr/0006-testing-strategy.md).
5. **Fail state.** Fail-state behaviour follows [ADR 0008](../adr/0008-gentle-fail-state.md), as amended by [ADR 0010](../adr/0010-carried-home-full-reset.md).
6. **Update check.** Any update check is first-party only and sends no player data.
7. **Tuning numbers.** Tuning numbers are adjustable starting points, not part of the contract.
8. **Areas as data.** Each area is described as data: its layout, look, hazards, hiding spots, treasures, snacks, exit, and special treasure. New habitats can be added without rewriting game rules. Moving from one area to another is a world feature, not a special case inside one area. Built in the area framework (issue #73, [ADR 0014](../adr/0014-area-framework.md)):
    - Each area's data holds its id, its number, its habitat, and its size in screens (wide x tall). Its layout holds the start, the Home Spot safe area, the reed beds, the cave and current hiding spots, the currents, the cave, the main swim path, the stranding places, its treasures, its snack counts, and its pads. It also holds its tilapia routes, its other predator routes (each naming its species), its hazards, and its timing or puzzle elements. The area also names its exit, its special treasure, and how its snacks drift, and it may name its own look and which of its snacks dart (issue #98).
    - The game builds each area's layout from its data and a seed. The built layout also has the area's size, its sea bed top, and its number of screens across and down.
    - A habitat is data too: an id, a name, and a look.
    - Predator species and hazard types are data (N3). The tilapia keeps its own column of routes beside the other predator routes (open question 125).
    - Areas only for development and tests are never in the area list and never ship (open question 122).
    - The lily pad pond, Area 2, is built as data on the framework (issue #75). Its one new rule set is the plastic worm's, a hazard that only slows (N3).
    - The pond at dusk, Area 3, is built as data on the framework (issue #98). It adds the heron, the second predator species on the shared predator rules, with two herons that wade near the surface and warn on sight (N3, issue #111). Its species data turns on three shared rule options, each absent on the tilapia, so the tilapia plays exactly as before: hunting by sight, so it cannot see an axolotl in cover; a strike spot fixed at the warning start; and standing still through its cooldown (open questions 207, 210, and 213). The encounter takes the area's cover (its reed patches, pads, and cave) from the area's layout. Area 3 has no timing element: issue #111 removed the heron's shadow cycle, the timer-started warning that issue #98 built, and no species uses a timer now. The generic timing element data stays for later areas (open question 217). Area 3's look is a variant of the pond's look, `pond-dusk`, named in its area data (`AreaData.look`). The art layer reads each look's properties (its sky and wash, the stream, the kind of cave, the pond props, and the fireflies) and never checks a look id (open question 168). Its darting snacks are area data too (H4).
    - The chinampa gardens, Area 4, are built as data on the framework (issue #76; [ADR 0018](../adr/0018-chinampa-gardens-ghost-nets-and-predator-ladder.md)), in a new habitat, `chinampa`, with its own look, `chinampa`. Its one new rule set is the ghost net's, a hazard that only slows (N3). Its layout may also hold, as optional data that older areas leave out, so they play exactly as before: its own tilapia tuning (the predator ladder, N2; absent means the shared tilapia tuning), and its overhanging banks with the hiding spots under them (the bank cover, B5; never built from the bank art's columns). The chinampa look adds its own properties too (the garden plot banks, the root tangle cave, the gap edges either side of a current, and the chinampa props), read like the others.
    - The chinampa gardens' Area 5 is built as data on the framework (issue #103; [ADR 0020](../adr/0020-u-turn-areas-garter-snake-and-hyacinth-mats.md)), in the chinampa habitat with the habitat's own look. It adds the garter snake, the third predator species, and the water hyacinth mats, the first timing element to ship and the first thing that blocks the explorer (N3). It generalises the framework for a U-turn area (N2, the area shape rule): the safe area is area data and need not be the Home Spot's whole column; the side past a current comes from the current's own flow, not from where home is (B6); and the open exit sign's arrow points toward the nearer side edge of the area (H2). An element type may carry a fact card and a meet range as optional data, and a mat pair spawn its geometry; elements without them play exactly as before.
9. **Per-axolotl data.** Every owned axolotl has a stable, unique identity. Its look (real morph, applied Dream colour, boy or girl, face, gill style, spots, tail, body, age, size, and name) and its outfit are saved per axolotl, against that identity. Wearables and Dream colours are referred to by their stable catalogue identities. Live axolotls are a rig of baked parts ([ADR 0013](../adr/0013-axolotl-rig-and-frame-budget.md), issue #46). Every part re-bakes when its appearance key changes: every look field (boy or girl, tail, body, age, size, and the rest), the morph colours and Dream colour, the outfit, and the bake scale. Each leg also re-bakes when its own leg state changes. A pose never re-bakes. Picture-button previews are still bakes, repainted only when the look or the outfit shown changes.
10. **Perks as data.** Each Wearable's perk type and value are data. Perks are modifiers applied on top of the base rules for speed, regrowth, Tail Dash, hits, hints, and snacks. Each perk type has a total cap, and only the capped total applies. Perk and cap rules are deterministic and can be tested without rendering.
11. **Looks never change play.** Size, boy or girl, tail, body, age, face, gill style, spots, morph, and Dream colour never change the hit area or speed. Only perks change play.
12. **Set-ready Wearables.** Each Wearable may carry an optional set identity in its data, so Wearable sets (N5) can be added later without reworking the catalogue or saves. The MVP has no sets and no set bonuses.

13. **Music as data.** Music follows [ADR 0025](../adr/0025-procedural-background-music.md) (N6).
    - Each cue is data in `src/logic/music.ts`: its key, tempo, meter, chords, and tune. The Home cue stands alone, and the habitat cues are keyed by habitat id.
    - The arrangement rules are pure: given a cue, its version (day or dusk), a step, and a seed, they return the notes to play. The dusk version is one shared rule over any cue (M4).
    - Which cue plays is a pure rule too: the Home cue on the Home side (M1), and on a trip the habitat's cue, in its dusk version when the area's look is dusk or night (M2, M4). Each area look variant says its time of day (day, dusk, or night), and the music rule reads that, never the look id.
    - The synth that turns notes into sound, the scheduler, and the fades are browser glue, in `src/platform/`.
    - The music switch is kept in device storage under its own key, `glam-gills:music`, outside the save. So the save, its `SAVE_VERSION`, and "Start over" are unchanged.
14. **Sounds as data.** Sound effects follow [ADR 0026](../adr/0026-procedural-sound-effects.md) (N7).
    - Each sound is a recipe in `src/logic/`: its notes as steps of the scale, its voices, timing, and loudness, its versions (such as a rarity or a star count), and its level (S13).
    - Pure rules turn a recipe, its version, the key, and the snack ladder into the notes to play. The key comes from the playing cue (S12), and the ladder and the repeat limit are pure rules too (S3, S13).
    - One shared audio engine in `src/platform/` holds the audio context, the unlock, the hidden-page stop, the reverb, the limiter, and the voices; the music player and the sound player use it, each on its own bus (S1).
    - A sound router turns game events into sounds, so scenes stay thin. It listens where the trip events are sent and to the shared button press, including keyboard presses.
    - The sounds switch is kept in device storage under its own key, `glam-gills:sounds`, outside the save. So the save, its `SAVE_VERSION`, and "Start over" are unchanged.

## Testing Decisions

- **Headless rule tests** prove the deterministic rules, per [ADR 0006](../adr/0006-testing-strategy.md). They cover:
  - leg state changes;
  - exactly one leg lost per hit;
  - the protected window;
  - regrowth timing, including during pause;
  - top speed decreasing as legs are lost;
  - beating every current with one leg;
  - at most one tilapia telegraphing at a time, and a 1-leg axolotl reaching the Home Spot from anywhere with every tilapia patrolling;
  - shared predator rules from species data (N3): the tilapia as the first species, playing as before with its tests unchanged; a warning starting only within range, outside the safe area, and when the species allows it; the warning lasting its wind-up, the strike following the aim locked at its start, then the cooldown; and, with a test species beside the tilapia, at most one predator warning or striking at a time across every species (A1, open question 118). The shared rule options (issue #111; `src/logic/predators.test.ts`), each absent on the tilapia: with sight cover, no warning at a target in cover, a warning whose target is in cover at the end-of-warning re-check cancelled, cover re-checked only at that end, and a strike never connecting with a target in cover, while a species without the option ignores cover; with a fixed strike spot, the spot fixed at the warning start and stabbed from where the predator stands, missing a target that moved and connecting once the stab tip comes within the hit reach of a target that stayed, the stab tip worked out from the state alone in a straight line over the strike, and no strike spot ever on a predator without the option; and with a still cooldown, standing still facing the target through the cooldown, then patrolling again, while a predator without the option swims its route;
  - the wading heron (N3, issue #111; `src/logic/predators.test.ts`): its species data, the second species, a sight hunter that stabs a fixed spot, stands still to cool down, and never bumps; its tuning (a 1.2 second warning, above the 0.8 second floor and longer than the tilapia's, a 110 pixel stab in 0.3 seconds at the Medium hit reach, a 3 second cooldown, and a slow wade); only the shared sight-based phase events; wading its stretch back and forth at its wading speed while the axolotl is out of reach; the warning on sight starting on the first step the axolotl is within reach of where the heron stands, inclusive, measured from where it stands and not from its stretch, so it warns once it wades near enough; never warning at an axolotl below its reach, in the safe area (edges included), or in cover, where it wades on instead, nor while another predator is warning or striking (A1); standing still for the warning with its facing following the axolotl, and the strike spot fixed at the warning start; the stab hitting an axolotl that stayed once the tip is within the hit reach, the tip moving in a straight line from where the heron stands to the spot over 0.3 seconds, and never connecting with an axolotl in cover, even at the spot; a warning cancelled into the 3 second cooldown when the axolotl hides in cover or swims out of reach before it ends; standing still facing the axolotl for the 3 second cooldown after a stab, hit or miss, or a cancel, then wading on; and freezing in every phase on zero, negative, or non-finite steps; and the route loop distance, the wrap segment included, matching the hiding rules' route distance exactly;
  - the heron in play (N3, issue #111): in the encounter, the tilapia first, then each heron route with the heron species and its own tuning; never bumping, even with the axolotl on its strike spot, whatever its touch reach (open question 170); a connected stab from where the heron stands as one hit, costing one leg and starting protected time and the bump cooldown; a Shell perk blocking the first stab; the fourth stab carrying the axolotl home; its cover taken from the zone data, so it never warns at an axolotl in reeds, under a pad, or in the cave, but warns and stabs in open water, while the tilapia ignores cover; a warning cancelled into the cooldown when the axolotl hides before it ends; a stab at the spot fixed at the warning start, a hit when the axolotl stays and a miss when it moved; the cover geometry copied and kept on reset; only one predator warning or striking at a time across two herons and a tilapia (A1); and the heron frozen while paused (`src/logic/encounter.test.ts`). On a trip, a heron met from the first step within its 180 pixel meet range of where it stands, and not past that, wherever it has waded to; nothing met inside the safe area; a heron met with the axolotl in cover, because cover stops a warning, not a meeting; and the cover data kept on the next trip (`src/logic/trip.test.ts`; open question 216). Its strike zone for B10: the heron route's strike range S is its species lunge range, 110, banded by the shared bands; it warns at a sampled point, standing on the nearest point of its stretch, exactly when that point is in the strike zone and not in cover, the zone edge counting as in reach, never from anywhere on its stretch outside the zone, in deep water below it, or in the safe area; and it never warns at a covered point under a pad, in the root arch, or in reeds, though the risk ladder still counts that point as near (`src/logic/hiding.test.ts`; open questions 207 and 215). The heron fact card's id, name, title, and body, with no banned words and a meet range larger than its reach (`src/logic/factCards.test.ts`);
  - hazard hits (N3, open question 126): touching a hazard that costs a leg, within its reach of the shape edge, costs one leg, starts protected time (A5), is blocked by an unused Shell perk (A22), carries the axolotl home on the fourth leg (A11), and leaves the bump cooldown unchanged; a hazard that only slows never hits; and timing elements repeat on their cycle;
  - plastic worms (N3, issue #75): slow-only, with the snack head reach, its meet range, and its fact card, never a threat and never costing a leg; touched by the head, not the body centre, within the reach, inclusive; eating one starts 3 seconds of queasy time and spits only that worm 48 pixels past the head reach centre, clamped to the swim bounds, or leaves it where it was when that landing is inside a current or the safe area; no stacking and no timer reset while queasy; a spat worm slowing again once queasy time ends; queasy time counting down to 0 and ending at once; only the top speed scaled while queasy (54 pixels per second with one leg), so Tail Dash is not slowed; and the wobble peaking at its amplitude, 0 under reduced motion and when not queasy;

the refuge cards after the area cards, so the book counts 13 fact cards; the heading "Facts: N of M found" (issue #124), with a made-up 13-card book's heading taking the same room as the 9-card heading did on every supported canvas; each unfound card's hint naming the habitat of the first area where its hazard, predator, or element appears ("Explore the lily pad pond to find this card." for the heron); the book at 1 row a page in 8 + 16 + 13 pages; and the "Collection Book" title hiding only on a featured page whose entry does not fit under it, measured on every supported canvas: only the found plastic worm, freshwater shrimp, heron, punting pole, and water hyacinth pages, at the canvases listed in C14 (`src/logic/collectionBook.test.ts`); and the garter snake and water hyacinth cards' ids, titles, and bodies, and every area fact card fitting every supported canvas, the shrimp, heron, and water hyacinth bodies wrapping at the tight-padding width at 320 x 440 (`src/logic/factCards.test.ts`, `src/logic/cardCopyFit.test.ts`);
  - nothing mid-trip restored (D3): a loaded save opens in the Home Tank with a fresh canal, and unbanked finds go back into the canal and can be picked again;
  - treasures banked at the Home Spot mid-trip saved at once (D1);
  - "Start over"'s two steps (D7): erasing only after two go presses in a row, Keep and Escape closing either card, and the button showing only once an axolotl is owned;
  - the update decision;
  - perk caps for every perk type, including a full Legendary outfit still losing a leg to every unblocked hit;
  - Shell perks blocking at most one hit per trip in total;
  - every look option and size being looks only: the same hit area and speed at every size, boy or girl, tail, body, and age;
  - the look option lists and the creator's step order (11 steps, at most 8 options each);
  - look options being easy to tell apart at every size and age: the boy's taller tail fin, the girl's rounder belly, bigger eyelashes, and cheek heart, each body's depth, each spot pattern's count, size, and placement, each mouth's ink box and traits, and Grumpy told apart from Determined by its brow and its half-shown eye;
  - "Surprise me": on the creator's Colour step a valid whole new axolotl, with boy or girl set and a suggested name that fits it; on every other step only that step's choice changing, always to a different choice, with nothing else changing; and on the hidden restyle tabs (Colour, Boy or girl, Dream colour, and Outfit) doing nothing;
  - restyling never changing the real morph or the boy or girl;
  - suggested names fitting boy or girl, and a rescued axolotl getting its seeded boy or girl and the first unused name that fits;
  - the name box: the "Type a name" placeholder whenever it is empty, the cursor timing (0.5 seconds on and 0.5 seconds off, steady under reduced motion), and room for the pencil at its right end;
  - he or she text: text about one axolotl matches its boy or girl and never says "they" or "it";
  - outfit and slot rules: one Wearable per slot, each Wearable in one slot only, and on at most one axolotl at a time;
  - keep-clear zones for every slot: each worn Wearable, in all eight slots, at every size, boy or girl, tail, body, and age, and every swim phase, never covers the eyes (eyelashes included), the gill roots, or a girl's cheek heart, using the keep-clear zones as revised in the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 6i, the cheek heart's zone included, and Socks and Feet draw only on a leg that is present;
  - the swim-view floor (F13): each worn Wearable's visible fill, the fill not covered by its own ink or by a later layer, is at least 64 square CSS pixels at every look and swim phase, for a Small axolotl at 320 x 440 (0.684 CSS pixels per local unit). No item is excepted;
  - the slot minimums (F13): each Wearable meets its slot's minimum sizes on every look, in all eight slots (Head, Neck, Face, Shirt, Back, Tail, Socks, and Feet). The numbers are in `WEARABLE_SLOT_MINIMUMS` (`src/art/wearableFit.ts`) and the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 6i;
  - the durable size rule (F13): one suite reads every Wearable in the whole catalogue (`ALL_TREASURES`, the canal's then the pond's), so each new Wearable is held to the same rule. It fails when a Wearable has no art, or art has no catalogue entry. It fails when any Wearable falls below the floor or its slot minimums. It fails when the recorded per-item table (`WEARABLE_RECORD`) is missing an item, or lists one that is not in the catalogue. A later area's Wearables must be added to `ALL_TREASURES`;
  - every Tail item keeping one Dream marker star or more 0.60 units or more clear, at every look, swim phase, and tail amplitude;
  - every Socks and Feet item wearing on present legs only, with its lowest ink at y 59 or less;

- **Music rule tests** (N6; `src/logic/music.test.ts`) cover: every habitat in the habitat list has a cue, and no two share one (M3); the cue chosen for each screen and each area, Area 3's dusk version included (M1, M2, M4); a dusk version plays the same tune and chords as its day version, slower, with no drums (M4); the same seed gives the same notes, and a new pass changes some of them (M10); every note is in a set range; and no rule reads danger, legs, or trip state (M5).
- **Sound rule tests** (N7) cover: every sound has a recipe and a level, and touch sounds sit 3 dB under event sounds (S13); every cue names its key, and the key of each cue (S12); a recipe's notes all come from the key's pentatonic scale (S12); every sound rings out within 3 seconds (open question 465); the snack ladder climbs one note a snack, stops at 10, and starts again after a pause of game time (S3; open question 467); each of the last 3 snacks plays the twinkle and leaves the ladder where it is, also when a trip starts with 3 or fewer snacks left (S3; open question 470); find sounds grow with rarity, more notes and a higher top note and a longer length from Common to Legendary, all under 2.5 seconds (S4); the "uh-oh" falls and does not end on the home note, and the leg-back sound ends on it (S5); every win, every bank count and every star count included, ends on the home chord: its last pitched notes are all home chord notes, in any octave (S7, S8; open question 476); which trip moment plays which sound, once per event, and that the moments the sound guide keeps silent play nothing: a find by its rarity, the "uh-oh" for a hit that costs a leg and nothing for a Shell block, the leg back for each regrowth, the bank by count up to 5, Tail Dash once per Dash, the exit once per trip after the last snack's sound, and the win by the stars earned this trip (S2, S11; open questions 469 and 471 to 475); the warning plays once per telegraph start, and nothing more for a cancelled one (S9); the repeat limit and one find sound at a time, a second find starting when the first ends (S4, S13; open question 468); the sounds switch defaults to on and is kept outside the save (S14); the Home Tank, book and menu rules (issue #167): a button press plays the close or back tap only for a close or back button, the tap for every other, and nothing when the button does not tap (S15); arriving home plays its sound after every ending except beating an area; a restyle plays at most one sound, a new Dream colour first, then a Wearable put on or swapped, then one taken off; Surprise me counts as pressed, by its button or by Enter or Space while it has the focus, only where it shows; and the area map plays its new area sound only when an unlocked area is new; and the hazard and rescue rules (issue #166; open questions 480 to 497): each new sound has its recipe, its level and its touch flag, with the shrimp flick the only touch sound among them (S13); the lullaby's last pitched note is the home note (S10), and the rescue's safe sound ends on the home chord; the Dash pot plays the same notes every time; the Shell block, the carry home, a sealed Dash pot touched and a Dash pot broken, meeting a stranded axolotl, the plastic worm and the ghost net snag each play their sound once per event, while the strike, the bump, the regrowth start, the end of protected time and the arrival home play nothing; a tadpole or shrimp plays the catch on its ladder step and climbs the ladder, and among the last 3 plays the twinkle; the Paint shell plays after its find, and the rescue's safe sound after a bank that holds a rescued axolotl; Dash ready plays once per recharge and never at a trip's start; each hazard edge plays once, the snag's end only while swimming, and a punting pole's warning start and a mat pair's opening only on screen and at most once a frame, with no edge on a trip's first frame and no pole ripple on a frame that already has a predator's warning; each shrimp that starts a flick reports its start, under reduced motion too, and a hop does not; and the catch waits in the snack line, the Paint shell in the find line, the lullaby for the "uh-oh" and the rescue's safe sound for the bank, each starting when the sound ahead of it really ends.
- **Manual phone play-tests** on the GitHub Pages build, per [ADR 0006](../adr/0006-testing-strategy.md), cover what rules cannot: feel, telegraph readability, a non-scary tone, a greyscale check, reduced motion, touch target sizes, and whether the full reset when carried home feels too harsh (open question 15). They also cover the area map (N2: the flow from Go! through the map to the trip, Back to the explorer card, a locked area's message, drag and wheel scrolling, and reduced motion) and, under `npm run dev` with `?area=dev-test-area`, swimming a tall area that scrolls up and down (H1, X10). Under `npm run dev` with `?decor`, they also cover placing, moving, and putting away the Floating and Hanging test Decorations, and the Home Tank controls and the Menu button, at 460 x 320, 667 x 375, 844 x 390, 1024 x 768, 1280 x 720, 1920 x 1080, 320 x 440, and 390 x 844: nothing covers the tank while the menu is folded, and the tank's size feels right; Menu and Hide by touch and by Tab then Enter or Space, the button never moving or hiding, and the tap playing; tapping an axolotl while the menu is folded; the tank arrival card, a milestone card, and the Sound card over the hub; the tank growing to full size while decorating and going back after Done at 1024 x 768 and 460 x 320, and keeping its size elsewhere; and the menu icon, in colour and in greyscale (C7, C9, C16, open questions 145 and 498 to 505). For the lily pad pond (issue #75), they cover swimming Area 2 to its exit, whether it feels a little harder than the canal, the plastic worms (the stiff, shiny tell against the wiggling real worms, in colour and in greyscale; the "bleh", the green cheeks and the wobble never looking scary or sick; the tint without the wobble under reduced motion; and how the queasy slowdown feels), the tray of 8 Decorations, and the pond's boot bake size, measured by the owner (open question 127). and frame time with music and sounds on in the busiest areas (open question 477); and, for the hazard and rescue sounds (issue #166), with music on and with music off: under `npm run dev` with `?area=dev-danger-area`, a predator's ripple once per warning and nothing more when it gives up, a punting pole's ripple as its warning starts on screen and none for a pole off screen, a Shell perk block, the ghost net twang and the wriggle when the snag ends, catching a tadpole and a shrimp, each climbing with the ladder and boinging, and a shrimp's flick; whether the ripple, the lullaby, the "bleh" and the twang feel kind and never scary, and whether a punting pole that stays on screen and ripples at every warning, every 4 seconds, or staggered poles rippling more often, ever feels like a warning repeating or building (S9, S11; open question 483); every new sound about as loud as the others, with the shrimp flick a little softer (S13); and frame time with music and sounds on in the busiest areas (open question 496). For the Dash pot tip (B11, issue #149), they cover: whether an 8-year-old reads and follows it; that it names Space after a key press and Dash after a tap; that it never shows during a warning or a strike; closing it by a tap, by Enter or Space, and by waiting; and its fit at 460 x 320 and 320 x 440, in colour and in greyscale.
- Every acceptance criterion ID maps to either a rule test or an item on the play-test checklist.
- There are no browser end-to-end tests yet, per [ADR 0006](../adr/0006-testing-strategy.md).

## Success Metrics

Measured in supervised play-tests. The game has no analytics.

- At least 4 of 5 test kids can explain, after one trip, that finds are safe only after the Home Spot.
- No test kid shows fear beyond mild disappointment when carried home. Most choose to go back out.
- Most test kids find a Rare or better treasure within 15 minutes. Some, but not all, find a Legendary in a first session.
- Test kids decorate the tank or dress up axolotls without being prompted.
- After a deploy, testers see the new version number on the first reload, or after one tap.
- Zero reports of lost banked progress across reloads and updates.
- Frame time stays within the [ADR 0013](../adr/0013-axolotl-rig-and-frame-budget.md) budget in its four cases (the fourth is the dev test area): p95 at most 33 ms under 6x CPU throttling, p95 at most 16.7 ms on the owner's phone, and axolotl drawing at most 2 ms a frame. The developer measures this with the dev-only frame-time overlay, not in play-tests (open question 106).

## Out of Scope

These are not part of the MVP. Several are planned next.

- Sound and music. Both are built after the MVP, under issue #147: background music is N6, and sound effects are N7.
- Breeding (simple genetics).
- Three challenge levels.
- Restoring the lake and the Glow Gills power.
- New predators and hazards (see N3). The heron is built after the MVP, in Area 3 (issue #98), and is a wading stalker since issue #111. The ghost net is built after the MVP too, in Area 4 (issue #76), and the garter snake and the water hyacinth mats in Area 5 (issue #103).
- The GFP morph.
- Capacitor iOS and Android apps.
- Cloud saves, accounts, and sync.
- Multiple save slots or player profiles.
- Shops, currency, and trading.
- Trading or gifting Wearables.
- Perks stacking beyond their caps.
- Ads, purchases, and analytics.
- Areas beyond the one canal (see After MVP, N2). The lily pad pond, Area 2, is built after the MVP (issue #75), and so are Area 3, the pond at dusk (issue #98), Area 4, the chinampa gardens (issue #76), and Area 5, the chinampa gardens again (issue #103).
- Magic habitats, such as reefs. Dream colours are colours only.
- Everything in After MVP (N1 to N7), except the area framework already built for N2 and N3 (issue #73): areas as data, the area map, unlocking, and shared predator and hazard rules; the lily pad pond, Area 2, with its plastic worms (issue #75) and its tilapia chase, tadpoles, and freshwater shrimp (issue #121); the pond at dusk, Area 3, with two wading herons (issues #98 and #111), darting snacks (issue #98), and its tilapia chase, tadpoles, and freshwater shrimp, which replaced the darting snacks (issue #120); the Xochimilco refuge, with its habitat milestones (issue #74); the chinampa gardens, Area 4, with its ghost nets, up-and-down scrolling, and the first step of the predator ladder (issue #76); and Area 5, the first U-turn area, with its garter snakes and water hyacinth mats (issue #103) and its tilapia and garter snake chase, punting poles, tadpoles, and freshwater shrimp (issue #118); and the danger pass's shared parts, the chase rule, moving hazards, the common carp, drifting ghost nets, and mobile snacks (issue #117), opt-in per area, and on only where an area's clause above names them.

## After MVP

These come after the MVP, in this order.
They are direction, not MVP acceptance criteria.
The N IDs exist so issues can link to them.
Each one follows [ADR 0007](../adr/0007-kid-safety-and-privacy.md) and [ADR 0008](../adr/0008-gentle-fail-state.md).

1. **N1 Tank Top Model** (issue #27). The first feature after the MVP. It starts from the Home Tank. The player tries on owned Wearables with a preview, then does a short strut or pose in a spotlight. It ends with a cheerful, kid-friendly reaction. There is no failing and no harsh scoring.
2. **N2 Area chain and Xochimilco refuge** (epic issue #32; issues #73 to #78). The next post-MVP phase has at least 10 areas, with room to keep adding more. A habitat is a place that holds one or more areas, with its own look and its own signature predator, marked in the area table below. From this phase on, a habitat is a group of areas, not another name for one area (X9, and the glossary's "Area" and "Habitat" rows). Built in the area framework (issue #73): each area names its habitat in its data. The canal habitat holds Area 1, the lily pad pond habitat holds Area 2 (built, issue #75) and Area 3 (built, issue #98), and the chinampa gardens habitat holds Area 4 (built, issue #76) and Area 5 (built, issue #103).
    - Area table: the table below is the one place that lists each area's habitat, starting size, tilapia, and new things. Other sections link to it and do not repeat it. Sizes are in screens, wide x tall, and are starting points, except that area 4 stays the first area taller than one screen, so it is the first area that scrolls up and down. Each area adds at most one new hazard or predator and at most one new timing or puzzle element, and the table has one cell for each. Sizes and scrolling do not count toward this limit. Carry-forward: within a habitat, each later area keeps that habitat's earlier hazards and its signature predator, so area 7 keeps the crayfish and areas 9 and 10 keep the black bass. Across habitats nothing carries forward except the tilapia, which follows the table's tilapia column. Area 10 is the exception below, because it mixes the tilapia with every earlier predator.

        | Area | Habitat | Starting size | Tilapia | New hazard or predator | New timing or puzzle element |
        |---|---|---|---|---|---|
        | 1 | Canal (the MVP) | 4 x 1 | Yes (A1) | Tilapia (signature predator) | None |
        | 2 | Lily pad pond (built, issue #75) | 5 x 1 | Yes, 4 | Plastic worms (slow only, N3) | None |
        | 3 | Lily pad pond (built, issue #98) | 6 x 1 | Yes, 4 | Heron (signature predator) | None (issue #111 removed the heron's shadow cycle) |
        | 4 | Chinampa gardens (built, issue #76; danger pass, issue #119) | 6 x 2 | Yes, 4 | Ghost nets (slow only, N3) | None (its slot is up-and-down scrolling, which does not count) |
        | 5 | Chinampa gardens (built, issue #103; danger pass, issue #118) | 7 x 2 | Yes, 3 | Garter snake (signature predator) | Water hyacinth mats (N3) |
        | 6 | Spring cave | About 7 x 2 | None | Crayfish (signature predator) | Glow-plant puzzle |
        | 7 | Spring cave | About 8 x 2 | None | Pulsing current | Pebble puzzle |
        | 8 | Deep canal | About 8 x 2 | Yes | Black bass (signature predator) | None |
        | 9 | Deep canal | About 9 x 2 | Yes | Murky water | None |
        | 10 | Deep canal | About 10 x 2 | Yes | Trajinera boats | None |

        Area 10 mixes the tilapia with every earlier predator.

        Area 2 as built (issue #75; starting values, to tune in play-testing): the lily pad pond's water is a warmer green than the canal's, with lily pads along the surface, some with flowers, and a soft mud bottom with cattails and reeds at the edges ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md)). It unlocks once the canal is beaten (its exit star saved). It holds 4 tilapia, whose closest routes are about 465 pixels apart since issue #132 (290 before it), and which chase since issue #121, at every depth and on the ramp chase's 80 pixel leash since issue #132, and 2 common carp, `carp-0` and `carp-1`, since issue #132 (Area 2, danger pass, below); a hollow log, the cave (B6); a stream flowing in at the right end, the current, with spots past it; two fixed lily pads with hiding spots, one Common over the main path, at x 530 since issue #132 (it was at x 915), and one Rare between the hollow log and the stream, at x 1680 since issue #132, moved from x 1240 to x 1760 in issue #121 so the fairness check holds with the chase (open question 296), and 5 lily pads for scenery; 6 plastic worms (N3); 5 stranding places; 20 snacks (10 worms, 6 bugs, 2 tadpoles, and 2 freshwater shrimp since issue #121, with at least 4 worms or bugs in the stream, 4 placed there on purpose; 12 worms and 8 bugs before it); and 7 treasure slots (B4). A stranded axolotl appears on 40% of trips (C2), as in every area. The exit is at the right end. The special treasure is the Frog statue (Rare, H6), so no trip is sure to hold an Epic or a Legendary. Its snacks drift a little faster than the canal's (H4, Tuning values).

        Area 3 as built (issue #98; starting values, to tune in play-testing): the lily pad pond at dusk, in the lily pad pond habitat, with its own dusk look, a variant of the pond's look ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md); open question 168): a lavender-to-peach sky under a dim rose wash, dimmer light rays, warmer and dimmer water, mud, and reeds, and a few fireflies hovering above the water, 2 for each screen of width, still under reduced motion. It unlocks once Area 2 is beaten (its exit star saved). It is 6 x 1 screens and never scrolls up or down. It holds 4 tilapia, all before the herons' stretches, which chase on a short leash since issue #120 and patrol at every depth in two pairs of loops since issue #133, their closest routes 290 pixels apart before it (Area 3, danger pass, below); two herons, its signature predator, which stand in the water and wade near the surface at the far end, each along its own short stretch (N3, issue #111): `heron-0` over the spots past the stream, and `heron-1` over the root arch; two carp since issue #133, which chase on a short leash: `carp-0` on the bed under both heron stretches, rising into the middle water at the stream's end, and `carp-1` near the surface just past the second tilapia pair (Area 3, danger pass, below); a root arch under the bank near the far end, the cave (B6), with 2 Legendary Dash pots near its top and 2 Epic spots at its bottom; a stream flowing in from under the bank roots, before the root arch, the current, a little stronger than Area 2's, with spots past it, 2 Legendary Dash pots near the surface and 2 Epic spots lower; two fixed lily pads with hiding spots, one Common over the main path and one Rare near the exit, at x 2775 since issue #120, moved from x 1240 so the fairness check holds with the chase (open question 326), both out of every heron's reach, and 6 lily pads for scenery; 5 plastic worms (N3), each out of every heron's reach; 6 stranding places, each out of every heron's reach (open question 164); 24 snacks (10 worms, 6 bugs, 4 tadpoles, and 4 freshwater shrimp since issue #120, with at least 4 worms or bugs in the stream, 4 placed there on purpose; 14 worms and 10 bugs before it, 3 of which darted away a little; H4); and 8 treasure slots (B4). Every Epic and Legendary spot sits in the root arch or past the stream, where a heron can strike, and a deep lane below y 230 under each stretch stays out of every heron's reach. The main swim path runs at y 200, so under the stretches it is inside the herons' reach; the water below y 230 is not (open questions 208 and 214). A stranded axolotl appears on 40% of trips (C2), as in every area. The exit is at the right end, past the root arch, out of every heron's reach and, since issue #133, every tilapia and carp strike. The special treasure is the Wooden duck decoy (Rare, H6), so no trip is sure to hold an Epic or a Legendary. Its snacks drift a little faster than Area 2's (H4, Tuning values). The stream art's mouth is at the right edge of the screen column that holds the current's right end; there it is in open water, beside the root arch, so the band and its flow marks fade out over the last 40 pixels and the mouth stones that spill past the edge are drawn on the next screen (open question 172).

        Area 4 as built (issue #76; starting values, to tune in play-testing; [ADR 0018](../adr/0018-chinampa-gardens-ghost-nets-and-predator-ladder.md); open question 191): the chinampa gardens, the first area of the chinampa gardens habitat, with its own look ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md); open question 206): clear green-teal water, cooler than the pond's, and a darker bottom row under the same contrast rule as the pond look; banks that read as chinampa garden plots, with corn, flowers, and lettuce above the surface and ahuejote willow roots reaching into the water. It unlocks once Area 3 is beaten (its exit star saved). It is 6 x 2 screens, 2880 x 720 world pixels, the first area that scrolls up and down (H1). Its shape is down and right (the area shape rule below): the Home Spot is in the top left, at (120, 200), its safe area is its screen column from the surface down to the bed of the bottom row, and the exit is deep in the bottom right, at (2780, 625). Two ways lead from the Home Spot toward the exit: the top-row way runs under the overhanging banks of the garden plots, then dives between two ghost nets to the bottom row; the bottom-row way runs past the root tangle and between two more nets. Both meet at the gap between two chinampas in the bottom row, where the current flows, and the main path leaves it by a lane along the bed to the exit. It holds 4 tilapia (issue #134): one over the root tangle; one on a slanting loop from the surface band down beside the spots past the current, under the top-right bank; one on a tall narrow loop between the bottom-row nets and the main path's dive; and one at the right edge of the top right. Each reaches into two depth bands, and together they reach all three. Every two are more than twice their lunge range apart, and all play Area 4's own tuning, with its chase (the predator ladder below). It also holds 4 common carp on tall narrow loops (issue #134): one under the main path's dive, one beyond it, and one in the current, each from the middle band down to the floor, and one in the top row of the gap screen, from the surface band down to the middle; and two punting poles, in the first screen and in the gap screen (Area 4, danger pass, below); 5 ghost nets (N3), the area's one new thing, the two in the bottom row drifting; a tangle of ahuejote roots on the bed of the bottom left, the cave (B6), a dead end with Legendary Dash pots near its top and Epic spots lower down; the current, water flowing left through the gap between two chinampas in the bottom row, a little weaker than Area 3's stream, with Legendary and Epic spots just past it; under-bank spots (B5), 6 Common ones under the first banks, near the main path, and 6 Rare ones under the bank in the top right, a dead end off the path; 6 stranding places; 28 snacks (8 worms, 6 bugs, 7 tadpoles, and 7 freshwater shrimp since issue #119, the tadpoles on wander loops and the shrimp on homes they hop about since issue #134, with at least 4 snacks in the current, 4 placed there on purpose, every one a worm or a bug, and no one-time darters; 16 worms and 12 bugs before it; H4); and 9 treasure slots (B4). It has no plastic worms, no heron, no reeds, and no lily pads: hazards carry forward only within a habitat. A stranded axolotl appears on 40% of trips (C2), as in every area. The special treasure is the Scarecrow (Rare, H6), so no trip is sure to hold an Epic or a Legendary. Its snacks drift a little faster than Area 3's (H4, Tuning values). The gap's chinampa edges are drawn just above and below the current (open question 203), and the Home Spot's burrow, which stands on the bed in a one-row area, stands at the Home Spot in the top row (open question 204).

        Area 5 as built (issue #103; owner decisions on issue #103, 2026-10-22 and 2026-10-23; its danger pass, issues #118 and #135, below; starting values, to tune in play-testing; [ADR 0020](../adr/0020-u-turn-areas-garter-snake-and-hyacinth-mats.md); open questions 221 to 247, 303 to 318, and 382 to 390): the chinampa gardens again, the second area of the chinampa gardens habitat, with the habitat's own look and no variant. It unlocks once Area 4 is beaten (its exit star saved), by area-table order, with no new rule. It is 7 x 2 screens, 3360 x 720 world pixels, with the bed top at y 678, and it scrolls up and down as Area 4 does (H1). Its shape is a U-turn (the area shape rule below): the Home Spot is in the top left, at (120, 200); the main path runs right along the top row, goes down in the rightmost screen column, and runs back left along the bottom row to the exit, straight under the Home Spot, at (120, 625) (open questions 222 and 225). Its safe area is the Home Spot's column in the top row only, x 40 to 300, from the surface down to y 360, so the exit is outside it. Along the top row the main path runs under the first bank, past the Common spots, then deep under the first garter snake's stretch, through the first water hyacinth channel, under the second bank, and deep under the other two snakes' stretches; along the bottom row it runs through the gap current, between two ghost nets, under the first channel's mats, and through the second channel to the exit. It holds 3 tilapia (issues #118 and #135), each loop reaching into two depths: one on a tall loop beside the root tangle, from the bottom-row path down to the spots past the current, one just past the current, from near the surface down into the middle of the water, under the second snake's stretch, and one up and down the right edge of the bottom row, more than twice their lunge range apart and playing Area 5's own tuning (the predator ladder below), with the chase; 3 garter snakes (N3; issue #118), the area's one new predator, each on its own short stretch at the surface in open top-row water, with a deep lane always open below it, each chasing along its stretch; 5 common carp (N3; issue #135), meaner than Area 4's, each on its own loop reaching into two depths, so carp patrol the water from near the surface down to the bed, two of them in the bottom row west of x 1850, each with the chase; 5 punting poles (N3; issue #118), a danger part, jabbing down across both rows of the main path in the west half; 5 ghost nets (N3), carried forward from Area 4; 2 water hyacinth mat pairs (N3), the area's one timing element, the first gating the top row and the second the bottom row, each placed so the way round it, through the other row, passes a ghost net (open question 226); a tangle of ahuejote roots on the bed of the bottom row, the cave (B6), a dead end with 2 Legendary Dash pots and 2 Epic spots; the current, water flowing right through the gap between two chinampas in the bottom row, against the main path, a little stronger than Area 4's, with 2 Legendary Dash pots and 2 Epic spots just past it, on its upstream side (B6; open question 223); under-bank spots (B5), 6 Common ones under the first two banks, near the main path, and 8 Rare ones off it, 1 at the right end of each of the first two banks and 6 under the bank in the top right, a dead end; 6 stranding places; 32 snacks (7 worms, 6 bugs, 10 tadpoles, and 9 freshwater shrimp, with 4 worms or bugs in the current; issue #118), with no one-time darters (H4); and 10 treasure slots (B4). It has no plastic worms, no heron, no reeds, and no lily pads. A stranded axolotl appears on 40% of trips (C2), as in every area; Area 5 sets no chance of its own. The special treasure is the Piñata (Rare, H6), so no trip is sure to hold an Epic or a Legendary. Its snacks drift a little faster than Area 4's (H4, Tuning values). The garden plots stand on the top row of the 2nd, 5th, and 7th screens, none over a snake, the first channel, or the gap current.
    - Growth: across the chain, from area 1 to area 10, areas grow in size, length, complexity, and risk. Along the way they bring more snacks, faster snacks (some dart away a little), stronger currents, and the timing and puzzle elements in the area table.
    - Predator ladder (owner decision on epic #32, 2026-10-22; first applied in Area 4, issue #76): from Area 4 on, each new area tightens its predators a step: a shorter warning, a faster and longer strike, a shorter cooldown, and a faster patrol. The values are set per area, as area data. The warning never goes below the 0.8 second floor (A2), so every strike stays avoidable with skill. Areas 1 to 3 are unchanged. The heron's Area 3 step stays as it is: a 1.2 second warning on sight, a 110 pixel stab reach, and a 3 second cooldown; a later area with a heron tightens it (N3). Area 4's tilapia warn for 0.85 seconds and strike at 400 pixels per second, reach 145 pixels, cool down for 2.2 seconds, and patrol at 50 pixels per second (N3, Tuning values; open question 192). Since its danger pass (issue #119), they also chase with the full-bar chase: sight 240 pixels, leash 160, and speed 125 pixels per second (open question 330). An area's tilapia tuning also sets its risk ladder: its strike range S is that tuning's lunge range (B10). Area 5's tilapia (issue #103) tighten one more step: strike at 420 pixels per second, reach 150 pixels, cool down for 2 seconds, and patrol at 55 pixels per second. Issue #103 kept Area 4's 0.85 second warning, the value the owner set; since issue #118 the warning is 0.825 seconds, one 0.025 second step under Area 4's and still above the floor (open questions 232 and 304). The 0.8 second floor, the 0.4 second strike, and every other value stay the same (Tuning values). The garter snake's first step, in Area 5, is a 0.8 second warning, the floor, a dart at 360 pixels per second for 0.35 seconds, a 110 pixel reach, a 2 second cooldown, and patrol at 55 pixels per second (N3).
    - Level layout ladder (owner decision on epic #32, 2026-10-22; applied in Area 4, issue #76): alongside the predator ladder, the levels themselves get harder from Area 4 on: more branching routes; dead ends that hold treasure, a reward for exploring and never a trap; and tighter gaps between obstacles, never under the axolotl's safe passing width. Every area must still be beatable with one leg, the exit must always be reachable, and every hit must stay avoidable with skill. Each area also uses its timing or puzzle slot (its one new hazard or predator plus at most one timing or puzzle element); Area 4's slot is up-and-down scrolling. Area 4 reads the ladder without walls, because the area framework is open water with no solid terrain (ADR 0018; open question 196):
        - Obstacles: the ghost nets, kept out of by their reach (a drifting net by its whole drift path), the tilapia and carp routes, kept out of by their strike range, and the punting poles' sweeps, kept out of by their touch reach (issue #119).
        - Branching routes: the top-row and bottom-row ways from the Home Spot to the gap current.
        - Dead ends: the root tangle in the bottom left and the bank in the top right, each holding treasure spots off the main path.
        - Gaps: a gap is the clear width between two obstacles' keep-outs, along the shortest line between them. The safe passing width is one body height, twice the net reach: 36 pixels. Every gap beside a net is at least that wide. Measured the same way, Area 3's narrowest hazard gap on its main path, between its first two plastic worms, `pond-2/plastic-worm-1` and `pond-2/plastic-worm-2`, is about 136.09 pixels. It was about 96.09 pixels, between its last plastic worm and the heron, until issue #111 raised the heron's reach from 60 to 110 pixels, which put Area 3's main path, at y 200, inside the herons' reach. Area 2's narrowest, between `pond-1/plastic-worm-2` and `pond-1/plastic-worm-3`, is about 143.47 pixels since issue #145, wider than Area 3's. Area 4's main path swims through a 64 pixel gap between two nets, tighter than Area 3's, and the bottom-row way has a 64 pixel gap between two nets too.
        - Area 5 (issues #103 and #135; open questions 226, 234, and 385) uses its timing slot for the water hyacinth mats, and reads the ladder without walls the same way. Its obstacles are the ghost nets, the tilapia, garter snake, and (since issue #135) carp reach, the current, and the mats. Its branching routes are through each mat channel or round it, through the other row, and the top-row and bottom-row ways. Its dead ends are the bank in the top right and the root tangle in the bottom row, each holding treasure spots. Each mat pair spans its row, so a closed pair gates that row, and the way round passes a ghost net: waiting for the channel is the skilful choice, and going round the risky one. The two pairs never gate both rows at the same x, and a way to the exit is always open. The gap measure moved into a pure module, `src/logic/layoutGaps.ts`, used by the Area 4 and Area 5 layout tests, with Area 4's numbers unchanged. Area 5's main path swims through a 48 pixel gap between `chinampa-2/ghost-net-2` and `chinampa-2/ghost-net-3`, at x 1917 to 1955 since issue #135 (at x 1110 before it), tighter than Area 4's 64 and wider than the 36 pixel safe passing width, and passes under the first channel's mats in the bottom row through a 50 pixel gap beside `chinampa-2/ghost-net-4`.
    - Danger pass (owner decisions on issue #117, 2026-10-23; [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md); open questions 248 to 278): after the owner's play test of Area 4 on 2026-10-23 found "zero danger" and snacks that "barely move", the areas from Area 2 on get more danger, and snacks that are harder to catch.
        - Phase split (owner decision on issue #117, 2026-10-23): phase 1, issue #117, builds the shared parts only, each opt-in per area or per predator and off in every shipped area: the chase rule, the moving hazard type with the trajinera punting pole, the common carp, and drifting ghost nets (N3), and the mobile snacks, the tadpole and the freshwater shrimp (H4). The dev area `dev-danger-area` shows every part, in dev builds only (open question 262), and the goldens for Areas 1 to 5 stayed byte-identical through it (open question 263); each phase 2 issue re-records only its own area's golden, on purpose (open question 297). Phase 2 is one issue per area, each adding its own danger parts and mobile snacks as data in its own files: Area 4 is issue #119, Area 5 is issue #118, Area 3 is issue #120, and Area 2 is issue #121 (open question 261). Until an area's phase 2 issue is built, the area plays exactly as before.
        - Full danger bar, from Area 4 on (open questions 259 and 343 to 349): about one moving threat per screen, so at least ⌈0.75 x screens⌉ moving threats, where the screens are the area's width over 480 times its height over 360, and each tilapia route, each other predator route, and each moving hazard is one moving threat; at least 2 predator species and at least 1 moving hazard type; every predator chases (N3, the chase rule), so there is no easy out-swim; the whole-water test (below): no quiet swim anywhere in the swim water is longer than 480 pixels, one screen across; and the depth rule, the floor rule and the carp rule (below) hold. Only the Home Spot and the safe area are threat-free.
        - Ramp bar, Areas 2 and 3 (open questions 259 and 343 to 349): the tilapia chase, with a short leash; the whole-water test (below): no quiet swim anywhere in the swim water is longer than 480 pixels, the density check against the area's size; the depth rule, the floor rule and the carp rule (below) hold; and no moving hazard type in Area 2, and at most one in Area 3.
        - Area 1 stays gentle: it is the first area, where players learn, so it has no chase, no moving hazard, and no mobile snack.
        - In reach (open questions 343 to 345): within a predator's lunge range plus its leash of its patrol loop (a tilapia by its area's own tuning; no leash for a predator that does not chase, or that warns on route), or within a moving hazard's touch reach of its whole sweep. Cover is ignored, and still hazards do not count. The whole-water test replaces the main path measure. It covers the swim water, x 40 to the area's width less 40 and y 94 to the bed top less 22: all of it is open water, across the line between screen rows too, and ghost nets, water hyacinth mats, currents and bank overhangs are water. The safe area is home, never quiet and never a threat. A grid covers the swim water, one point every 8 pixels, with its last row and column on the water's edges, and each point is in reach, home, or quiet. Quiet points that touch, diagonals included, make one quiet pocket. A quiet swim starts at an in-reach or home point, goes through the quiet points of one pocket only, and ends at an in-reach or home point or at the water's edge; its length is the shortest such way on the grid, 8 pixels across or up and down and about 11.3 pixels on a diagonal. So whatever way the axolotl swims, along the surface, along the floor or anywhere between, it never swims more than one screen without a threat in reach. Only this measure counts the leash; the risk ladder (B10) and the gap measure do not (open question 252). Each phase 2 issue proves its bar in its own layout test, with `src/logic/dangerBar.ts`.
        - Depth rule (owner decisions on issue #130, 2026-10-24; open questions 346 and 347): the swim water's height splits into three equal depth bands, surface, middle and floor; one screen tall, they are y 94 to 161, 161 to 229, and 229 to 296. It covers every tilapia loop, every carp loop, and the black bass's loops once it is built. Each loop reaches at least 24 pixels, a tilapia body, into at least two of the three bands, so no loop is held on one line, and each species' loops together reach at least 24 pixels into every band. So swimming predators patrol at every depth.
        - Floor rule (owner decision on issue #130, 2026-10-24; open question 348): an area has surface danger when it has any moving hazard, or any predator route lying wholly inside the surface band. An area with surface danger has predators on the floor: the floor lane, the line along the bottom of the swim water (y = the bed top less 22), sampled every pixel with the safe area counted as a threat point, has no stretch longer than 480 pixels out of every predator's reach. Only predators count here, not moving hazards.
        - Carp rule (owner decisions on issue #130, 2026-10-24; open question 349): an area has at least as many carp routes as its screens divided by 3, rounded to the nearest whole number with halves up, so 2 carp for 5 screens, 4 for 12, and 10 for 30. An area's crayfish, where it has them, come on top of its carp.
        - Gentle stakes stay (G2): leg loss is a sparkle poof, legs regrow, and the exit is reachable with 1 to 4 legs (A9). Every strike is warned for at least the 0.8 second floor (A2) and avoidable with skill, one hit costs one leg, every area can be beaten with one leg, and the predator ladder and the level layout ladder above still hold.
        - Snack ramp (owner decision on issue #117, 2026-10-23; open question 278): beside the danger bar, every area issue from Area 2 on must meet the snack ramp in H4: a growing share of mobile snacks, with faster tadpoles and quicker shrimp area by area, rising to a cap of about 70% from Area 6 on. Area 1 has no mobile snacks. Each area issue proves its own row in its own layout test.
        - New things (#32's one-new-thing rule; open questions 260 and 274): the danger parts, the common carp and every moving hazard type, are outside the one-new-thing rule of the area table above, so an area may add them beside its one new hazard or predator and its one timing or puzzle element. The mark is on the carp's species data, and comes with every moving hazard type; it is never a shared list. The danger parts are still threats, met, and in the Collection Book (C14). Mobile snack types are snacks, not hazards, predators, or timing elements, so neither the one-new-thing rule nor the danger bar counts them.
        - Each area has its own danger pass paragraph below, so each phase 2 issue edits only its own: its predators, its chase, its moving hazards and drifting nets, and the snack values it built (its counts, tadpole speed, shrimp values, and Snack magnet pull). A changed snack value goes in its paragraph and a new open question, never in the H4 table. Each paragraph says whether its area has one-time darters or freshwater shrimp (H4).

        Area 1, danger pass: stays gentle, with no change. The canal zone has at least three tilapia, with no chase. It has no moving hazard, no drifting ghost net, and no mobile snack: 16 snacks, 10 worms and 6 bugs, with no one-time darters.

        Area 2, danger pass: built in issue #121 (starting values, to tune in play-testing; open questions 289 to 300), and brought to the whole-water ramp bar in issue #132 (named defaults ND-1 to ND-10; owner decisions on issue #132, 2026-10-25; open questions 372 to 381). The lily pad pond (Area 2) meets the ramp bar. Its four tilapia, with no new one, play `POND_TILAPIA`: `TILAPIA` with the ramp chase, `TILAPIA_RAMP_CHASE`, as it is since issue #132, sight 200 pixels, leash 80, and speed 115 pixels per second; from issue #121 to issue #132 the leash was 60, the shortest of any area (open questions 289 and 372). Every other value is `TILAPIA`'s, so its risk bands do not change. Since issue #132 each tilapia loop reaches into at least two depth bands (owner decision on issue #132, 2026-10-25, that the danger layout is laid out again; open question 373): `tilapia-0` loops x 326 to 346, y 204 to 296, on the deep side beside the safe area (it was x 700 to 790, y 262 to 296); `tilapia-1` x 960 to 980, y 204 to 296, past `carp-0` (it was x 1080 to 1150, y 250 to 296); `tilapia-2` x 1445 to 1505, y 100 to 186, near the surface over the hollow log (it was x 1432 to 1588, y 110 to 144); and `tilapia-3` stays, x 2080 to 2145, y 100 to 296, through the stream. The closest two, `tilapia-1` and `tilapia-2`, are about 465 pixels apart, more than twice the 140 pixel lunge range; `tilapia-0` and `tilapia-1`, 290 pixels apart before, are 614. Two common carp, `carp-0` and `carp-1`, danger parts, play `POND_CARP`: `CARP` with a chase of sight 220 pixels, leash 60, and speed 115 pixels per second, and `CARP`'s own values otherwise, a 1.0 second warning and a slow 120 pixel sweep, so Area 2 stays gentle. `carp-0` loops x 729 to 749, y 204 to 296, on the deep side between the first Common reeds and `tilapia-1`, and `carp-1` loops x 1860 to 1870, y 100 to 190, near the surface in the stream before `tilapia-3`; together they reach into all three depth bands. Every carp loop is at least 210 pixels from every tilapia route, the spacing chosen inside the owner's 210 to 220, never 200 or less (owner decisions on issue #132, 2026-10-25): `carp-1` to `tilapia-3` is 210 and `carp-0` to `tilapia-1` is 211. The two carp loops are about 1111 pixels apart, more than 240 (open question 374). It has no moving hazard and no drifting ghost net (open question 291). Measured with the whole-water test, with a tilapia reach of 220 pixels, the 140 pixel lunge range plus the 80 pixel leash, and a carp reach of 180 pixels, the 120 pixel sweep plus the 60 pixel leash, the longest quiet swim is about 241.4 pixels, from (1256, 296) on the bed up to (1168, 94) at the surface, between `tilapia-1` and `tilapia-2`, under the bar's 480. Every tilapia and carp loop reaches 24 pixels into at least two of the three depth bands, and each species into all three, so the depth rule holds. It has no surface danger, so the floor rule holds, with a floor quiet stretch of 55 pixels. Its 2 carp meet the carp rule's 2 for 5 screens (open question 377). The whole-water test replaces the main path measure, whose longest quiet stretch was 390 pixels (open question 290). So that the bar and every fair start hold, the danger layout moved with the tilapia (open question 373): the first Common reeds from x 316, 372, 428, and 484 to x 548, at y 172 and 228, with second Common reeds added at x 1212, at the same heights, before `tilapia-2`; the top Rare reeds from x 580 and 640 to x 470, at y 110; the deep Rare reeds at y 288 from x 1316, 1376, and 1436 to x 1124, past `tilapia-1`, and from x 1700 to x 1644 and 1712, between the hollow log and the stream; the hollow log's Legendary pots from (1492, 206), (1552, 206), and (1612, 206) to (1480, 242), (1536, 248), and (1566, 200), and its Epic spots from (1492, 266), (1552, 266), and (1612, 266) to (1496, 296), (1566, 296), and (1596, 248); the Common lily pad from x 915 to x 530, by the first Common reeds, the Rare lily pad from x 1760 to x 1680, still between the hollow log and the stream, and the scenery pad from x 640 to x 900; the six plastic worms from (420, 120), (540, 290), (720, 105), (960, 170), (1280, 150), and (1300, 270) to (1185, 104), (1225, 150), (1245, 104), (1265, 150), (1290, 104), and (1300, 150), near the surface between `tilapia-1` and `tilapia-2`, a clump, then, in issue #145, spread out again to (488, 100), (585, 294), (670, 96), (855, 96), (1080, 96), and (1300, 144): near the surface past `tilapia-0` by the top Rare reeds, on the bed before `carp-0`, near the surface either side of `carp-0`, near the surface past `tilapia-1`, and in the open water before `tilapia-2`, at least 180 pixels apart and over about 1.7 screens of x (open question 426), each farther than a lunge from every tilapia route and a sweep from every carp route; the first four stranding places from (320, 110), (880, 292), (1340, 220), and (1700, 140) to (400, 110), (640, 290), (1330, 220), and (1780, 296), each at least 80 pixels from every tilapia and carp route; and the main path, which now runs from the Home Spot along y 200 and up round the Common pad, through (500, 200), (530, 145), and (560, 200), then on to the exit. The current spots and the exit stay, and the exit is farther than a carp sweep from every carp loop. Every spot keeps a fair start on issue #130's relaxed safe start: farther than 220 pixels from every tilapia loop and 180 pixels from every carp loop (open question 378). It has 20 snacks, 20% of them mobile: 10 worms, 6 bugs, 2 tadpoles, and 2 freshwater shrimp, with at least 4 worms or bugs in the stream, 4 placed there on purpose (open question 292). The tadpoles swim at 40 pixels per second (issue #131) all around a wander loop placed after the predators (issue #132; open question 376). Since issue #145 both share one loop, along the bed between `tilapia-1` and `tilapia-2` with a thin spike up the middle, through (1180, 136), (1190, 236), (1310, 232), (1300, 294), (1140, 294), (990, 290), (985, 226), and (1170, 226), a lap of about 23.3 seconds, one from offset 0 seconds and one from about 11.7 seconds, half its lap, so the two are out of step. Tadpole loops and plastic worms both need water out of every predator's reach, and there are only two large stretches of it, by the first Common reeds and between `tilapia-1` and `tilapia-2`, so one shared loop leaves room to spread the worms (owner decision on issue #145, 2026-10-26; open question 426). From issue #132 to issue #145 the west tadpole looped from beside the first Common reeds out round `carp-0`, through (492, 110), (630, 104), (812, 170), (700, 290), (600, 288), and (492, 280), a lap of about 21.9 seconds, and the east one along the bed through (1034, 135), (1024, 288), (1200, 290), (1346, 286), (1200, 250), and (1100, 240), a lap of about 21.3 seconds, half its lap out of step. From issue #131 to issue #132 they swam their old 120 pixel lanes as 2-point loops (open question 367). The shrimp hop about 3-spot homes: (1650, 110), (1720, 110), and (1690, 160), near the surface between the hollow log and the stream, since issue #145 (it was (510, 120), (575, 140), and (525, 190), by the first Common reeds), and (1650, 285), (1720, 285), and (1690, 240), on the bed below it, with a 60 pixel trigger radius, a 2.0 second rest, and a 36 pixel flick at 100 pixels per second. Every loop and home meets the snack motion rules, the hard rules, and its threat rule (H4), and a one-leg axolotl catches each tadpole on its loop. Its Snack magnet pull is 48 pixels per second, 1.2 x 40, from its tadpoles (H4; open questions 293 and 362). It has freshwater shrimp, so it has no one-time darters (open question 294). Its layout test proves the bar, the depth, floor and carp rules, both chases, and its snack ramp row (open questions 295 and 377), and its golden was re-recorded on purpose, in issue #121, again in issue #132, and in issue #145 (open questions 297, 379, and 426).

        Area 3, danger pass: built in issue #120 (starting values, to tune in play-testing, posted on issue #120 before the build; open questions 319 to 329), and brought to the whole-water ramp bar in issue #133 (starting values, to tune in play-testing, posted on issue #133 before the build; open questions 408 to 418). The lily pad pond at dusk (Area 3) meets the ramp bar, with the whole-water test since issue #133. Its four tilapia, with no new one, play `POND_2_TILAPIA`: `TILAPIA` with the ramp chase, `TILAPIA_RAMP_CHASE`, sight 200 pixels and speed 115 pixels per second, on a 100 pixel leash since issue #133, one step over Area 2's and under the full bar's 160 (80 before it; owner OK on issue #133, 2026-10-24; open question 408). Every other value is `TILAPIA`'s, so its risk bands do not change, with S = 140 (open question 319). Its two herons keep their wading stalk, with no chase, and only one predator, a tilapia, either heron, or either carp, warns or strikes at a time (N3; open question 118). With a tilapia reach of 220 pixels, the 140 pixel lunge range plus the 80 pixel leash, and no predator added, the longest quiet stretch of the main path was 369 pixels, under the bar's 480 (open question 320). Since issue #133 the tilapia patrol at every depth, in two pairs of taller loops: `tilapia-0`, x 836 to 876 and y 110 to 190, and `tilapia-1`, x 750 to 790 and y 204 to 296, then `tilapia-3`, x 1390 to 1440 and y 204 to 296, and `tilapia-2`, x 1486 to 1526 and y 110 to 190, each loop reaching into two depth bands (open question 409). Two common carp, `carp-0` and `carp-1`, come after the herons and play `POND_2_CARP`, the carp's tuning with a chase of sight 220, leash 80, and speed 115 (open question 410): `carp-0` patrols the bed under both heron stretches, through (2100, 200), (2200, 262), (2480, 262), (2480, 296), and (2100, 296), rising into the middle band at the stream's end, left of `heron-0`, so staying deep still dodges the herons but not the floor; `carp-1` loops near the surface just past the second tilapia pair, x 1586 to 1614 and y 110 to 190. With every spacing rule, the predators left no open water for the tadpole and shrimp homes, so the build stopped (open question 411), and the owner chose option A on issue #133 (2026-10-25): for Area 3 only, the tilapia-to-tilapia spacing (280 pixels) and the carp-to-tilapia spacing (260 pixels) are dropped, so the loops of each tilapia pair sit 48.1 pixels apart, their strikes overlapping, and leave open water between the pairs. Every other rule stays. The carp loops are more than 240 pixels apart. Q1, answer A: the floor carp stays more than a heron's reach, 110 pixels, from every heron stretch, instead of the two lunges added together, 230 pixels, and the other carp more than 230 pixels. Q2, answer A: the tilapia loops may move sideways, and the carp-to-tilapia relaxation it allowed is not needed under option A. The longest quiet swim in the whole water is about 334.4 pixels, from (544, 102) to (296, 296) (about 952.7 before), the floor's longest quiet stretch is 210 pixels (750 before), the depth rule holds, and it has 2 carp, the carp rule's target for 6 screens (open question 414). To fit them, `plastic-worm-3` moved from (960, 170) to (1030, 190), `plastic-worm-4` from (1300, 270) to (2650, 290), the Common pad from x 915 to x 1080, and three stranding places (Tuning values). Its narrowest main-path hazard gap stays `pond-2/plastic-worm-1` to `pond-2/plastic-worm-2`, about 136.09 pixels. It has no moving hazard, though the ramp bar allows one there, and no drifting ghost net (open question 321). It has 24 snacks, 33% of them mobile: 10 worms, 6 bugs, 4 tadpoles, and 4 freshwater shrimp, with at least 4 worms or bugs in the stream, 4 placed there on purpose (open question 322). The tadpoles swim at 50 pixels per second (issue #131; open question 362). Since issue #133 they wander 4 homes placed after the predators, in the open water they leave, each loop 320 pixels wide, from near the surface to near the bed, a quarter of its own lap out of step, with home centres at x 470, 1050, 1300, and 1710, at least 240 pixels apart and none in the stream: by the Home Spot, round `plastic-worm-3` between the tilapia pairs, before the second pair, and past `carp-1` (Tuning values; open question 413). Before it, they swam their old 120 pixel lanes as 2-point loops (owner decision 7 on issue #131, 2026-10-24). The shrimp hop round 4 homes of 3 spots, two in each half, the last by the exit (Tuning values), with a 60 pixel trigger radius, a 1.7 second rest, and a 44 pixel flick at 115 pixels per second; before issue #133 each had a 1-spot home and only flicked. Every wander loop and home meets the snack motion rules and the threat rule (H4) on every seed, and a one-leg axolotl catches every tadpole. Its Snack magnet pull is 60 pixels per second, 1.2 x 50, from its tadpoles; its shrimp's top speed is about 21.1 (H4; open questions 323 and 362). It has freshwater shrimp, so it has no one-time darters: its area entry keeps the shared dart rule, and its shrimp turn it off. Every shrimp hop spot has a clear 44 pixel flick target, and the stream did not move (open question 324). So the fairness check holds with the chase, its three deep Rare reed spots moved from x 1420, 1476, and 1532 at y 292 to x 2604, 2660, and 2716 at y 256, and its Rare lily pad from x 1240 to x 2775, both into `heron-1`'s Rare band, right of the root arch, and its sixth stranding place moved from (2780, 140) to (2720, 180) (open question 326). Since issue #133, every pool spot's safe start is more than 240 pixels from every tilapia loop, more than 200 pixels from every carp loop, and out of every heron's reach, and every spot has a fair start (open question 415). Its layout test proves the bar and its snack ramp row (open questions 325 and 414), and its golden was re-recorded on purpose (open questions 327 and 416).

        Area 4, danger pass: built, issue #119 (named defaults ND-1 to ND-13, open questions 330 to 342), and brought up to the whole-water danger bar in issue #134 (named defaults ND-1 to ND-10, open questions 391 to 400; owner decision on issue #134, 2026-10-25, option (b): 4 tilapia and 4 carp; starting values, to tune in play-testing). Area 4 meets the full danger bar by the whole-water test on every seed. Measured: 10 moving threats over its 12 screens (4 tilapia, 4 carp, and 2 punting poles), at least the ⌈0.75 x 12⌉ = 9 it needs; 2 predator species; 1 moving hazard type; every predator chasing; and a longest quiet swim in the whole water of 365.02 pixels, from (1456, 102) to (1768, 214), under the 480 limit. Its depth bands are y 94 to 281.33 (surface), 281.33 to 468.67 (middle), and 468.67 to 656 (floor). Every tilapia and carp loop reaches 24 pixels or more into two bands, and each species reaches all three (the depth rule). Its poles are surface danger, so the floor rule applies: the longest stretch of the floor lane out of every predator's reach is 19 pixels, at most 480. It has 4 carp, the carp target for 12 screens (the carp rule). Its 4 tilapia, `tilapia-0` to `tilapia-3`, keep Area 4's own tuning, one step up the predator ladder, and chase with the full-bar chase: sight 240 pixels, leash 160, and speed 125 pixels per second.
