If the game closes first, the rescue is not kept, and that morph can be stranded again on a later trip. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 58 | In what order does a banner with no room retry at 20 pixels (X10, X6)? | Every place at 24 pixels first, then every place at 20 pixels, so 24 pixels wins wherever it fits anywhere. Only the snack banner and the first-trip hint retry; the find toast never shrinks. 20 pixels is still above the 18 pixel minimum (X6). The owner chose this fallback (option D in issue #61). Needs owner confirmation of the retry order. |
| 59 | What happens if reduced motion changes during the carry home (A11, X3)? | It takes effect on the next step. Turned on: the axolotl holds where it is in its still bubble and arrives after the rest of the carry or the still hold time, whichever is shorter, so the switch never lengthens the carry. Turned off: it floats home from where it is, timed like a fresh carry. Each switch off starts a fresh float, so toggling many times can lengthen the carry; this is driven by the player's device setting and each float is capped at the carry's longest time. From issue #34. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 60 | How many treasures does each trip hide (B4)? | Replaced by open question 137 (issue #94): each area has a set number of treasure slots, 6 in the canal, and chance picks each slot's rarity. The fixed 5 (2 Common, 1 Rare, 1 Epic, and 1 Legendary, issue #39) is gone. |
| 61 | How often does each rarity turn up, and where does a missed share go (B4)? | Replaced by open question 137 (issue #94): each slot is Common 65%, Rare 27%, Epic 6%, or Legendary 2%, so no trip is sure to hold an Epic or a Legendary. Rare on 80% of trips, Epic on 50%, and Legendary on 25% (issue #39) is gone. |
| 62 | What happens when only a few treasures are left (B4)? | Replaced by open questions 137 and 140 (issue #94), and updated by issue #111 (owner decision, 2026-10-22): a slot whose rarity has none left takes a more common rarity with one left, nearest first, then only the next rarer one (Common to Rare, Rare to Epic, Epic to Legendary). A slot with nothing left at those rarities stays empty. So a Legendary comes only from its own roll, or from an Epic slot once the area's Epics are all banked, and a save that has banked every Common, Rare, and Epic never gets a trip of all Legendaries unless every slot rolled Epic or Legendary. When only Common and Rare treasures are left and there are enough slots for all of them, all of them are placed (B4). |
| 63 | Are lost treasures placed on the next trip (A12, B4, D3)? | Yes, always, in this play session. They are extras on top of the treasure slots and do not use up a slot (updated by issue #94). This is never saved, so after the game is closed or reloaded they rejoin their area and can turn up on a later trip. Owner confirmed 2026-10-20. |
| 64 | Is the area's special treasure on every trip (B4, H6)? | Yes. It is always placed while it is unbanked, as an extra on top of the treasure slots, so Star 2 can be earned on every trip. It is always Common or Rare (open question 136). Owner confirmed 2026-10-20; updated by issues #93 and #94. |
| 65 | Do the hint sparkles change now that fewer treasures hide (B7)? | No. The hint ranges stay the same. Owner confirmed 2026-10-20. |
| 66 | Are better-hidden spots picked more often (B4)? | Yes. Each spot's weight goes from 1 for the spot nearest the main swim path to 3 for the farthest, linear within each treasure's pool. Any spot can still be picked. Common spots are all the same distance from the path today, so Common is not affected. Owner confirmed starting value 2026-10-20. Tune after play-testing. |
| 67 | Which Wearables fill the four new slots (B3, F8)? | One each, so every slot has one Wearable: Tank top (Shirt, Common, Regrowth 10%), Diving tank (Back, Rare, Dash 15%), Stripy socks (Socks, Common, Snack magnet 30), and Flippers (Feet, Rare, Speed 6%). They are added after the Paint shells in the catalogue, so the trip seed still picks the same spots for every earlier treasure. Shell perks stay on Epic and Legendary Wearables only (the Golden bow). No Wearable has a set ID yet (Implementation Decision 12). Needs confirmation. |
| 68 | Where does the player dress an axolotl (C10, F6, F8)? | On a restyle-only Outfit tab, right after the Dream colour tab. Its slots view shows the eight slots, each with its name, the worn Wearable and its perk icon, or a dashed outline and a plus badge when empty, then the outfit summary, or a caption for the focused slot when the summary does not fit. A slot opens its choices view: "None", then the banked Wearables for that slot, each with its perk and "On <name>" when another axolotl wears it. "Back to slots" and Escape return to the slots view. At 460 x 320 the slots view shows 3 slots a page, and the choices view hides the tab row and puts each label beside its picture. With the pond's and Area 3's Wearables, the choices view shows 1 choice a page at 460 x 320 and 320 x 440, with More wearables (issues #75 and #98; open question 152). With Area 5's Wearables, Head and Back have 7 choices (None and six), and at 320 x 440 no earlier fit step holds 2 or more choices, so a last fit step, 9, follows, tried only when nothing before it fits: one choice a page, with More wearables beside the picture and the label block under them, wrapped at the band width. It is used only at 320 x 440, and every layout that fit before is unchanged (owner decision on issue #103, comment 6374010919; F6). Done keeps the outfit; Cancel leaves without keeping it. Needs confirmation. |
| 69 | What does the Go exploring flow look like (F7)? | The explorer card, "Who's going exploring?", over the dimmed Home Tank: every owned axolotl as a picture in its own look and outfit, the chosen one's outfit summary, and Go!, Change outfit, and Back. The remembered explorer is chosen when it opens, else the first owned axolotl. Change outfit opens the Outfit tab and comes back to the card. Needs confirmation. |
| 70 | What does the Dash perk change (F9)? | Only the dash length: each Tail Dash lasts longer by the perk's percent, up to the cap. The recharge stays the same. One rule is simpler to explain than "longer, or a shorter recharge", and the Dash button's recharge timing stays the same for every outfit. Needs confirmation. |
| 71 | How does the Snack magnet pull snacks (A19, F9)? | Each snack within the perk's radius of the explorer's head moves straight toward the head at 40 pixels per second, the same for every radius. That is about 3 times the fastest snack drift in Area 1, so the pull is easy to see, and well under the slowest swim (90 pixels per second with one leg), so the explorer still swims to its snacks. At the 70 pixel cap a snack crosses the whole radius in under 2 seconds. In Area 2 the drift peaks at about 14.8 pixels per second, in Area 3 at about 15.2, in Area 4 at about 15.6, and in Area 5 at about 15.8, so the pull is still at least twice the drift (issues #75, #98, #76, and #103). The pull never moves a plastic worm (N3). The eat reach does not change. Needs confirmation after play-tests. |
| 72 | What shows when a Shell perk blocks a hit (A22)? | Four small twinkles round the axolotl, once, for 0.5 seconds; under reduced motion they show still for 0.5 seconds. No leg is lost and there is no leg-loss puff. The protected ring starts as after any hit. The HUD does not change: the legs display stays the same, and there is no Shell counter. Needs confirmation. |
| 73 | When do Socks and Feet change with the legs (F8)? | In the picture, a lost leg's sock and shoe go at once with the leg, under its puff. They come back only when that leg has fully regrown, never on a part-grown leg. The Socks and Feet perks keep working while at least one leg is left. Losing the fourth leg ends the trip, so in play they work for the whole trip. Needs confirmation. |
| 74 | How is each perk worded (F10)? | Its name and its value, for example "Speed +4%", "Regrowth +15%", "Dash +15%", "Treasure sense +35%", "Snack magnet +30" (the pull radius in pixels, with no unit shown), and "Shell blocks 1 hit". The outfit summary shows each total in the same words, with "max" after a total at its cap, or "No perks yet". Needs confirmation of the wording, especially "Snack magnet +30". |
| 75 | Why does Go! wait before it takes presses (F7)? | So the press or key that opened the explorer card cannot also start the trip. Go! ignores presses for the first 0.8 seconds the card shows, like the OK button on the tank arrival card and the go buttons of "Start over". The other buttons work at once. Needs confirmation. |
| 76 | What does the explorer card hide on the smallest canvases (F7)? | With the longest names and a six-row outfit summary, it shows 1 axolotl picture per page at 460 x 320 and at 320 x 440, with no title and no outfit summary. At 460 x 320 "More axolotls" also moves beside the picture. The names and the buttons always show, and "Change outfit" still shows the summary on the Outfit tab. Larger canvases show more pictures, the title, and the summary. Needs confirmation. |
| 77 | Does "Surprise me" change the outfit (F6)? | No. It never changes the outfit, like the colour, the boy or girl, and the Dream colour. It changes only the current tab's choice: a look option on a look tab, or a name on the Name tab. On the Outfit tab it is hidden (open question 108). Needs confirmation. |
| 78 | How is the canal laid out so every rarity has spots in its risk band (A1, B10)? | The three tilapia patrol small loops, left to right: `tilapia-0` through (700, 262), (790, 262), (790, 296), (700, 296); `tilapia-1` through (1080, 100), (1145, 100), (1145, 296), (1080, 296); and `tilapia-2` through (1432, 110), (1588, 110), (1588, 144), (1432, 144). Neighbouring routes are 290 and 287 pixels apart, at least twice the lunge range, so A1 still holds. The reed beds have fixed hiding columns, so reed spots are fixed area data and are no longer seeded. Only lily pad 2 hosts a spot. The cave and past-current spots are re-gridded inside the same cave and current. Stranding place 3 moves to (1340, 220), and every stranding place stays at least 80 pixels from every route. The pools hold 8 Common, 7 Rare, 6 Epic, and 6 Legendary spots. Needs confirmation. |
| 79 | Where are the risk band edges (B10)? | Common farther than 200 pixels from every route, Rare more than 140 and at most 200, Epic more than 70 and at most 140, and Legendary at most 70, in a Dash pot. The Rare and Epic edge is the lunge range, so only Epic and Legendary sit where a lunge can start. Every spot keeps 60 pixels from every turn. Measured over 1000 trip seeds, the mean route distance is 318 pixels for Common, 165 for Rare, 119 for Epic, and 57 for Legendary. Needs confirmation after play-tests. |
| 80 | How much does risk change which spot is picked (B4, B10)? | Each spot's risk weight goes from 1 to 2, linear in route distance within each pool, toward the rarity's risky side: nearer for Rare, Epic, and Legendary, farther for Common. It is multiplied by the hiding spot weight (1 to 3, open question 66). Any spot can still be picked, and the same trip seed gives the same spots. Needs confirmation after play-tests. |
| 81 | How is "fair to grab" checked (B10)? | A headless test with the real encounter rules and four legs. For every spot a treasure can hide in, it tries start times every 0.25 seconds over the longest patrol loop, swimming straight at base top speed from the nearest point outside lunge range, then back out. A spot passes when some start time gets in and out with no hit. One Tail Dash is allowed, only into a Legendary's pot. Today every start time passes. Grabbing with one leg is a play-test check. Needs confirmation. |
| 82 | Is the risk ladder a new rule or part of B6 (B6, B10)? | A new rule, B10, so B6 keeps its hiding places as they are. B6 points to B10. An area with no tilapia uses B6 alone. Needs confirmation. |
| 83 | Which green is Common (B2)? | #1fb98d, a teal-leaning green, in the Common badge and the Common glow ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 3e). Its relative luminance is 0.369, with 5.75:1 contrast with ink #2b2350 (at least 4.5:1, because badges are UI on a panel). It stays apart from the nearby greens and teal: 1.61:1 from the reed green #86e660, 1.98:1 from the GFP halo #7dff8f, 1.91:1 from the Mint Dream colour #a8f0cf, and 1.61:1 from the secondary button #55e4d8. Against the canal water's mid tone #1cb6cc it is only 1.03:1, so the glow never stands alone: the badge shape and name carry rarity. Greyscale pairs: Common and Rare 1.16:1 (was 1.80:1), Common and Epic 1.17:1 (was 2.44:1), Common and Legendary 1.54:1 (was 1.36:1), Rare and Epic 1.36:1, Rare and Legendary 1.33:1, and Epic and Legendary 1.80:1. Common and Rare, and Common and Epic, are now closer in greyscale than before; this is allowed because the badge shape and the rarity name carry rarity (X2). Legendary keeps gold #ffc12e (owner decision on issue #68, 2026-10-20). Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 84 | What does "revealed" mean in the canal (B8)? | A treasure drawn as a cue object, once a crumbled Dash pot shows it, and its pickup pop. A treasure under cover, drawn behind the reeds and under the haze, never glows, even while its hint sparkle shows, and a sealed Dash pot shows no glow. So the hint sparkle stays the only cue for a hidden treasure (B7; owner decision on issue #68, 2026-10-20). Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 85 | Does the Trip Bag show the glow (A16, B2)? | No change. The Trip Bag panel shows the bag icon, a count, and "not safe yet", with no treasure icons. The find toast, for each find going into the bag, and the banking card, for the bag's contents, carry the glow. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 86 | Where else does the rarity glow show (B2)? | On every owned or found treasure icon and Dream colour swatch: the find toast, the banking card, the tank arrival card, the first-find card's picture (C15), the Treasure Chest's found rows (C11), the decorate tray, and placed Decorations. Not on unfound silhouettes (open question 91), the restyle Dream colour tab's swatches, the Collection Book, perk icons, or Wearables worn on an axolotl. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 87 | Does the rarity glow move (B2, B8, X3)? | Only on trips, in every area (the canal and the pond): on a revealed treasure, which already bobs, its alpha breathes slowly from 0.6 to 1.0 of its peak, once every 2.4 seconds. It is static everywhere else, and static everywhere under reduced motion. It never pulses faster than once every 2.4 seconds. Its peak alpha is 0.35, about as faint as the GFP halo. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 88 | How big is the rarity glow (B2)? | A soft disc in the rarity's badge colour, fading out to its edge: 40 pixels across behind each 32 pixel icon box and behind the 28 pixel canal icon, 56 pixels in the 64 pixel decorate tray square, and 96 pixels behind each 80 pixel placed Decoration. Behind a 32 pixel box, it reaches 4 pixels past the box into the gaps that are already there, so no layout changes. It has to: the Dream colour swatch tiles fill their 32 pixel box, so a glow kept inside the box would not show round them. Each glow is drawn once, like the icons, and adds at most one picture per treasure. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 89 | Where do unfound treasures show (C11)? | Issue #69's recommendations: in the Treasure Chest, not a new screen. Every catalogue treasure is listed in catalogue order within its section, so a silhouette fills in where it stands. An unfound name shows as "???", with a hint by rarity based on B6 (open question 90), which now names the treasure's area first (issue #106; open question 188). An unfound Wearable shows its slot but not its perk. An unfound Dream colour shows a plain swatch silhouette with a "?" and keeps the label "Dream colour — not found in nature". Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 90 | What do the unfound hints say (C11)? | The treasure's area, then where to look by rarity (issue #106; open question 188). By rarity, based on B6, under the name, with the badge and rarity name on the name line: Common "Look near the main swim path!", Rare "Look off the main path!", Epic "Look in a cave or log, or past the current!" (was "Look in the cave or past the current!" before the pond's hollow log, issue #75; open question 150), and Legendary "Break a Dash pot with Tail Dash!". Paint shells use the same hint for their rarity. Issue #69's example puts the rarity first ("Rare: look off the main path!"), but the badge and the rarity name already show it. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 91 | Do unfound silhouettes glow (B2, C11)? | No. Unfound silhouettes never glow, so the glow marks only the treasures the player owns. Each is a flat silhouette of its own icon in the unfound-morph silhouette style: a pale fill, an ink outline, and an ink "?". Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 92 | How do the Treasure Chest section lines fit the smallest canvases (C11)? | The full line shows wherever it leaves room for at least one whole row. Where it does not, a short line shows instead, chosen by fit: Decorations "Place them in decorate mode.", Wearables "Wear them in Restyle for perks.", and Dream colours "Magic paints. Use in Restyle.". At 460 x 320 and 320 x 440 the chest shows one row per page, so all 72 treasures, the canal's, the pond's, Area 3's, Area 4's, and Area 5's, take 72 pages (issue #103; at 3 rows a page they take 9 Decoration, 11 Wearable, and 6 Dream colour pages); the tallest row is an unfound Dream colour, 137.5 pixels with the area in its hint (issue #106; it was about 115), so the "Treasure Chest" title hides there (open question 190). Paging works as before, and the chest has no new buttons. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 93 | When is a first-find card due (C15)? | When the player has banked at least one treasure of its kind and its card has not been seen, checked right after the tank arrival card's OK. So a save from 0.18.0 that already owns one sees the card once, after its next trip, and a card missed by closing the game shows after the next trip. The other option, showing it only on the trip that banks one, would never explain Dream colours to a player who already owns them. Cards never show mid-trip, or when the Home Tank opens from the title screen. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 94 | What does "once per save" mean for first-find cards (C15, D2, D7)? | The seen kinds are saved in the hub. "Start over" always erases the save, so the cards show again in the new game; issue #83's "not repeated after Start over unless the save is erased" is read this way. With device storage off, the seen cards last only for the page session. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 95 | In what order do several due first-find cards show (C15)? | One at a time: Decoration, then Wearable, then Paint shell, so "Try it now!" ends the run of cards. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 96 | What do the first-find cards say (C15)? | From the owner's drafted text. Decoration: the title "You found a Decoration!" and the Decorations section line as the body. Wearable: the title "You found a Wearable!" and the Wearables section line. Paint shell: the title "You found a Paint shell!" and the body "It unlocks a Dream colour: a magic paint for your axolotl. Try it in Restyle.". Smaller canvases use the short body "It unlocks a Dream colour. Try it in Restyle.", and the smallest also use the short title "New Paint shell!". Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 97 | Which buttons do the first-find cards have (C15)? | The Paint shell card: "Try it now!" (primary, with the play icon, focused first), which opens restyle on the Dream colour tab for the explorer, or the first owned axolotl, and "Maybe later" (with the close icon), which closes the card. The Decoration and Wearable cards: "OK" with the tick icon, like the tilapia fact card. Only existing button icons are used. Every button ignores presses for the first 0.8 seconds, like the tank arrival card's OK. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 98 | What picture does a first-find card show (C15)? | The icon of the first banked treasure of its kind, in a 32 pixel box beside the title, with its rarity glow (B2). Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 99 | Where does the Treasure Chest section line go (C11)? | Under the section heading, 4 pixels below it, in ink 18 pixel text, wrapping at the list width ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) section 8). It is part of the heading block, which is sized by the tallest heading and line across the sections, so the rows per page stay the same on every page. It shows on every page of its section, with or without finds. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 100 | Do the live axolotls stay within the frame-time budget (X11)? | Built: the tools and method in [ADR 0013](../adr/0013-axolotl-rig-and-frame-budget.md). The results are to be measured: the owner runs the four cases (the canal with the explorer and a rescue, the Home Tank with four axolotls, the title screen, and the dev test area, taller than one screen, with the explorer and a rescue while the camera scrolls both ways, via `?area=dev-test-area&perf` in a dev build), each on WebGL and on Canvas, on desktop Chrome with 6x CPU throttling and on the phone, and fills in ADR 0013's table. If a case misses, one shared cap is added in `src/logic/`, not one per scene. Awaiting the owner's measurements. |
| 101 | How does the owner reach the frame-time overlay on the phone? | Built: `?perf` works only under `npm run dev`, so on the phone it needs the dev server. Recommended to keep it dev-only. Open: may `?perf` also work on the GitHub Pages build, as an on-screen overlay that stores and sends nothing ([ADR 0007](../adr/0007-kid-safety-and-privacy.md))? `?compare` stays dev-only either way. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 102 | How does the tail move on the Canvas renderer, where a texture cannot bend? | Built: a strip of baked tail frames, 12 over one swim cycle at three tail amplitudes (normal, faster, and a full dash), 36 frames, keyed like the other parts. The nearest frame shows. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 103 | How does a rig axolotl blink? | Built: three baked eye frames, open, half and closed, on the existing blink timing. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 104 | How does a regrowing leg show on the rig? | Built: each leg state is its own baked leg. A regrowing leg re-bakes at each of the trip's 10 progress steps (`LEG_PROGRESS_STEPS`), never every frame. A lost or regrowing leg wears no sock or shoe (open question 73). Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 105 | How does the Iridescent sheen move on the rig (F15, F16, open question 50)? | Built: the body has 16 baked sheen steps, one every 0.25 seconds. The tail stripe is drawn live as a flat-colour vector overlay, so it is never bent with the tail, and no texture is repainted. Under reduced motion and on picture buttons the sheen stays still at x -10 ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 6p). Open: is a live vector overlay fine under "no live per-frame repaint"? Recommended yes, since it repaints and uploads no texture. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 106 | Are X11 and the frame-time Success Metrics bullet the right bar? | Recommended: yes, the budget in ADR 0013 (p95 frame time at most 33 ms under 6x CPU throttling and at most 16.7 ms on the owner's phone, and axolotl drawing at most 2 ms a frame), in its four cases (the fourth is the dev test area, taller than one screen, with the explorer and a rescue, via `?area=dev-test-area` in a dev build; to be measured by the owner). Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 107 | How do the gills move on the rig, and are the rig's approximations acceptable? | Built: each gill has 10 baked flutter frames plus a small live turn. The accepted approximations, each bounded by a test, are: on WebGL the Hearts, Tie-dye and Tie-dye galaxy tail patterns bend with the tail (worst 4.88, 12.38 and 2.37 units at a full dash), and the outline thins a little on the steepest tail bends. Recommended: accept, after the owner checks them by eye in `?compare`. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 108 | How does restyling show that "Surprise me" does nothing on the Colour, Boy or girl, Dream colour, and Outfit tabs (F6, F16)? | Built: it is hidden there, not shown as disabled, because the game has no disabled look. Its place is kept, so Cancel and Done never move between tabs. Tab does not reach it, and a stray "Surprise me" action does nothing. This holds on both Outfit views. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 109 | What does "Surprise me" do on the Boy or girl step (F1, F17)? | Built: it always switches to the other one, because there are only two. If the name is a suggested name that does not fit the new boy or girl, it becomes a random suggested name that fits. A suggested name that fits (a name for anyone) stays, and a typed name always stays. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 110 | What does the helper line say about "Surprise me" (F1, F6)? | Built: after the step's usual helper text, one sentence. Colour: "Surprise me picks a whole new axolotl.". Boy or girl: "Surprise me picks boy or girl.". Eyes: "Surprise me picks new eyes.". Mouth: "Surprise me picks a new mouth.". Gills: "Surprise me picks new gills.". Spots: "Surprise me picks new spots.". Tail: "Surprise me picks a new tail.". Body: "Surprise me picks a new body.". Age: "Surprise me picks a new age.". Size: "Surprise me picks a new size.". Name: "Surprise me picks a new name.". Restyling uses the same sentences on Eyes to Size and Name, and none on Colour, Boy or girl, Dream colour, and Outfit, where "Surprise me" is hidden (open question 108). The sentence shows on every step where "Surprise me" shows, and is never shortened or left out; the fit steps (F1) are unchanged at 460 x 320 and 320 x 440. On some mid-size canvases, 803 of 17045 cases measured, the longer helper line now hides (fit step 1) where it showed before: mostly portrait 320 to 430 wide by 568 to 932 tall, and landscape 568 to 1024 wide by 360 to 480 tall. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 111 | How are the unlocked areas worked out (N2)? | Built: from the saved stars, with nothing new saved. An area is beaten once its exit star (Star 1, H6) is saved. Area 1 is always unlocked, and area N+1 unlocks when area N is beaten, with no star gates. The other option, a saved list of unlocked area ids, could disagree with the stars. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 112 | Which area does the map start on, and what is saved (N2, D2)? | Built: the hub saves the last area chosen on the map (`lastAreaId`), Area 1 by default. A saved area that is unknown or locked loads as the newest unlocked area. The map's focus starts on the last area played, else on the newest unlocked area. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 113 | How does the map show a new area (N2)? | Built: a "New!" pill, text on a badge, on the marker of the newest unlocked area while that area has no stars. It goes once the area has any star. It is still under reduced motion. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 114 | Does the map show when there is only one area (N2, F7)? | Built: yes. The map always shows after Go!, even with only Area 1, so players meet it from the start. It shows only the areas in the area data, with no placeholders for areas still to come. The other option was to skip the map while only one area is unlocked. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 115 | What does a locked area do on the map (N2)? | Built: it can be focused and pressed. It shows its number and a padlock badge. Pressing it shows "Beat Area N to unlock!" for 3 seconds, where N is the area before it, and does not start a trip. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 116 | Where does the map sit in the Go exploring flow (F7, N2)? | Built: the map is its own screen, opened by Go! on the explorer card. Go! no longer starts the swim; picking an unlocked area on the map does. Back on the map returns to the explorer card, with the same explorer chosen and Go! focused. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 117 | What does each area remember between trips (A12, D2)? | Built: each area keeps its own trip memory, by area id: its eaten snacks, its last trip seed, its stranded axolotl left waiting, and its lost treasures. It lasts for this play session only and is never saved, as the canal's was before. Start over clears it. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 118 | With more than one kind of predator, how many may warn at once (A1, N3)? | Built: one. Only one predator warns or strikes at a time, across every predator in the area, whatever its species, as A1 already says for the tilapia. A predator that is held back keeps patrolling. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 119 | How are found fact cards saved (C14, D2)? | Built: the hub saves a list of the hazard and predator ids met (`metHazards`), in area-table order, under the additive rule with no save version bump ([ADR 0012](../adr/0012-save-on-device.md)). An older save whose `book.tilapiaCard` is true loads with the tilapia met. The save still writes `book.tilapiaCard`, true once the tilapia is met, so an older build still shows the card. The Collection Book lists the fact cards in area-table order, then "Boy or girl?". The refuge fact card ids (issue #74) join the same list, after the hazard and predator ids, with no new field and no save version bump; the Book lists them before "Boy or girl?" ([ADR 0017](../adr/0017-xochimilco-refuge-and-habitat-milestones.md)). Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 120 | How is risk measured for other hazards and predators (B6, B10)? | Built: every predator route and every hazard that costs a leg is a threat with a strike range S: a predator's lunge range, or a hazard's reach from its shape's edge. The bands are Common farther than S + 60 pixels, Rare more than S and at most S + 60, Epic more than S / 2 and at most S, and Legendary at most S / 2. For the tilapia (S = 140) that is exactly 200, 140, and 70 pixels, so the canal's spots and placements do not change. Every spot keeps 60 pixels from every predator turn. Spot weights use the shortest distance to any threat. Hazards that only slow are not threats, and an area with no threat uses B6 alone. It changes play only from area 2 on. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 121 | How is the middle of the play area measured in an area that scrolls up and down (X10)? | Built: on screen, from the water line or the screen top, whichever is lower, to the sea bed or the screen bottom, whichever is higher. The 30% to 70% rule applies inside that. The messages move again when that water moves by 8 CSS pixels or more, or 0.1 seconds after a smaller move, so they are not redrawn every frame. The canal never scrolls up or down, so it is unchanged. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 122 | How does the developer try an area that is not in the game? | Built: a dev-only `?area=<id>` flag, read only under `npm run dev`, like `?perf` (open question 101). `?area=dev-test-area` opens a 6 x 2 screen test area, numbered 99, that is never in the area list and is not in the `npm run build` output. A trip there earns no stars. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 123 | When threats have different strike ranges, which band does a spot get (B10)? | Built: the riskiest band any one threat puts it in. So a spot inside a tilapia's lunge range never counts as Rare just because a threat with a shorter strike range is nearer. When every threat has the same strike range, this is the band of the shortest distance. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 124 | Does the dev `?area` flag show the area map (N2)? | Built: no. In a dev build with `?area=<id>`, Go! skips the map and starts the trip in that area at once. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 125 | Does the tilapia move into the area data's predator routes (Implementation Decision 8)? | Built: not yet. The area data keeps its own column of tilapia routes (`tilapiaRoutes`) beside the predator routes (`predatorRoutes`), which name their species, so the canal's data and its golden test stay exactly as before. The tilapia still plays by the shared predator rules. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 126 | Does a hazard hit start the bump cooldown (N3)? | Built: no. A hazard hit costs a leg and starts protected time (A5), but leaves the bump cooldown as it was. A predator's hit still starts it. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 127 | When are the areas drawn? | Built: every area's background is baked when the game starts. In dev, the test area adds about 33 MB of background pictures; the `npm run build` output has the canal and the lily pad pond. The pond adds its scenery pieces and its hollow log, plastic worm, and green cheek pictures, about 13.8 MB by estimate at bake scale 2 (issue #75); the owner is to measure it. Baking each area only when it is played is still a follow-up, before many areas ship. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 128 | What colour is a girl's cheek heart, how big is it, and where does it sit (F2, F13)? | Built: pink #ff5c9a, stronger than the blush, so it contrasts against all eight morphs and the 12 Dream colours ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 3a and 6u). It is on the near cheek only, below and behind the eye, with a white-backed ink outline. Its measured size and place are in 6u and 6i. The owner chose a heart on the cheek, no new boy cue, and the heart on every Girl picture (owner decision on issue #87, 2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21). Awaiting owner confirmation of the colour, size, and place. |
| 129 | How is the Grumpy brow drawn (F2)? | Built: a short, gentle stroke above each eye, angled down toward the snout at under about 20 degrees, in ink 2.0 over a white under-stroke, like the eyelashes. Its length and angle are in the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md). It sits outside the blink squash, so a closing eye never drops it onto the lid. The owner chose the Grumpy shape: a lid sloping down toward the snout, a short brow angled down toward the snout, and only the lower half of the eye showing (owner decision on issue #87, 2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21). Awaiting owner confirmation of the length, angle, and ink. |
| 130 | What ink do worn Wearables use (F13)? | Built: ink 2.0, down from 2.8, on every worn item, so the outline does not swallow the fill ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 4b and 6i). The owner chose the thinner item ink everywhere (owner decision on issue #88, 2026-10-20). Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 131 | How big must a worn Wearable be in the swim view (F13)? | Decided: every Wearable, now and every one added later, shows at least 64 square CSS pixels (about 137 square local units) of visible fill, at every look and swim phase, for a Small axolotl at 320 x 440. Visible fill is the fill not covered by the item's own ink or by a later layer. Each item also meets its slot's minimum sizes. No item is excepted: when an item misses, its shape, zone, or layer changes, never the floor. Each item's numbers are in the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 6i. The owner found most items "so small they may as well not exist", and asked for a rule so items are right-sized from now on (owner decision on issue #88, 2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21). Built, with every current item past the floor. Whether to raise the floor is open question 133. |
| 132 | Which keep-clear zones, footprints, and layers change for the bigger Wearables (C10, F13)? | Built: the revised keep-clear zones, a new zone for a girl's cheek heart, and the new footprints, all listed in the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 6i. The eyes, the eyelashes, the gill roots, and the cheek heart stay clear, and Back and Head items may reach past the body outline. The mouth is no longer kept clear: it draws over the Shell necklace. Shirt and Tail items draw over the near gills. The Shell necklace is a collar: a thick cord with shells along the chin, inside the body outline. The Outfit tab slot crops are worked out from the new footprints. The owner allowed revising the zones where they made an item unreadable, and Back and Head items past the outline (owner decision on issue #88, 2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21). Awaiting owner confirmation of the collar and the layers. |
| 133 | In a full outfit, how much of each item must show, and should the swim-view floor go up (F13)? | Built: in a full outfit, every worn item keeps some visible fill, more than 0. Stripy socks under Flippers show only 0.50 square CSS pixels, because the Feet item covers the Socks item. Open: should a full outfit keep a share of the 64 square CSS pixel floor for each item? Should the floor itself be raised? Recommended: keep both as built until the owner checks them on the phone. Awaiting owner decision. |
| 134 | What shape are the axolotl choices card's buttons (F6)? | Built: secondary square tiles, the icon over the label, like the hub's Decorate, Treasure Chest, and Collection Book tiles. Restyle, Outfit, and Dream colour are one width, 171 x 64 with Dream colour shown and 105 x 64 without; Back is 65 x 64. They flow in centred rows, 12 pixels apart ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 8). Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 135 | Does Escape on the axolotl choices card wait for the 0.8 second press guard (F6)? | Built: no. Escape is Back at once, repeats included, because Back changes nothing. Only button presses, and Enter or Space, wait. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 136 | Which rarity can an area's special treasure be, and what happens to stars when it changes (H6, H7)? | Decided: always Common or Rare, never Epic or Legendary. The canal's special treasure is now the Toy boat (Rare), not the Castle tower (Epic). The Castle tower stays owned by everyone who banked it and is now an ordinary Epic that turns up by the normal chance. Stars already earned are kept: a player who earned Star 2 with the Castle tower keeps it, and a player who already banked the Toy boat earns Star 2 the next time they beat Area 1. From now on, Star 2 needs the Toy boat (owner decision on issue #93, 2026-10-21). |
| 137 | How does a trip pick its treasures (B4)? | Decided: each area has a set number of treasure slots per trip, 6 in the canal and 7 in the lily pad pond (issue #75). The trip seed picks each slot's rarity on its own: Common 65%, Rare 27%, Epic 6%, and Legendary 2%. A slot takes an unbanked treasure of its rarity not already on the trip. If none of that rarity is left, it takes the next more common rarity with one left, then only the next rarer one (one step only since issue #111; open question 62). A slot with nothing left stays empty. The special treasure and lost treasures are extras on top of the slots. The slot rarities and the treasure picks each draw from their own stream of the trip seed. Slot counts and chances are area data with these defaults. No save change (owner decision on issue #94, 2026-10-21). Tune after play-testing. |
| 138 | How often is there a stranded axolotl (C2)? | Decided: on 40% of trips, about 4 trips in 10 (owner decision on issue #111, 2026-10-22, which replaces the 30% of issue #94). It is the default for every area that sets no chance of its own, and an area planned at "30%" uses this default unless the owner sets a value for it. It comes from its own stream of the trip seed, so a trip seed with a stranded axolotl still picks the same morph, place, and boy or girl as before. The lifetime cap does not change: only morphs not yet owned, so at most 7 rescues (C6) (owner comment on issue #94, 2026-10-21). Tune after play-testing. |
| 139 | Does a stranded axolotl left waiting come back on the next trip (C5)? | Decided (owner, 2026-10-22): yes, always, on the next trip in that area, with no new roll, so "He's waiting for you!" keeps its promise. The 40% chance (open question 138) applies only when nobody is waiting. After a reload nobody is waiting (D2), so the next trip rolls. |
| 140 | When does a treasure slot count a rarity as having none left (B4)? | Decided (owner, 2026-10-22): when that rarity's spot pool has no free spot, as well as when no unbanked treasure of it is left. The canal has 7 Rare treasures, the Toy boat included, and 7 Rare spots, and `canal/spot-pad-2` is not there for every zone seed. The canal's spots do not change, and no hiding spots are added. |
| 141 | What do Up and Down do on a Floating or Hanging Decoration (C9)? | Built: nothing. These Decorations move only along their line, so Up and Down neither move them nor show the invalid cue (the dashed outline and the "no" icon). Left and Right step 16 units along the line, and a step past the tank sides shows the invalid cue, as for a Floor Decoration. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 142 | How wide can a Decoration be, and what follows its width (C9, B2)? | Built: a Decoration may set its own width in tank units; the standard is 80. The wide test Decoration is fixed at 144 units, about a third of the 448-unit glass, so its base x runs from 96 to 384. Its footprint (its width by 80 tall), overlap, suggested spots, focus rings, placement ghost and "no" icon, and rarity glow (1.2 x its width across by 96 tall, so 96 x 96 for the standard width, as in open question 88) all follow its width. Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 143 | Where do the Home Tank controls go, now that Floating and Hanging Decorations use the tank's top band (C7, C9, X5)? | Decided: they keep clear of the band from tank y 20 to 108 (the top band, y 24 to 104, plus 4 units for the bob and sway). They go in the wall room above the tank lid, else in the open water between that band and the floor Decorations. Full-size controls come first; only where full size does not fit do they get smaller, and never below X5 (48 x 48 targets, primary actions 64 tall). At 1280 x 720 the controls keep their full size and labels, but the hub moves down into the open water under the band: the name panel at y 228, the buttons at y 306.5, and the best-stars badge under them (owner decision on issue #95, 2026-10-22). |
| 144 | How do the compact Home Tank controls look (C7, X5)? | Built: Decorate, Treasure Chest, Collection Book, and Put away become 48 x 48 icon-only tiles, each a 32 pixel icon, and a focus caption names the focused tile: in the hub a white panel with its label in bold ink 18 pixels, and in decorate mode the decorate caption. Go exploring keeps its 225 x 64 body and its label, Done becomes label-sized at 110 x 64, and tray squares are 48 pixels, a 32 pixel icon over its 40 pixel glow. When the room under the controls is full, the caption may sit over the floor Decorations. At 414 x 440 and 430 x 440 the hub rows split across the wall and the open water. At 460 x 320, a tray of more than 4 Decorations wraps into rows over the floor band, still clear of the top band. Resolved by issue #75: the tray has no paging. With the pond, 8 Decorations shipped, and with Area 3, 14 ship (issue #98); a tray of 14 uses this same rule for trays of more than 4, with a reach-room fallback (C9; open questions 153 and 171). Awaiting owner confirmation. |
| 145 | How does the developer try Floating and Hanging Decorations before any ship? | Decided: a dev-only `?decor` flag, read only under `npm run dev`, like `?area` (open question 122). It adds three test Decorations to the Home Tank: Test lily pad (Floating, Common, 80 units wide), Test firefly jar (Hanging, Rare, 80 units wide), and Test string lights (Hanging, Epic, 144 units wide). They are owned for the page session only, never saved, never in the Treasure Chest, and absent from the `npm run build` output. Saves stay `{id, x, y}` with no save version change: a placement's kind comes from the catalogue by id, and only shipped Decorations are saved (owner decision on issue #95, 2026-10-22). |
| 146 | Which perk does each pond Wearable give (B3, F9)? | Built: one existing perk each, at the canal's value for its rarity (Tuning values): Lily flower crown Regrowth 10%, Pond-weed scarf Snack magnet 30, Dragonfly glasses Treasure sense 25%, Raincoat Speed 6%, Ladybug backpack Shell 1 hit (Epic, so Shell stays on Epic and Legendary only), Rainbow dragonfly wings Dash 25%, and Froggy boots Speed 10%. The owner's brief said six Wearables, but its table lists seven, and all seven are built. They come after the canal's treasures in the catalogue, and the canal's treasures and spots do not change. No Wearable has a set ID yet. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 147 | Is the plastic worm fact card's source right (C13)? | Built: the owner's title "Plastic is not food" and body, word for word, with the source NOAA Marine Debris Program, <https://marinedebris.noaa.gov/why-marine-debris-problem/ingestion>. The owner is to review the source before release (C13). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 148 | What colour is Lily green (B3, F15)? | Built: a solid, Rare Dream colour with the body colour #ddf274, shown with its name label like the other solid colours. It is the 13th Dream colour, held by the pond's one Paint shell, and is told apart in greyscale by its name label ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md)). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 149 | Does being queasy slow Tail Dash (A9, A10, N3)? | Built: no. Queasy scales only the top speed, to 60% for 3 seconds; the acceleration and every Tail Dash value stay the same. This is an exception to A9 for up to 3 seconds of lower headway. Plastic worms start at least 371.5 pixels from every current, so a queasy one-leg axolotl never loses headway in one. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 150 | What does the unfound Epic hint say now that the pond has a hollow log (C11)? | Built: "Look in a cave or log, or past the current!", one line for every area (was "Look in the cave or past the current!"). It names the log so the hint stays true in the pond. Needs owner confirmation of the wording. |
| 151 | How do the plastic worm fact card and its Collection Book page fit the smallest canvases (C14)? | Built: on a trip, the fact card title wraps at a tight-padding width (the canvas width less twice the margin and the tight padding, from 120 to 420 pixels; 404 pixels at 460 wide) and always shows. The body wraps at the body width first; issue #98 added a fallback to the tight-padding width, used only when the body does not fit, and today only by the heron card at 320 x 440 (open question 173). In the Collection Book, the "Collection Book" title hides only on a featured page whose entry does not fit under it, so the heading moves up and the list gains 47 pixels. Measured, it hides only on the found plastic worm page, at 320 x 440, 460 x 320, and 460 x 340, and on the found heron page, at 320 x 440, 340 x 440, 460 x 320, 460 x 340, 480 x 320, and 480 x 340 (issue #98). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 152 | How do the Outfit tab's choices fit with more Wearables per slot (F6)? | Built: a slot now has up to 5 choices (Back: None and four Wearables, with Area 3's Firefly cape; issue #98). A label block beside its picture (fit step 8) may wrap wider than 168 pixels, up to the band width less 60 pixels (204 pixels at 320 x 440), only when 168 does not fit; a label block under its picture keeps 168. At 460 x 320 and 320 x 440 the choices view shows 1 choice a page, with More wearables, where 460 x 320 showed both choices on one page before. With Area 4's Wearables (issue #76), the Head and Back slots each have None and five Wearables, so up to 6 choices, still 1 a page at 460 x 320 and 320 x 440. With Area 5's Wearables (issue #103), they have None and six, so up to 7 choices, still 1 a page; at 320 x 440 they take the new fit step 9 (open question 68). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 153 | Where does a decorate tray of all the shipped Decorations go (C7, C9)? | Built: there is no tray paging. It uses the rule for a tray of more than 4: centred rows that may reach over the floor band, never into the top keep-clear band. Rows that fit no usual room try the reach rooms (the wall down to a gap above the top band, then the open water over the floor Decorations), else stack from the top of the tallest room. With 8 (issue #75): at 460 x 320 they sit compact in two rows over the floor band; at 320 x 440 in three rows on the wall, with the caption in the water; and at 340 x 440 Done sits alone in the open water below the top band. With all 14 (issue #98): at 460 x 320, two rows of 7 over the floor band, then a row with Put away and Done, and the caption hidden; at 320 x 440, three rows on the wall, 5, 5, and 4 with Put away beside the last 4, Done in the open water, and the caption hidden; at 340 x 440 the caption shows (open question 171). This resolves the tray paging left open in open question 144. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 154 | Is it fine that the "Rainbow dragonfly wings" find toast waits at the smallest landscape canvases (X10)? | Built: at 460 x 320 and 460 x 340 with Go home showing, the "Rainbow dragonfly wings" toast has no place and waits, by the same rule as the "Tie-dye galaxy Paint shell" toast (open question 38). It does not happen in play: Go home shows only at the Home Spot, where the Trip Bag is banked and the toast cleared. The find toast never shrinks (X10). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 155 | Does the heron re-check at the end of its warning (N3)? | Superseded by #111: the wading heron keeps the shared end-of-warning re-check, with cover added: if the axolotl is out of reach, in the safe area, or in cover, the warning is cancelled into the 3 second cooldown; if it has only moved, the stab goes to the fixed spot and misses (N3; open question 210). Before #111: Built: yes, like every predator. If the axolotl has dived out of the heron's band, or reached the safe area, by the end of the 1.2 second warning, the warning is cancelled. If it has only swum sideways along the band, the beak still dips at the spot fixed at the warning start, and misses. So diving is the sure escape, and swimming sideways also works. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 156 | What happens when the heron's warn mark comes while another predator is warning or striking (A1, N3)? | Superseded by #111: there is no warn mark. A heron warns on sight; while another predator is warning or striking, it starts no warning and keeps wading, and it can warn once that predator is done (A1; open questions 118 and 209). Before #111: Built: that heron cycle is a pass, with no warning and no strike, and the next chance is the next warn mark, 8 seconds later. While the heron warns or dips, no tilapia starts a warning. Only one predator warns or strikes at a time (open question 118). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 157 | How long do a heron strike and a heron pass last (N3)? | Superseded by #111: there is no cycle and no pass. A stab, hit or miss, and a cancelled warning are each followed by the 3 second cooldown, standing still, then wading (open question 213). Before #111: Built: both last one 8 second cycle, and so does a cancelled warning, so the heron is shown on the same windows whatever happens and its rhythm is easy to learn. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 158 | Can the heron be met, or strike, while it is away in its gap (N3, C14)? | Superseded by #111: there is no gap. The herons are always shown, so a heron can be met at any time within 180 pixels of where it stands, outside the safe area, even with the axolotl in cover (open question 216). Before #111: Built: no. In its 4 second gap the heron is not shown, so it cannot be met (its fact card waits until it is shown within its 180 pixel meet range), and it cannot warn, strike, or bump. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 159 | Does the heron fact card have a picture in the Collection Book (C14)? | Built: no. The heron has no Book art, so its found card shows its text only, like the plastic worm card. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 160 | Does the heron show a "!" bubble when it warns, like the tilapia (A2, N3)? | Superseded by #111: still no "!" bubble, but the cue is now the heron's S-neck pose, not a darker shadow (open question 212). Before #111: Built: no. Its shadow darkens for the 1.2 second warning, and that darker shadow is the cue, read by its tone in greyscale and shown at once, without motion, under reduced motion. The tilapia keeps its "!" bubble. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 161 | What ids do the Area 3 treasures use (B3, X9)? | Built: "pond/<slug>", like Area 2's, for example "pond/wooden-duck-decoy", because both areas are in the lily pad pond habitat. Ids stay stable forever, so this cannot change after release without a save upgrade. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 162 | How wide are the Firefly string lights (B3, C9)? | Built: 144 tank units, like the dev Test string lights, about a third of the 448-unit glass, so their base x runs from 96 to 384 (open question 142). They are the first wide Decoration to ship. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 163 | Which snacks dart, and how often (H4)? | Built: 3 snacks in Area 3, picked by the area data, spread evenly through the snack list, with no seeded draw, so every trip has the same darters. Each darts at most once per trip. Area 1 and Area 2 have no darters. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 164 | Do the Area 3 plastic worms and stranding places stay out of every heron's reach (N2, N3)? | Built: yes. Every plastic worm and every stranding place is out of every heron's reach, more than 110 pixels from each heron's stretch (issue #111). A plastic worm spat out mid-trip may land in a heron's reach; worms only slow, so this is a recorded residual ([ADR 0019](../adr/0019-heron-wading-stalker.md)). The worms also keep the pond's rules (N3), and the stranding places stay 80 pixels from every route. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 165 | Are the new Area 3 tuning values right (A9, H4, N3)? | Built, provisional: the stream at 74 pixels per second, the snack drift (periods 7.8 and 6.24 seconds), the heron's values (Tuning values; the wading stalker's since issue #111), and the dart values (3 darters, a 60 pixel trigger, a 40 pixel hop at 120 pixels per second). To tune after play-testing. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 166 | Does the unfound Epic hint change for Area 3 (C11)? | Built: no. It stays "Look in a cave or log, or past the current!". The root arch counts as a cave (B6), so the hint stays true. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 167 | Which perk does each Area 3 Wearable give (B3, F9)? | Built: one existing perk each, at the canal's value for its rarity: Feather cap Speed 4%, Lily-pad socks Snack magnet 30, Water-lily shirt Regrowth 15%, Pearl tail charm Shell 1 hit (Epic, so Shell stays on Epic and Legendary only), and Firefly cape Treasure sense 50%. No Wearable has a set ID yet. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 168 | How does an area get its own look (X9, Implementation Decision 8)? | Built: the area data may name a look variant of its habitat's look (`AreaData.look`), today only `pond-dusk` for Area 3. Without one, an area uses its habitat's look. Each look carries properties (its sky and wash, the stream, the kind of cave, the pond props, and the fireflies), and the art reads those properties instead of checking look ids, so a new look needs no new checks. The area table puts Area 3 in the lily pad pond habitat (N2), so its dusk look is a variant, not a new habitat. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 169 | Are the heron's reach and its strike zone on the risk ladder the same rule (B10, N3)? | Superseded by #111: no longer exactly one rule. A heron warns from where it stands, within 110 pixels, so its strike zone, every point within S = 110 of its stretch, holds every point it can warn at from somewhere on its stretch, but at any moment it reaches only the points within 110 pixels of where it stands. Cover stops a warning but does not change the risk ladder (open questions 207 and 215). Before #111: Built: yes, one measure. A point is in the heron's reach exactly when its route distance to the heron's stretch is at most 60 pixels and it is outside the safe area, and the risk ladder uses the same distance, with S = 60. So a spot the ladder calls Epic or Legendary is always one the heron can strike, and a Common or Rare spot never is. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 170 | Does the heron ever bump the axolotl (A4, N3)? | Built: no. The heron species is marked as never touching, so it never bumps, whatever its touch reach. Only its stab can reach the axolotl, as one hit (issue #111; open question 211). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 171 | Is it fine that the decorate caption hides with all 14 Decorations in the tray at the smallest canvases (C9)? | Built: yes, by the caption's existing rule: it shows only where it has room under the controls. With all 14 in the tray, it has no room at 460 x 320 or 320 x 440, so it hides there; at 340 x 440 it shows. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 172 | Where does the stream art start when the current is not at the area's end (N2)? | Built: the stream mouth is at the right edge of the screen column that holds the current's right end, so the art sits just upstream of the current. In Area 3 that edge is in open water, beside the root arch, so the band and its flow marks fade out over the last 40 pixels before the mouth, and the mouth stones that spill past the edge are drawn on the next screen. The Area 3 stream reads as flowing from under the bank roots. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 173 | May a trip fact card's body wrap wider when it does not fit (C14)? | Built: yes. The body first wraps at the body width; only when that does not fit the canvas height does it wrap at the title's tight-padding width, which takes fewer lines on canvases under 484 pixels wide. Today only the heron card at 320 x 440 uses it: 9 lines at 240 pixels, but 8 at 264, using 397 of 408 pixels of height. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 174 | How many treasures can one tank arrival or banking card name (A12, B9)? | Built: the card fit test bounds one trip by `MAX_TRIP_FINDS`, the largest area catalogue, 24 (the canal's), because a trip finds only its own area's treasures. Wording that names up to the whole catalogue, 60 treasures with Area 4's (issue #76) and 72 with Area 5's (issue #103), is still checked for clipping on every supported canvas. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 175 | What colour is Firefly gold (B3, F15)? | Built: a solid, Rare Dream colour with the body colour #ffd52a, shown with its name label like the other solid colours. It is the 14th Dream colour, held by Area 3's one Paint shell. It is told apart from every other Dream colour in greyscale and in colour, but it is close to golds that are not Dream colours, the Golden albino morph's #ffd36b and the Legendary badge gold, so the owner is to check it by eye ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md)). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 176 | How does the heron's shadow appear and leave (N3, X3)? | Superseded by #111: the shadow is gone. The herons stand in the water and are always shown, and under reduced motion each pose change is a switch with no tween (open question 212). Before #111: Built: under full motion it fades in over the first 0.4 seconds of its glide-in and out over the last 0.4 seconds of its glide-off, so it never pops in or out at the ends of its route; it darkens for the warning with a 0.4 second crossfade. Under reduced motion it fades in where it is over the last 0.4 seconds of the glide-in and out over 0.4 seconds where it was, and darkens at once. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 177 | What does the Water-lily shirt look like (B3, F13)? | Built: a pale water-blue shirt on the Tank top's silhouette, with one hem band across the torso and a small lily blossom on the chest. The Rare step of the Wearable look ladder allows one trim band; the blossom is a second detail, so whether a Rare shirt may carry both is the owner's ruling ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md)). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 178 | Where does the best-stars badge go when the hub reaches over the floor Decorations (C7)? | Decided: option A. When the hub reaches, the badge always uses its stacked layout, the area label above the stars (about 104 x 78.5, with the same 18 pixel text and 24 pixel stars), in the bottom-right corner, never centred. Centred, it does not fit at 480 x 375 and 480 x 414. The focus caption stays 17 pixels clear of it at 460 wide and 27 pixels at 480 wide (owner ruling on issue #74, 2026-10-22). |
| 179 | What is the habitat milestone card's title (N2)? | Built, provisional: "All clean!". It hides where it does not fit, at 460 to 1024 wide by 320, 340, and 360 tall; the picture, the body, and "Keep exploring" always show. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 180 | When does a habitat milestone card show (N2, C15)? | Built, provisional: on the Home Tank return after the trip, after the tank arrival card and its first-find cards, and before the hub takes input, one card per newly clean habitat, in habitat order. After the Paint shell card's "Try it now!", the milestone cards show first, then restyle opens. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 181 | Which axolotl does "See it again" name (N2)? | Built, provisional: the current explorer, because the save does not keep which axolotl cleaned up each habitat. The card shown after the trip names the trip's explorer. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 182 | What are the refuge fact cards' unfound hints (C14)? | Built, provisional: "Visit the refuge to find this card." for "Axolotl refuges", and "Beat an area, then visit the refuge to find this card." for "Canal clean-ups". The habitat hint that the area cards use does not fit, because neither card belongs to an area. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 183 | Do the refuge fact cards have a picture in the Collection Book (C14)? | Built, provisional: no. Neither has Book art, so each found card shows its text only, like the plastic worm and heron cards. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 184 | Where do the refuge's Previous and Next tiles go (N2)? | Built, provisional: labelled tiles, an arrow over the label, Previous 118 x 64 and Next 65 x 64, at the top right, beside Back, when the canvas is at least 439 pixels wide (room for Back, Previous and Next 12 pixels apart inside the margins); else in a centred bottom row. At 460 x 320 they sit at the top and two whole sections still show; at 320 x 440 they sit in the bottom row. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 185 | How long does the refuge clean-up take (N2, X3)? | Built, provisional: a 0.6 second hold after the refuge opens; the cleared trash floats up 24 pixels and fades with a sparkle over 0.9 seconds; then a newly clean section crossfades to its clean picture as its plants grow, over 0.9 seconds. A pan glides for 0.3 seconds. Under reduced motion each step is a 0.2 second fade, with no float, sparkle, or growth, and a pan is at once. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 186 | Is it fine that five hub actions make the hub compact on more canvases (C7)? | Built, provisional: yes, by the existing rule that full size shows only where it fits. The hub is now compact at 320, 340, 459, 460, and 520 x 568, 320, 340, and 520 x 600, 480 x 568, and 844 x 320 to 480. The "Home Tank" name panel now shows at 320, 340, 459, 460, and 520 x 568 and 320, 340, and 520 x 600, and now hides at 320, 340, and 520 x 667, 459, 460, and 480 x 600, and 844 x 568, 600, 667, and 844. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 187 | Are the refuge fact cards true (C13, C14)? | Built, provisional: the bodies and sources in C14. The owner is to read the sources and check both cards before release. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 188 | What does an unfound treasure's area hint say (C11)? | Built, provisional: the area label and the habitat name, as in "Area N, Habitat: ", and then the rarity hint with its first letter lower-cased, such as "Area 2, Lily pad pond: look off the main path!". The rarity hints keep their text, so the Epic hint keeps "or log": "Area 3, Lily pad pond: look in a cave or log, or past the current!". Issue #106's example, "Area 2, Lily pad pond: look in a cave or past the current!", leaves "or log" out. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 189 | Which area does a Dream colour's hint name, and a treasure in more than one area (C11)? | Built, provisional: a Dream colour names the area of its Paint shell. A treasure names the first area, in area-table order, whose layout holds it, found from the area data and never from its id. Today every treasure and every Paint shell is in exactly one area. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 190 | Where does the "Treasure Chest" title hide (C11)? | Built, provisional: only where even the short section lines leave no room for one whole row, as the Collection Book's title does (C14). The heading moves up to the title's place and the list gains 47 pixels. Measured, it hides only at 320 x 440, 340 x 440, 360 x 440, and 460 x 320. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 191 | Are the Area 4 layout and tuning values right (N2, H1, H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #76): 6 x 2 screens; the Home Spot at (120, 200), with its safe area its column, x 40 to 300, from the surface to the bed; the exit at (2780, 625), radius 40; 2 tilapia; one current, x 2140 to 2340 and y 380 to 600, flowing left at 72 pixels per second, so one leg makes 18 pixels per second of headway; 28 snacks (16 worms and 12 bugs, 4 in the current), drifting with periods of 7.6 and 6.08 seconds, about 15.6 pixels per second at most; 3 darters by the shared dart rule; 9 treasure slots (they often stay empty under the one-step fallback; open question 220); 6 stranding places; and 5 ghost nets. The owner set the size, the counts, and the slots; the rest are derived. To tune after play-testing. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 192 | Are Area 4's tilapia values right, and how are they carried (N2, N3, B10)? | Built, provisional: the owner's values (a 0.85 second warning, strike speed 400, reach 145, a 2.2 second cooldown, and patrol speed 50), with the 0.8 second floor, the 0.4 second strike, and every other value kept. The tuning is optional area layout data; an area without it plays and bands exactly as before. With it, the area's tilapia routes are banded by its own strike range, S = 145 (Common over 205, Rare over 145, Epic over 72.5, Legendary within 72.5), and its fairness check plays the same tuning. Whether the step feels a little quicker but still fair is a play-test check. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 193 | Is the ghost net's snag right (N3)? | Built, provisional: 50% top speed for 2 seconds, multiplied with the leg factor, with the acceleration and Tail Dash unchanged; a snag only on entering a net's reach, never each step inside, never stacked, and one entry for overlapping nets; a meet range of 100 pixels from the net's edge, like the plastic worm's; the message "Snagged on an old net! Wriggle free." in the snack banner's place; a wriggle of 0.1 radians, one cycle every 0.4 seconds, under full motion only; and a small snag cue over the head in both motion modes. The owner set the slowdown, the time, no stacking, and the reduced-motion rule; the rest are derived. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 194 | How near must the axolotl come to snag on a net (N3)? | Built, provisional: 18 pixels from the net's rectangle edge to the body centre, the edge included. It is the half height of a Medium Regular body with its ink outline, (19 + 3.5) x 0.95 x 0.85, about 18.2, rounded, so a snag starts when the inked top or bottom of a Medium body reaches the net, and a head or tail swimming in end-on pokes into the loose mesh first. It is the same at every size and age, because size is for looks only (F4), as the tilapia's reach matches Medium. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 195 | How far from a current must a ghost net be (A9, N3)? | Built, provisional: at least 190.5 pixels. That is the farthest a snagged axolotl at the speed perk cap swims before its snag ends, 2 seconds x 150 x 1.15 x 0.5 = 172.5 pixels, plus the 18 pixel net reach, worked out the way the plastic worm's 371.5 pixels was. A snagged one-leg axolotl swims 45 pixels per second, under the 72 pixel per second current, so a snag inside the current could not be beaten; this clearance means it never happens. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 196 | How is the level layout ladder read without walls (N2)? | Built, provisional: the obstacles are the ghost nets (kept out of by their reach) and the tilapia routes (by their strike range); the branching routes are the top-row and bottom-row ways; the dead ends are the root tangle in the bottom left and the bank in the top right, both holding treasure; a gap is the clear width between two obstacles' keep-outs, along the shortest line between them, measured in a test (`src/logic/chinampaLayout.test.ts`). The safe passing width is one body height, twice the net reach, 36 pixels, so the axolotl may stray half a body either way off the middle of a gap and still pass clear. Area 4's narrowest main-path gap is 64 pixels, against about 136.09 in Area 3 (between its first two plastic worms; issue #111 raised the heron's reach to 110 pixels, so the heron no longer bounds Area 3's narrowest gap), and no gap beside a net is under 36. If a later layout needs solid terrain, that is a framework change, in its own PR with an ADR (N2, the area shape rule). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 197 | How are Area 4's under-bank spots made (B5)? | Built, provisional: a new cover, under the bank, from new optional area data, one overhang rectangle under each top-row garden plot bank, from the surface down to y 140 and inset 20 pixels from its screen's edges; there is none over the gap current's column. It is never built from the bank art's columns, so the canal's and the pond's covers do not change. It is shallow cover, for Common and Rare spots only. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 198 | How does the decorate tray fit 20 Decorations (C9)? | Built, provisional: tray paging (owner decision on issue #76, 2026-10-23, <https://github.com/brenpike/glam-gills/issues/76#issuecomment-6355162967>). Under the tray rule alone, the tray fit every supported canvas only up to 17 Decorations. Now a tray that fits neither the usual rooms nor the reach rooms goes in pages of at most two rows of squares (one where two do not fit), as many a row as fit across, with Back and Next on the row of Put away and Done, wrapping past either end. Back and Next are the Treasure Chest's 78 x 64 tiles, or 48 x 48 icon-only tiles named by the caption when compact. They show only with more than one page, and a tray that fits on one page keeps its layout exactly. Every target stays at least 48 x 48, nothing goes into the top band, and it keeps working as more Decorations ship. With 20 shipped, the tray pages only for 18 to 20 at 360 x 440 and 375 x 440, and for 19 or 20 at 460 and 480 wide by 320, 340, 360, and 375 tall and at 480 x 414. At 460 x 320: 14 a page (two rows of 7), so 20 take pages of 14 and 6; Back {85, 236, 48, 48}, Next {145, 236, 48, 48}, Put away {205, 236, 48, 48}, Done {265, 228, 110, 64}; no caption. At 360 x 440: 10 a page (two rows of 5 on the wall, y 16 and 76); Back {35, 271, 48, 48}, Next {95, 271, 48, 48}, Put away {155, 271, 48, 48}, Done {215, 263, 110, 64} in the open water; the caption at y 339. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 199 | Is the ghost net fact card true (C13)? | Built, provisional: the owner's title and body, word for word, with the two NOAA Marine Debris Program sources in C13. The sources support lost nets trapping animals and people removing them from lakes and waterways, but neither names canals. The owner is to read the sources and check the word "canals" before release. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 200 | How wide are the Papel picado banner and the Willow swing (B3, C9)? | Built, provisional: the Papel picado banner is 144 tank units wide, like the Firefly string lights, so its base x runs from 96 to 384 (open question 142); the Willow swing has the standard width, 80. Both are Hanging. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 201 | Which perk does each Area 4 Wearable give (B3, F9)? | Built, provisional: one existing perk each, at the canal's value for its rarity: Straw sun hat Treasure sense 15%, Marigold necklace Regrowth 10%, Garden overalls Snack magnet 40, Monarch hair clip Dash 20%, and Monarch wings Speed 10%. None is a Shell perk. No Wearable has a set ID yet. The owner asked to check these in the PR. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 202 | What colour is Poppy red (B3, F15)? | Built, provisional: a solid, Rare Dream colour with the body colour #e8402f, shown with its name label like the other solid colours. It is the 15th Dream colour, held by Area 4's one Paint shell. Its body is 3.57:1 with ink and 4.03:1 with white. It is told apart from every other Dream colour in greyscale and in colour; its closest is Rainbow, which also carries its bands ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md)). The owner is to check it by eye. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 203 | Where are the chinampa edges of the gap current drawn (N2)? | Built, provisional: one chinampa edge just above the current's rectangle and one just below it, 2 pixels off it, centred on it and flared past its ends, with their roots streaming the way the water flows. They are placed from the current's rectangle only, so they are art, not obstacles. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 204 | Where does the Home Spot's burrow stand when the Home Spot is in the top row of a tall area (A13, N2)? | Built, provisional: in a one-row area, and in the bottom row of a tall one, the burrow stands on the bed as before. A tall area's top row has no bed, so there the burrow stands at the Home Spot itself, its arch centred on it and ringed by the safe-ring dots, so the burrow and its flag show where the explorer banks. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 205 | Is the boot bake memory still fine with Area 4 (open question 127)? | Built, provisional: every area's scenery is still baked when the game starts. By estimate, a 6 x 2 area bakes about twice Area 3's 15.8 MiB of scenery at bake scale 2, plus the chinampa props. Baking each area only when it is played is still a follow-up ([ADR 0018](../adr/0018-chinampa-gardens-ghost-nets-and-predator-ladder.md)). The owner is to measure boot memory and frame time in Area 4 on the phone. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 206 | Does the chinampa look read (N2)? | Built, provisional: the look, the root tangle, the gap edges, the ghost net mesh, and the area map's garden plot marker in the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md). The owner is to check them by eye, in colour and in greyscale, and that the garden plots read as distinct from the canal's willow banks. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 207 | What is cover from a heron, and what does it change (B10, N3)? | Decided (owner decision on issue #111, 2026-10-23): "The bottom band and hiding spots are safe" means a heron hunts by sight and cannot see an axolotl in cover: inside a reed patch, under a lily pad, or in the root arch. Past the current is open water, not cover. A heron does not warn at an axolotl in cover, a warning is cancelled if its target goes into cover, and a stab never connects with an axolotl in cover. The tilapia ignores cover, as before. Treasure spots may still sit within a heron's reach (B10). Built: a reed patch is cover over its whole rectangle, from the surface to the sea bed top, not only where reeds are drawn; under a lily pad is within the pad's half-width and from the surface down to 30 pixels below it, for every lily pad, the pads for scenery included; and the root arch is its whole cave box. Cover does not stop a meeting (open question 216). The risk ladder still measures distance only, so a spot in cover still counts as near a heron, because getting in and out of it crosses the heron's reach (open question 215). This replaces "No cover shields the axolotl: only depth does" (issue #98). The other reading, that no treasure spot may sit in a heron's reach, is not used. Needs owner confirmation of the built details. |
| 208 | How far does a heron reach (N3)? | Built, provisional: a heron's stretch is the line where its beak rests, at y 120. It reaches 110 pixels from where it stands on that line, so the deepest point it can warn at is y 230, the owner's "about y 230", and the bottom band below that, down to the sea bed top at y 318, gets no warning. A stab hits within 26 pixels of its tip, so the lowest axolotl centre a stab can hit is about y 256. The art draws each heron standing with its head above the water and its beak tip resting near the water line over its point, and draws every stab from the drawn beak to the spot the rules use ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 7h). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 209 | When does a heron warn (A1, A2, N3)? | Built, provisional: on sight, like the tilapia: when the axolotl is within 110 pixels of where the heron stands, outside the safe area, not in cover, and no other predator is warning or striking (A1). The warning lasts its 1.2 second wind-up, above the 0.8 second floor (A2), which does not change. The heron stands still while it warns; only its facing follows the axolotl. This replaces the timer-started warning of issue #98. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 210 | Where does a heron's stab go, and what does the end-of-warning check do (N3)? | Built, provisional: to the spot where the axolotl was when the warning started. This is a shared rule option, the fixed strike spot, used by the heron and not by the tilapia, which still locks its aim at the strike start, so the tilapia's play does not change. The end-of-warning check is the shared one: if the axolotl is out of reach, in the safe area, or in cover, or the species' rule says no, the warning is cancelled and the 3 second cooldown runs. If the axolotl has only moved, the stab still goes to the fixed spot and misses. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 211 | How does a heron's stab move and hit (A4, N3)? | Built, provisional: the heron stays where it stands through the warning, the stab, and the cooldown. The stab's tip moves in a straight line from the heron to the fixed spot over 0.3 seconds. It connects when the tip is within 26 pixels of the axolotl, the tilapia's Medium hit reach, outside the safe area and not in cover, checked on every capped game step. The heron's strike speed is its reach over its stab time, about 367 pixels per second, recorded as the stab's top speed; no heron rule reads it. A stab that connects is one hit (A4, A5, A11, A22), with the usual bump cooldown, never a bump. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 212 | What is a heron's warning cue (A2, X2, X3, N3)? | Built, provisional: the S-neck pose. The heron freezes and draws its neck back into an S, a clearly different silhouette, which carries the cue in greyscale. There is no "!" bubble and no glint; A2 allows a pose change, and open question 160 was the precedent. The stab is the neck straight toward the spot, with the beak closed. Under reduced motion every change is a pose switch with no tween, and the heron still wades, but its legs do not step ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 7h). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 213 | What does a heron do between strikes (N3)? | Built, provisional: after a stab, hit or miss, or a cancelled warning, it stands still for its 3 second cooldown, with its beak back at rest and its facing following the axolotl, then wades on. Standing still through the cooldown is a shared rule option the tilapia does not use. It wades back and forth along a two-point stretch 180 pixels long, at 20 pixels per second (the tilapia patrols at 45), so a wade there and back takes 18 seconds. These values are provisional. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 214 | Where do the two herons stand in Area 3 (N2, B10)? | Built, provisional: both at the far end, at y 120: `heron-0` from x 2190 to 2370, over the spots past the stream, and `heron-1` from x 2440 to 2620, over the root arch. `heron-0` and `heron-1` are stable route ids ([ADR 0019](../adr/0019-heron-wading-stalker.md)).
