Every Epic and Legendary spot there stays near a heron (B10), the deep lane stays open, and no tilapia route moves. To keep every spot 60 pixels from every heron waypoint and in its band, the spots past the stream and in the root arch are re-laid with the same ids: past the stream, Legendary pots at (2252, 120) and (2308, 120) and Epic spots at (2260, 190) and (2330, 196); in the root arch, Legendary pots at (2500, 112) and (2556, 112) and Epic spots at (2470, 196) and (2540, 196). The root arch has 4 spots, down from 6: `pond-2/spot-cave-3` is now Epic (it was Legendary), and `pond-2/spot-cave-5` and `pond-2/spot-cave-6` are gone. The main swim path still runs at y 200, so under the stretches it is inside the herons' reach. The stranding places, plastic worms, pads, reed columns, tilapia routes, exit, and cave box do not change. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 215 | What is a heron's strike range on the risk ladder (B10)? | Built, provisional: S = 110, its reach, through the shared bands, measured as route distance from its stretch, as for the tilapia: Common farther than 170 pixels, Rare more than 110 and at most 170, Epic more than 55 and at most 110, and Legendary at most 55. A heron warns from where it stands, so its strike zone holds every point it can warn at from somewhere on its stretch, rather than being exactly the same rule; this replaces open question 169. The ladder measures distance only, so a spot in cover still counts as near a heron (open question 207). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 216 | When is a heron met (C14, N3)? | Built, provisional: with no gap, the herons are always shown. A heron is met the first time the axolotl comes within 180 pixels of where it stands, its meet range, unchanged, outside the safe area, wherever it has waded to. Cover does not stop a meeting. This replaces open question 158. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 217 | What happens to the timer-started warning and the heron's shadow cycle (N2, N3, Implementation Decision 8)? | Built, provisional: both are removed, with the heron's cycle tuning, because no species uses a timer any more (#108 drops Area 10's 6 second cycle too). The `heron-shadow-cycle` timing element type is retired ([ADR 0019](../adr/0019-heron-wading-stalker.md)). The generic timing element data stays for later areas: the `cycle` element kind, element spawns, an area's elements, and the new things counted per area. Area 3 adds no timing element, so its N2 area table cell is "None", and the one-new-thing rule still holds. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 218 | When does a heron's head follow the axolotl (N3)? | Built, provisional: only while the axolotl is within 180 pixels of the heron, its meet range, and not in cover. Otherwise the heron looks along its wading direction, so an axolotl hiding in cover is not watched ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 7h). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 219 | How does the Area 3 fairness check stay non-empty for a pot in cover (B10)? | Built, provisional: the check that an axolotl waiting at each Legendary pot is hit waits at the pot itself when the pot is in open water. For a pot in cover, such as one in the root arch, where a heron cannot see the axolotl, it waits instead at the last uncovered point on the straight way in from the pot's safe start that a heron can reach. The test's name says so (`src/logic/riskFairness.test.ts`). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 220 | Do Area 4's treasure slots often stay empty under the one-step fallback (B4, N2)? | Open (issues #111 and #76): in a measured 200 fresh trips, about 136 left 1 or 2 of Area 4's 9 slots empty (84 left one, 52 left two). Area 4 has 4 Common and 4 Rare treasures, and the Scarecrow, a Rare, is always placed as the special treasure, so only 7 Common and Rare treasures are left to fill the slots; a slot that rolled Common or Rare with none left takes only the one-step fallback of B4, and stays empty if that finds nothing. The owner checks in play whether that feels fine, or whether Area 4 needs more Common and Rare treasures or fewer slots. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 221 | What are Area 5's stable ids (N2, B3, D2)? | Built, provisional (issue #103, named default ND-1; [ADR 0020](../adr/0020-u-turn-areas-garter-snake-and-hyacinth-mats.md)): the area `chinampa-2`, in `src/logic/chinampa2Layout.ts`, with `CHINAMPA_2_AREA`, `CHINAMPA_2_LAYOUT`, and `CHINAMPA_2_TILAPIA`; the treasures, in `CHINAMPA_2_TREASURES` after `CHINAMPA_TREASURES`, `chinampa/watering-can`, `chinampa/floating-gourd`, `chinampa/bee-headband`, `chinampa/bandana`, `chinampa/pinata` (named "Piñata"), `chinampa/sunflower-sunglasses`, `chinampa/embroidered-shirt`, `chinampa/paint-shell-plum`, `chinampa/talavera-fountain`, `chinampa/hummingbird-tail-ribbon`, `chinampa/alebrije-statue`, and `chinampa/rainbow-serape-cape`, keeping the habitat prefix as Area 3 kept `pond/`; the Dream colour `plum`; the predator species and fact card `garter-snake`, on routes `garter-snake-0` and `garter-snake-1`; the element type and fact card `water-hyacinth`, with spawns `chinampa-2/hyacinth-mats-1` and `chinampa-2/hyacinth-mats-2`; the nets `chinampa-2/ghost-net-1` to `chinampa-2/ghost-net-5`; and the spots, the cave, and the stranding places `chinampa-2/...`. One shared pure function decides the side, for both the hiding rules and the past-current marker. | 226 | How do the water hyacinth mats look and gate the way in the side view (N2, N3)? | Decided (owner decision, 2026-10-23: the mats are kept as built; issue #103, named default ND-6; kept exactly as built by issue #118, named default ND-7, open question 309): Area 4 draws the gap between two chinampas with its edges above and below the current, and the channel between two mats uses the same mapping. Each pair is one mat above and one mat below a narrow horizontal channel, and the two drift together along y to close it. A pair spans its row's full height at its x, so a closed pair gates that row, and the way round is through the other row. Pair 1 gates the top row at x 1500 to 1650, its channel at y 220; pair 2 gates the bottom row at x 580 to 730, its channel at y 500. The way round pair 1, through the bottom row, passes `chinampa-2/ghost-net-4` under it, and the way round pair 2, through the top row, passes `chinampa-2/ghost-net-1` over it, so waiting for the channel is the skilful choice and going round the risky one. The two pairs never gate both rows at the same x, and a way to the exit is always open. The other reading, not chosen: both mats float at the surface and drift together along x. |
| 227 | How do the mats time their cycle (N3)? | Built, provisional (issue #103, named default ND-7): an 8 second cycle, open for 3 seconds, the first 1 second of which the mats drift apart, mirroring the 1 second of closing, then closing for 1 second (the warning), then closed for 4 seconds. Pair 1 starts its cycle at 0 seconds and pair 2 at 4 seconds, so when one is open the other is closed. The channel counts as open once its clear height is at least 36 pixels, the safe passing width; fully open, it is 72 pixels tall, two safe passing widths. A channel is short enough that a one-leg axolotl that enters as it opens is through before closing starts: at most 189 pixels long, 150 x 0.6 x (3 − 0.5) − 2 x 18, and a longer one is refused; Area 5's channels are 150 pixels. The tuning is `HYACINTH_MATS` in `src/logic/tuning.ts`. The element type keeps its kind, a cycle of period 8, and gains only optional fields, so the timing rule and existing data do not change. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 228 | How do the mats block, and what must stay clear of them (N3)? | Built, provisional (issue #103, named default ND-8): each mat is solid at all times, at its position for the current time, and the explorer's body centre stays out of each mat grown by 18 pixels, the net reach, the body's half height. A mat that moves onto the explorer pushes it out along the channel to the channel's nearer end, or to the other end when the nearer one is in a net's reach, a current, another mat, or out of the swim bounds. Tail Dash and a bump do not pass through a mat. Only the explorer is blocked: the following rescued axolotl, the predators, the snacks, and the carry home are not. So every predator route and its reach, every snack start, every darted snack landing (an optional mats list in the dart rule, absent in Areas 3 and 4), every hiding spot, stranding place, and net, and the exit stay clear of each mat pair's whole sweep, and nothing a player needs is ever inside a mat. Needs owner confirmation. |

| 247 | How is a garter snake kept from warning deeper and deeper after each dart (N3)? | Decided (owner decision on issue #103, 2026-10-23, option 1, <https://github.com/brenpike/glam-gills/issues/103#issuecomment-6371850978>): a new shared rule option, warn on route (`warnOnRoute`). Under the plain shared rules a snake could warn again from wherever a dart left it, and a probe walked a snake down to y 599. With the option, a predator warns only while it is within 2 pixels of its route as a closed loop, inclusive; off its route it does not warn and cancels a warning at its end, and it swims its route through the cooldown and patrol, warning again once back on it. The garter snake opts in; the tilapia and the heron do not, so they play as before. The dart itself is unchanged and has no depth cap. The bounds: warnings at y 212 or less, hits at y 254 or less, the head at y 228 or less, and y 260 and below safe. Measured on Area 5's real routes over 60 seconds: the deepest head at y 227.96, the deepest warning target at y 206.96, and no events in the deep lane. Options 2 (a dart depth cap) and 3 (both) were not chosen. |
| 248 | What are the danger pass's stable ids and names (N2, N3, H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-1; [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md)). Named defaults ND-1 to ND-31 were posted on issue #117 before building as provisional build choices the owner can override (<https://github.com/brenpike/glam-gills/issues/117#issuecomment-6377797982>; no owner reply yet). The chase: `ChaseTuning` {sight, leash, speed} and an optional `chase` on the predator tuning in `src/logic/tuning.ts`, the defaults `TILAPIA_CHASE`, `TILAPIA_RAMP_CHASE`, `GARTER_SNAKE_CHASE`, and `CARP_CHASE`, and `tunedSpecies(species, tuning)` in `src/logic/predators.ts`. The carp: species and fact card id `carp`, named "Common carp"; `CARP_SPECIES`, after the garter snake in the species list; `CARP` in `tuning.ts`; `CARP_FACT` and `CARP_MEET_RANGE` in `src/logic/factCards.ts`; and phase 2 route ids `carp-<n>`. Moving hazards: `src/logic/movingHazards.ts`, with `MovingHazardType`, `MovingHazardSpawn`, `movingHazardSpawn()`, and `PUNTING_POLE`, whose type, look, and fact card id is `punting-pole`, named "Punting pole"; its tuning `PUNTING_POLES` (`PuntingPoleTuning`) in `tuning.ts`; the area data field `movingHazards`; and spawn ids `<area id>/punting-pole-<n>`. Drifting nets: an optional `drift` (`NetDrift`: dx, dy, period, offset) on a hazard spawn, with `hazardShapeAt` and `hazardSweep` in `src/logic/hazards.ts`, `netDrift()` in `src/logic/ghostNets.ts`, and `DRIFTING_NETS` (`DriftingNetTuning`) in `tuning.ts`. The danger part mark: `dangerPart: true` on a species (the carp), and on every moving hazard type. The dev area: `dev-danger-area`, Area 98, `DEV_DANGER_AREA`. New modules: `src/logic/dangerBar.ts` and `src/logic/riskFairness.ts`. Built on top: `factCards.ts` became a runtime leaf module to fix an import cycle, with `src/logic/moduleCycles.test.ts` as its regression test. Earlier ids and order never change. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 249 | How does the chase rule work (N3, A1, A3)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-2): only a predator whose tuning has a chase chases, and the tilapia, heron, garter snake, and carp tunings have none, so without a chase every predator plays step for step as before. It chases only on patrol; if it may warn (A1, A3), it warns as before. Otherwise, when the axolotl is exposed (outside the safe area and, for a sight hunter, not in cover) and within its sight, it swims toward it at its chase speed, stopping just inside its lunge range (1e-6 pixels inside, so it can warn), where it warns, or waits in place while another predator warns or strikes (A1). It never ends a step more than its leash from its patrol loop; a step that would go farther ends on the leash's edge, on the line from the nearest point of the loop. It chases only while it is within its leash of its loop; beyond it (for example after a strike) it swims back along its route. The bounds and the safe area keep-out apply to every move; where they conflict, it holds its place. It faces the way it moves. Out of sight or with the axolotl in the safe area, it swims its route at patrol speed. The warning, the strike, and the cooldown do not change. A warn on route species chases along its route only, with its leash counted as 0, so the garter snake slides along its stretch and its depth bounds (open question 247) hold. Chasing is not attacking, so any number of predators may chase at once. There is no new cue, and reduced motion does not change the chase. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 250 | What are the chase limits (N3)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-3): the speed strictly between the 2-leg top speed (112.5 pixels per second) and the 4-leg top speed (150), worked out from the swim top speed and the leg factors; the sight greater than the lunge range of the tuning that carries the chase; and the leash 0 or more. `tunedSpecies` refuses anything else, and any value that is not a finite number, with a RangeError, and refuses a chase on a species with the fixed strike spot or still cooldown option (the heron). A test checks every default against the limits. A snag (half speed), queasy time (60%), or lost legs can leave the axolotl slower than a chaser; Tail Dash (330 pixels per second) always outruns one. An area's own tilapia tuning is not checked against these limits when it is built, so each phase 2 issue that adds a tilapia chase checks it in its own layout test (a residual in [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md)). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 251 | What chase does each predator type get (N3)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-4), opt-in per predator; phase 2 uses them: a tilapia in a full-bar area (Area 4 on), `TILAPIA_CHASE`, sight 240 pixels, leash 160, speed 125 pixels per second; a tilapia in a ramp-bar area (Areas 2 and 3), `TILAPIA_RAMP_CHASE`, sight 200, leash 80, speed 115, with Area 2's leash shorter than Area 3's, for example 60 (issue #121); the garter snake, `GARTER_SNAKE_CHASE`, sight 200, leash 0 (along its stretch), speed 120; and the carp, `CARP_CHASE`, sight 220, leash 120, speed 115. The heron has none and keeps its wading stalk (issue #120). The owner is to check in play that a 4-leg axolotl can swim away and a 1-leg one can escape with Tail Dash. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 252 | Does the risk ladder count a chaser's leash (B10, N2)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-5), a key default for the owner to decide: no. The risk ladder (B10) and the gap measure ignore the chase: a chasing predator's strike range S stays its lunge range, measured from its patrol loop, and the gap measure keeps out of it by the same reach. With the leash added, a Common spot in a full-bar area would need to be about 365 pixels from every tilapia loop, which leaves almost no Common spots; and the chase widens where a predator can warn by the same leash for every spot near it, so the ladder's order still holds. Only the danger bar counts the leash (open question 259). The other option: S = lunge range + leash. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 253 | What is the common carp (N3)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-6), opt-in per area: a predator on the shared rules, with no rule options, a danger part. Its tuning: a 0.8 second floor, a 1.0 second warning, a 120 pixel lunge range, a sweep at 200 pixels per second for 0.6 seconds (120 pixels), a 38 pixel hit reach (the tilapia's is 26), a 41 pixel touch reach, a 32 pixel body reach, a 2.5 second cooldown, patrol at 35 pixels per second, and the tilapia's bump (160 pixels per second, 0.6 seconds). Meet range 180 pixels; touching it gives a gentle bump. It roots along the bed, and phase 2 gives it a route near the bed, so with its chase it threatens only the low water. For B10, S = 120: Common farther than 180 pixels, Rare more than 120 and at most 180, Epic more than 60 and at most 120, and Legendary at most 60. Its warning cue is a nose-down tip and a small mud puff, with an ink outline in greyscale, shown still and fading under reduced motion; its strike a slow, wide tail sweep along the aim locked at the end of the warning. Its art is friendly, round, and bronze-gold, with two short barbels, a closed mouth, and no teeth, never shown biting or holding the axolotl ([ADR 0007](../adr/0007-kid-safety-and-privacy.md)). Its few frames are baked at boot with the other predators', a residual in [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 254 | Is the common carp fact card true (C13)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-7): the title "Meet the carp" and the body in C13, shown gently the first time the player meets a carp, with its sources in C13 and source comments in `src/logic/factCards.ts`; no source is invented, and the copy did not change. Owner to verify: the egg claim rests on two secondary sources (National Geographic Kids and The Wildlife Society), and no primary study of carp eating axolotl eggs in Xochimilco was found; "big fish" is not checked against a size source. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 255 | How does the moving hazard type work (N3, A1, A2, A4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-8; [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md)): a type holds its id, name, look id, timing (rest, warn, jab, hold, and lift, which fill its period), depth below the surface, shaft half width, touch reach, meet range, and fact card; a spawn holds its id, type, x, offset in seconds, and an optional depth of its own. `movingHazardSpawn()` refuses a warning under the 0.8 second floor, a negative time, a period of 0, or a depth not above 0. A straight shaft comes down from the surface (y 80) at its x; at rest and during the warning it touches nothing. The axolotl's body centre within the touch reach of the shaft, from the surface to the tip, is a touch: one hit by the shared hit rules (A4, A5, A11, A22), from the tip, counted in legs lost (Star 3), never a bump. Each encounter step checks the predators, then still hazards, then moving hazards, then the bump, with at most one hit per step; the touch is checked at the hazard time the step reaches, the same time the view draws. Its clock is the encounter's own hazard time, present only when the area has moving hazards, so every encounter state today stays the same: it starts at 0 each trip and runs only in encounter steps, so it stops with the pause, the banking card, and the carry home. It is not a predator, so it is outside A1's one-at-a-time rule, a residual in ADR 0021; its fixed cycle is always on show, and phase 2 keeps each sweep farther than every predator's lunge range from that predator's loop. It is a B10 threat (its full sweep, S its touch reach), met within its meet range of its sweep outside the safe area, a shape for the gap measure kept out by its touch reach, 80 pixels from every snack start, and a moving threat for the danger bar and a danger part. Looks are data: `MOVING_HAZARD_LOOKS` in `src/art/movingHazardTextures.ts` maps a look id to its painters, the view throws for a look with none baked, and the boot bakes a look only when some area has it. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 256 | What is the punting pole (N3, A2)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-9), opt-in per area: `PUNTING_POLES`, a 4 second cycle of rest 1.6 seconds, warn 1.0, jab 0.3, hold 0.5, and lift 0.6; depth 180 pixels, so the tip reaches y 260; half width 5 pixels, a 10 pixel pole; touch reach 18 pixels, the net reach and the body's half height; meet range 180 pixels. So the danger zone is x ± 23 pixels from the surface down to y 278: in an area two screens tall everything below y 278 is safe from it, and in an area one screen tall the 18 pixel band just over the bed passes under it. Its look is a trajinera's bright, painted bow on the surface over the pole, with no people, and the pole. Its warning cue: the boat's shadow darkens on the water under the bow, and the pole's tip dips to the surface with a ripple ring; in greyscale the shadow and the ring have an ink outline, and under reduced motion they show still while the jab still moves. As drawing only, the view shows the resting tip 24 pixels above the surface and dips it in the warning. It is never shown hitting or pinning the axolotl. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 257 | Is the punting pole fact card true (C13)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-10): id `punting-pole`, the title "Trajineras", and the body in C13, with its sources in C13 and source comments in `src/logic/factCards.ts`; no source is invented, and the copy did not change. Owner to verify: "flat-bottomed" rests on Wikipedia (its Fodor's source returned 403); the same Wikipedia page says a rower uses an oar, while the other sources describe poles; and "the mud" is the sources' canal floor, sediment, and marshy bottom. The UNESCO World Heritage listing returned 403 and was not read. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 258 | How do drifting ghost nets work (N3, A9)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-11), opt-in per area: a ghost net drifts when its data has a drift (dx, dy, period, offset), back and forth from its place to its place plus (dx, dy), its share of the path (1 − cos(2π (t + offset) / period)) / 2, where t is the trip's element time; a net with no drift never moves. `DRIFTING_NETS`: a 16 second cycle and a path of at most 200 pixels; `netDrift()` refuses a longer path or a period of 0. A 160 pixel path averages 20 pixels per second and peaks at about 31. It snags like a still net, measured where it is now, never costs a leg, and is not a B10 threat; it is met within 100 pixels of where it is now. The snag and the meeting use the element time the step reaches. Every placement rule uses its whole drift path (its shape's bounding rect over the path): snack starts, mobile snack lanes and spots, the gap measure, and phase 2's net rules (190.5 pixels from every current, the predator clearances, the spots, the stranding places, and the exit). The mats' push-out checks each net where it is now. Phase 2 lays each drift path along the flow of the current nearby. Under reduced motion it still drifts, with no extra sway; in greyscale it looks the same as a still net. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 259 | What are the danger bar and the ramp bar, and how are they measured (N2)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-12), a key default for the owner to decide, with one part open. The full bar, from Area 4 on: at least ⌈0.75 x screens⌉ moving threats, where each tilapia route, other predator route, and moving hazard is one, and the screens are the area's width over 480 times its height over 360; at least 2 predator species and 1 moving hazard type; every predator chasing; and the longest quiet stretch of the main path at most 480 pixels. The ramp bar, Areas 2 and 3: the tilapia chase, the longest quiet stretch at most 480 pixels, and no moving hazard type in Area 2 and at most one in Area 3. Area 1 stays gentle. In reach means within lunge range plus leash of a predator's loop (no leash without a chase or with warn on route), or within touch reach of a moving hazard's sweep, the main path sampled every 1 pixel. `src/logic/dangerBar.ts` has the counts, the quiet stretch, and both bar checks; its tests use made-up layouts and the dev area, and assert nothing about Areas 1 to 5. The dev area does not meet the full bar (6 moving threats of the 9 its 12 screens need, 3 predator species, 1 moving hazard type, and a longest quiet stretch of 899.16 pixels): it is a showcase, not a bar proof. Open: the issue asks for a "short leash" in the ramp bar but gives no number, so the ramp bar checks that the tilapia chase but not a leash length; the other option is a number, for example at most `TILAPIA_RAMP_CHASE`'s 80 pixels. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 260 | How does the one-new-thing rule treat the danger parts (N2)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-13), a key default for the owner to decide: the danger parts are outside the rule. Counting new things per area leaves out a species marked as a danger part (the carp) and every moving hazard type, so each area's one-new-thing pins and its limit stay as they are when phase 2 adds danger parts. The mark is on the species, or comes with the type, never a shared list of ids. The danger parts still count as threats, have fact cards, and are met. Mobile snack types are not counted either (open question 274). The other option: count them, so an area adding the carp or a moving hazard type would use its one new hazard or predator. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 261 | How does phase 2 add the danger parts to an area (N2)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-14): each area's own files only. A tilapia chase goes in the area's own tilapia tuning in its layout file, `{ ...<its tuning or TILAPIA>, chase }`; Areas 2 and 3 get a `tilapia` field for the first time, and with only `chase` added their risk bands do not change. Any other species chases, or takes per-area tuning, through `tunedSpecies(SPECIES, { ...TUNING, chase })` in the area's layout file, keeping its id. Moving hazards: the area's own `movingHazards` list, built with `movingHazardSpawn()`. Drifting nets: `drift: netDrift(...)` on the area's own ghost net data. Mobile snacks: `mobileSnacks: mobileSnackMix({...})` in the area's own layout file, with its worms and bugs lowered by the mobile count; its area entry in `src/logic/areas.ts` stays as it is. Proofs live in the area's own layout test (the bars, the gaps, its snack counts, its snack ramp row, the clear check on every lane and spot, its magnet pull, and no one-time darters with shrimp), its own fairness file, and its own golden, re-recorded on purpose. Each phase 2 issue edits only its own N2 paragraph, its rows, the snack values it built, and new open questions; never the H4 snack ramp table. The only shared code edit in phase 2 is a pond look in Area 3 (issue #120): one painter, one look entry, one moving hazard type, and its card. The Collection Book pins move with whichever issue first adds the carp, the pole, the tadpole, or the shrimp to an area, and the issue that merges second rebases and fixes them. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 262 | What does the danger pass dev area hold (N2)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-15): `dev-danger-area` (`DEV_DANGER_AREA`, Area 98), after the dev test area in the dev areas, never in the area list, loaded in dev builds only with `?area=dev-danger-area`. Chinampa habitat and look, 6 x 2 screens (2880 x 720, bed top 678), with its own data written out as numbers. The Home Spot at (120, 200), radius 72; the safe area x 40 to 300 from the surface to 678; the exit at (2780, 625), radius 40. Its tilapia tuning is the tilapia's with `TILAPIA_CHASE`; `tilapia-0` on a loop x 640 to 760, y 160 to 240, and `tilapia-1` x 2160 to 2300, y 460 to 540; `garter-snake-0` with `GARTER_SNAKE_CHASE` on a stretch from (1700, 100) to (1900, 100); `carp-0` with `CARP_CHASE` on a loop x 900 to 1200, y 600 to 640; `dev-danger-area/punting-pole-1` at x 1150, offset 0 seconds, and `dev-danger-area/punting-pole-2` at x 1350, offset 2 seconds; a current x 1500 to 1700, y 400 to 640, flowing left at 70 pixels per second, with 4 snacks in it; `ghost-net-1` still at x 2440 to 2500, y 400 to 460, `ghost-net-2` at x 840 to 900, y 420 to 480, drifting 160 pixels left over 16 seconds from offset 0, and `ghost-net-3` at x 1240 to 1300, y 380 to 430, the same from offset 8, both paths at least 200 pixels from the current; tadpole lanes from (330, 330), (1340, 520), and (2400, 200), each 120 pixels at 60 pixels per second, with offsets 0, 4/3, and 8/3 seconds; shrimp at (560, 420), (1000, 300), and (2600, 420), with a 60 pixel trigger, a 1.2 second rest, a 60 pixel flick, and 145 pixels per second; 16 snacks, 6 worms, 4 bugs, 3 tadpoles, and 3 shrimp; Area 4's drift (amplitudes 16 and 8, periods 7.6 and 6.08 seconds); no one-time darters; a 72 pixel per second magnet pull; a root tangle cave x 720 to 880, y 560 to 678; and stranding places at (400, 560), (1400, 330), and (2600, 560). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 263 | How are Areas 1 to 5 kept unchanged by the danger pass (Testing Decisions)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-16): a golden test for Area 5, `src/logic/chinampa2Golden.test.ts`, modelled on the Area 4 golden, recorded from main in the first commit, before any shared change. It and the canal, pond, Area 3, and Area 4 goldens stayed byte-identical through issue #117, and no golden was re-recorded to make it pass; since then each phase 2 issue re-records only its own area's golden, on purpose (open question 297). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 264 | How does the fairness check handle the chase and moving hazards (B10)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-17): the shared helpers move into a pure module, `src/logic/riskFairness.ts`, and the per-area blocks into one file per area (`canalFairness`, `pondFairness`, `pond2Fairness`, `chinampaFairness`, and `chinampa2Fairness`), with every test name and assertion kept, so the test count does not change; `src/logic/riskFairness.test.ts` keeps only tests of the harness. A safe start is farther than lunge range from the loop of every predator that does not chase, farther than leash plus the larger of sight and lunge range from the loop of every predator that chases (the leash as written, also for a warn on route species), and outside every moving hazard's sweep grown by its touch reach. The start times span each moving hazard's cycle too. Visits step the real encounter, so chases and poles play. With no chase and no moving hazard, every result is the same as before. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 265 | How do the scenes draw the danger parts and mobile snacks (N3, H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-18): the tilapia view draws the tilapia only, so a new species never gets a tilapia sprite, and its poof and halo handle a hit from a moving hazard. A carp view, like the garter snake's: swim frames while the carp moves, the warning pose with the mud puff, the sweep pose, turning away in the cooldown, and one pose under reduced motion. A moving hazard view reuses its bow, shadow, shaft, and ripple ring, placed each frame from the hazard's phase and tip depth. A mobile snack view sets each tadpole's and shrimp's frame and facing each frame, with every snack sprite kept in one map, so the pop and the last-3 stars work unchanged. The ghost net view moves drifting nets each frame by the element time. The swim scene builds each new view only when its area has that part, and an area without them builds and calls nothing new. The boot bakes the carp frames with the other predators', and the moving hazard looks and mobile snack textures only when some area, or the loaded dev area, needs them. The moving hazard and ghost net views allocate one small object per moving or drifting spawn per frame through the logic helpers; only areas with those parts pay it (a residual in [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md)). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 266 | What happens to saves and the Collection Book (C14, D2)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-19): no save change. A danger pass card joins the book once a shipped area has its part, so issue #117 kept the book's 9 cards and changed no book, save, or session pin. In the dev area each card shows when it is met. The session keeps only met ids that have a book card, and a card with no book entry cannot be marked found, so a new card with no book entry shows again on each dev trip; that is a dev-only residual in [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md), with no code change. The copy fit test checks the four cards directly, so phase 2 never edits it for copy fit. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 267 | Where are the danger pass's architecture decisions recorded? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-20): in [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md), with a pointer from the follow-ups of [ADR 0014](../adr/0014-area-framework.md). It records the ids (open questions 248 and 269); the chase as opt-in tuning, its limits, and the warn on route rule; the risk ladder ignoring the chase, and the other option; the moving hazard type in the encounter with its own clock, and why it is neither an element (elements never cost a leg) nor a still hazard (a still hazard has no clock); drifting nets on the element clock, placed by their whole drift path; the carp on the shared rules; the danger parts outside the one-new-thing rule; the mobile snacks; the phase 2 seams (open question 261); and the residuals (open questions 250, 253, 255, 265, 266, and 273). [ADR 0022](../adr/0022-danger-pass-phase-2-seam-amendments.md) amends it for phase 2 (open question 302). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 268 | Which docs record the danger pass (N2)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-21): this PRD (the N2 danger pass part with one paragraph per area, N3, A1 to A4, A9, A17, A19, H4 with the snack ramp, C13, C14, the Tuning values, the Testing Decisions, the Glossary, and open questions 248 to 278); CONTEXT.md; the [art style guide](../art-style-guide.md); and the README's dev area list, which adds `?area=dev-danger-area`. No new player bullets in the README, because nothing changes in the shipped areas. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 269 | What are the mobile snacks' ids, names, and modules (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-22; [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md)): the snack kinds gain `tadpole` and `shrimp`, with spawn ids `snack-tadpole-<n>` and `snack-shrimp-<n>`, n from 1 in data order. `src/logic/mobileSnacks.ts` holds the rules and their constructors (`tadpoleRule()`, `tadpoleLane()`, `shrimpRule()`, and `mobileSnackMix()`), the lane a tadpole carries, the clear check, the top speed and magnet pull, the one-time darter choice, meeting, the drawn pose, and the snack ramp (`mobileSnackShare()`, `SNACK_RAMP`, and `meetsSnackRamp()`). `src/logic/shrimpFlick.ts` holds the repeat flick. The limits are `TADPOLES` and `SHRIMP` in `src/logic/tuning.ts`, with no per-area values. The fact cards are `TADPOLE_FACT` (id `tadpole`, "Tadpole") and `SHRIMP_FACT` (id `shrimp`, "Freshwater shrimp"), with `TADPOLE_MEET_RANGE` and `SHRIMP_MEET_RANGE`. The area data field is `mobileSnacks`, copied onto the built layout only when set. Also: `meetSnacks` in `src/logic/trip.ts`, an optional `keepClear` on the spit rule in `src/logic/plasticWorms.ts`, and an optional `lane` on a snack. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 270 | How are mobile snacks set and counted per area (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-23): fixed area data written out as numbers in the area's layout file, tadpole lanes and shrimp spots, never seeded, so they add no random draw. They come after the seeded worms and bugs, tadpoles first, then shrimp, in data order. An area without mobile snacks builds exactly as before. A mix with lanes and no tadpole rule, spots and no shrimp rule, or any value outside its limits is refused. Each area keeps its total snack count, and phase 2 lowers its worms and bugs by its mobile count, which re-draws that area's seeded snacks, so its phase 2 issue re-records only its own golden, on purpose (open question 297). An area's snacks in the current are a floor: that many are placed in its first current on purpose, stripe snacks may also start there, and every snack in a current is a worm or a bug ([ADR 0022](../adr/0022-danger-pass-phase-2-seam-amendments.md); open question 302). The phase 2 counts are in the H4 snack ramp table. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 271 | How does the tadpole swim (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-24): a straight lane from (x, y) to (x + dx, y + dy), 80 to 160 pixels long (120 in Areas 2 to 5), swum there and back at a steady speed, turning at once at each end. At time t on the snack drift clock, which runs only on swimming steps and starts at 0 each trip, its distance along a lane of length L is min(s, 2L − s), where s = ((t + offset) x speed) mod 2L, clamped to the swim bounds. The stored place is the lane start, so the Snack magnet moves the lane with it. No drift and no bob, the same with and without reduced motion. Lane n of N gets offset (n − 1) x (2L / speed) / N. It faces the sign of its x velocity, and right on a lane with dx = 0. The wiggle is drawing only: 4 tail frames at 8 frames a second from the scene clock and a ±8 degree sway; under reduced motion, frame 0 with no sway. Its speed is above 0 and at most 72 pixels per second, and an offset outside [0, 2L / speed) is refused. It comes back to each lane end every 2L / speed seconds. Speeds: Area 2, 25 pixels per second; Area 3, 35; Area 4, 48; Area 5, 60; Area 6 on, 60 to 72, never slower than the area before. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 272 | How does the freshwater shrimp flick (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-25): at rest it drifts and bobs like a worm or a bug, faces the head, and is ready at the trip start. A ready, uncollected shrimp drawn within its trigger radius of the head reach centre (inclusive, but not on it) flicks straight away from the head by its flick distance, only when its drift and bob box at the landing passes the clear check (open question 273); else it stays ready. The flick moves its stored place, gliding at its flick speed, or landing at once under reduced motion. It then rests, a ready-at time on the drift clock, and is ready again, with no limit. A collected shrimp never moves, and a flick whose shrimp is eaten stops; a magnet pull adds to a glide. Limits (`SHRIMP`): a trigger radius above 36 pixels, a rest of at least 1.0 second, a flick of 24 to 72 pixels, a flick speed above 0 and at most 150 pixels per second, and the catch limit rest x 70 ≥ trigger radius + flick distance − 36, where 70 is a one-leg axolotl's 90 less 20 for drift (every area's drift is under 16). Values, with a 60 pixel trigger in every area: Area 2, 2.0 seconds, 36 pixels, 100 pixels per second; Area 3, 1.7, 44, 115; Area 4, 1.4, 52, 130; Area 5, 1.2, 60, 145; Area 6 on, Area 5's or harder, inside the catch limit, 145 to 150 pixels per second. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 273 | Where may a mobile snack go (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-26), a key default for the owner to decide: one shared check. A tadpole's whole lane, and a shrimp's whole drift and bob box at its spot or at a flick landing, must be inside the swim bounds and below the highest wave; outside the Home Spot ring, the safe area, every current, and every mat pair's whole sweep, edges counting as inside; at least 80 pixels from every plastic worm where it is now, every hazard's shape (a drifting net by its whole drift path), every element sweep, and every moving hazard's sweep; and farther than S from every B10 threat: each predator route by its lunge range from its loop (a tilapia by the area's own tuning), with no leash added, and each hazard that costs a leg by its reach. Phase 2 proves every lane and spot with it, and each flick landing is checked at run time with the plastic worms where they are now. A spat plastic worm never lands within 80 pixels of a tadpole lane, and nothing changes without lanes. A magnet-pulled tadpole's lane moves with it and can briefly cross a keep-out, a residual in [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md); the pull is faster than every snack, so it is eaten soon. The other option: S + leash for a chasing predator, which leaves much less room in full-bar areas. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 274 | How do mobile snacks count, and what happens to the one-time darters (H4, H2, H3)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-27), a key default for the owner to decide: a tadpole and a shrimp each count as one snack for the counter, the last-3 sparkle, the exit, the snack speed-up, and the reset when carried home. The shrimp replaces the one-time darter: an area whose layout has shrimp gets no one-time darters, and otherwise the darters are picked from the worms and bugs only, so a tadpole is never one, and Areas 1 to 5 get exactly today's darter ids. The one-time darter rule, its data, and its tests do not change; phase 2 leaves the area's one-time darter data in its area entry, and the shrimp turns it off. Mobile snack types are snacks, not threats or elements, so the one-new-thing rule and the danger bar never count them. The other option: each phase 2 issue deletes the one-time darter line from its area entry, which is a shared-file edit. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 275 | How fast does the Snack magnet pull in an area with mobile snacks (A19)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-28), a key default for the owner to decide: each trip's pull is max(base, 1.2 x the fastest top speed of the area's mobile snacks), with base the perk's 40 pixels per second. A tadpole's top speed is its speed, and a shrimp's flick distance / (rest + flick distance / flick speed), the farthest it gets per second over one rest and one flick; a flick on its own is a short hop like a darter's. A pulled snack always closes on a still head. The swim scene works it out once per trip from the zone. The perk's constant and its tests do not change, and an area with no mobile snacks gets exactly 40, so Areas 1 to 5 and their goldens are unchanged. Values: Area 2, 40 (1.2 x 25 = 30 is lower); Area 3, 42; Area 4, 57.6; Area 5, 72; the dev area, 72. The 72 pixel per second tadpole limit keeps every pull at or under 86.4, below a one-leg axolotl's 90. The other option: one bigger pull for every area, which would change the perk constant and its tests. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 276 | What are the mobile snack fact cards, and when are they met (C13, C14, A21)? | Built, provisional (issue #117, named default ND-29): "Meet the tadpole" (id `tadpole`, "Tadpole") and "Freshwater shrimp" (id `shrimp`, "Freshwater shrimp"), with the bodies and sources in C13 and source comments in `src/logic/factCards.ts`; no source is invented, and the copy did not change. The axolotl meets a type the first time its centre is within 120 pixels of an uncollected snack of that type, where it is drawn, outside the safe area; the id joins the trip's met list, so the card comes due and shows with play paused, as in A21. An area's snack type cards come after its element cards, tadpole first. Owner to verify, on the shrimp card: "tiny" (the source lists a size of 7 cm), and the sources are about Australian species, not species of Xochimilco. Needs owner confirmation. |

| 306 | Can Area 5 reach the full danger bar (N2)? | Answered by issue #135 (owner decision, 2026-10-24, named default ND-1; open question 382): yes, with the pole rule strike only. Each pole's sweep only has to be farther than every predator's strike reach from its loop, as ADR 0021 says, not the strike reach plus the chase leash. With five carp (open question 383) and the whole-water test, Area 5 meets the full danger bar (open question 387). Before issue #135: Built, provisional (issue #118, named default ND-4; owner decision in session, 2026-10-24, option A). A feasibility probe found that the full bar cannot be reached with the mats as built and no shared change. With the defaults as first written, no legal place exists for a third or fourth tilapia anywhere in the area, in a scan of about 16,000 loop centres, which leaves 9 threats and a 1,005 pixel quiet stretch. No legal tilapia or carp loop exists in x 300 to 1800 in either row, so only punting poles deeper than 180 pixels can cover the bottom row west of x 1850, and with every pole at the default 180 pixel depth the longest quiet stretch is 1,761 pixels. A pole deeper than 234 pixels drew its shaft detached from its boat, because the shaft art is 224 pixels long. The owner chose option A: one shared fix, so a pole's drawn shaft follows its spawn's own depth (`src/scenes/MovingHazardView.ts` and `src/art/drawPuntingPole.ts`, with `src/art/movingHazardArt.test.ts`). A deep pole draws the pole art with wood-only copies of its middle stacked above it, each lifted by whole 32 pixel node spacings, so the shaft reaches its boat with no seam ([art style guide](../art-style-guide.md) 7b). It is backward compatible: a pole at the default 180 pixel depth draws exactly as before. Area 5 is built to the full bar, and spots, stranding places, and routes move as the rules need (open question 316). Every Area 5 check holds except the second half of "keep the main path out of every predator reach", which the danger bar proof replaces (open question 311). Needs owner confirmation. |

| 308 | Where are Area 5's punting poles (N2, N3)? | Built, provisional (issue #118, named default ND-6): five, in top-row columns with no garden plot (x, depth, offset): `chinampa-2/punting-pole-1` at x 476, 440 pixels, 0 seconds; `chinampa-2/punting-pole-2` at x 964, 400 pixels, 2 seconds; `chinampa-2/punting-pole-3` at x 1400, 360 pixels, 0 seconds; `chinampa-2/punting-pole-4` at x 1760, 340 pixels, 2 seconds; and `chinampa-2/punting-pole-5` at x 1840, 340 pixels, 0 seconds. Each jab crosses the main path in both rows. Each sweep is farther than every predator's strike reach plus its chase leash from its loop (150 + 160 pixels for the tilapia, 110 + 0 for the snakes), more than the [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md) bound of the strike reach alone. That bound measures the sweep, not its touch zone, and the strike reach, not the whole reach of a strike, so it does not keep every jab out of every predator's reach. The closest to a snake is `chinampa-2/punting-pole-2` and `garter-snake-0`, 111 pixels against 110: the pole's 18 pixel touch zone comes 17 pixels inside the snake's warning reach, and near the surface its sweep is inside the snake's 154 pixel dart reach (the measure the mat tests use, open question 309). The closest to a tilapia is `chinampa-2/punting-pole-5` and `tilapia-0`, 315 pixels against 310, so its touch zone comes 13 pixels inside. So a predator can warn at an axolotl at the edge of those two jabs; the owner checks these on the PR. No two pole touch zones overlap, and each stays at least 36 pixels from every mat sweep, out of the safe area, every bank overhang, and the exit, with each tip a touch reach above the bed. Neighbours are 2 seconds out of step. A deep pole draws its whole shaft up to its boat (open question 306). Area 5 is the first shipped area with moving hazards, so its frame time is still to be measured: the owner runs a trip through all five poles on WebGL and with `?renderer=canvas`, on the 6x throttled stand-in and the owner's phone, against the [ADR 0013](../adr/0013-axolotl-rig-and-frame-budget.md) budget, as [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-danger-pass-chase-rule-and-moving-hazards.md) sets for phase 2. Each frame, every pole's view works out its phase and tip depth, which makes one small object per pole. If the budget is missed, that work is optimized before release. Needs owner confirmation. |

| 343 | How does the danger bar test all the water (N2)? | Built, provisional (issue #130, named default ND-1): with quiet pockets. A grid covers the swim water, one point every 8 pixels (`QUIET_GRID_STEP`), with its last row and column on the water's edges. Each point is in reach (open question 345), home (inside the safe area), or quiet. Quiet points that touch, diagonals included, make one quiet pocket. A quiet swim starts at an in-reach or home point, goes through the quiet points of one pocket only, and ends at an in-reach or home point or at the water's edge; its length is the shortest such way on the grid, 8 pixels across or up and down and about 11.3 pixels on a diagonal, which matches the old measure from one in-reach sample to the next. `longestQuietSwim(layout)` gives the longest quiet swim and its two end points, so a PR can say where it is, and it is exact. The bar: at most 480 pixels. The bars first take a quick bound per pocket (twice the farthest swim from one point), skip any pocket whose bound is at most 480, and stop at the first swim over 480. Threats do not depend on the zone seed, so a layout test proves the bar on the default seed and checks that the threats match on other seeds. It errs a little on the strict side: a diagonal on the grid counts up to about 8% long. Other options: the safest route from the Home Spot to the exit, with the old measure along it and along every horizontal lane (a second way can still be quiet); or the old measure on every horizontal lane, every 8 pixels (it misses slanting swims, and up-and-down swims in tall areas). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 344 | Which water does the whole-water test count (N2)? | Built, provisional (issue #130, named default ND-2): the swim water, `swimBounds(layout)`, x 40 to the width less 40 and y 94 to the bed top less 22. The area framework has open water and no solid terrain, so all of it counts, across the line between screen rows too. Ghost nets, water hyacinth mats, currents and bank overhangs are water: they slow, block for a while, or push, but never cost a leg, so a swim through them can be quiet. The safe area is home: never quiet and never a threat. An area that later adds solid terrain teaches the test about it in its own PR. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 345 | What is in reach for the whole-water test (N2, B10)? | Built, provisional (issue #130, named default ND-3): unchanged from open question 259. A predator is in reach within its lunge range plus its leash of its patrol loop, a tilapia by its area's own tuning; the leash is 0 with no chase or with the warn-on-route rule. A moving hazard is in reach within its touch reach of its whole sweep. Cover is ignored, positions are fixed (a chaser counts its whole leash, and a pole its whole sweep), and still hazards (plastic worms, ghost nets) do not count. The risk ladder (B10) and the gap measure still ignore the leash (open question 252). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 346 | How is the swim water split into depth bands (N2)? | Built, provisional (issue #130, named default ND-4): into three equal bands of its height, surface, middle and floor. One screen tall: y 94 to 161, 161 to 229, and 229 to 296. Two screens tall: y 94 to 281, 281 to 469, and 469 to 656. Three screens tall: y 94 to 401, 401 to 709, and 709 to 1016. Other options: bands per screen row, or fixed 120 pixel bands at the top and the bottom. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 347 | How strict is the depth rule (N2, N3)? | Built, provisional (issue #130, named default ND-5; owner decisions 1, 6 and 8 on issue #130, 2026-10-24): it covers tilapia routes, carp routes (species id `'carp'`) and, once it is built, the black bass (#78). (a) Each loop reaches at least 24 pixels, a tilapia body, into at least two of the three bands, so no loop is held on one line. (b) Together, each species' loops reach at least 24 pixels into every band. Other options: every loop spans all three bands (in a two-screen-tall area a loop would be about 560 pixels tall, leaving room for far fewer predators); or (b) only. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 348 | What is the floor rule, and when does it apply (N2)? | Built, provisional (issue #130, named default ND-6; owner decision 2 on issue #130, 2026-10-24): an area has surface danger when it has any moving hazard (every type today comes down from the surface), or any predator route lying wholly inside the surface band (today, the heron and the garter snake). Then the floor lane, the line along the bottom of the swim water (y = the bed top less 22), sampled every 1 pixel with home as a threat point, has no stretch longer than 480 pixels out of every predator's reach. Only predators count here, not moving hazards. Once the depth rule holds, a tilapia or carp loop never lies wholly in one band, so only surface species trigger the rule. Other options: every lane in the floor band, or a quiet-pocket test inside the floor band with predators only. Needs owner confirmation. |

| 353 | How is the fair start relaxed (B10)? | Built, provisional (issue #130, named default ND-11; owner decision 9 on issue #130, 2026-10-24): a safe start only needs to be farther than the lunge range plus the leash from every predator loop, the same reach as open question 345; sight is no longer counted. It must still be clear of every moving hazard and inside the water. The search reaches the whole area, not 600 pixels, and still takes the nearest safe start to the spot. Known limit: the search checks rings 1 pixel apart at 360 angles each, so it can miss a safe pocket narrower than that gap; then it finds no start and the fairness test fails loudly, never passing unfairly. The fair-start check (`firstFairStart`) does not change: from that start there must be a moment to swim to the spot, park and leave without a hit, with every chase and moving hazard playing. Each area's fairness test may raise its timeout; no other check changes. If a spot still has no fair start, the area issue stops and asks the owner. Other options: keep the clearance and only widen the search; or count only the lunge range, with no leash. Planner note, the old clearance pins: two area fairness test files pinned the old rule, so only these pins moved to the new rule, with their tests renamed to match: `src/logic/pondFairness.test.ts` uses 200 pixels (140 lunge range plus 60 leash) instead of 260, and `src/logic/chinampaFairness.test.ts` uses 305 pixels for the tilapia (145 plus 160) instead of 400, and 240 pixels for the carp (120 plus 120) instead of 340. Every other check in every fairness file stays, and no timeout changed. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 354 | How does a tadpole wander (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #131, named default ND-1; owner decision 1 on issue #131, 2026-10-24, that tadpoles never stop; [ADR 0024](../adr/0024-mobile-snack-wander-loops-and-shrimp-hops.md)): each tadpole swims a closed wander loop of 4 to 8 turn points, written as numbers in its area's layout file, at its area's steady speed, forever, turning at each point. Where it is drawn comes from the snack drift clock alone, as the lane's did: ((t + offset) x speed) mod lap pixels along the loop. There is no random draw and no seed: `buildZone` still adds mobile snacks after the seeded worms and bugs with no draw, so seeded snacks, treasure placements, and the encounter and trip traces stay the same, and ADR 0021's "fixed area data, not seeded" still holds. The fairness harness reads no snacks. Uneven legs and zig-zags through the depths make it look like wandering, not a patrol. Not taken: a smooth closed curve (its speed is not steady, it cannot bend round a keep-out, and it looks mechanical); turn points from a fixed hash of the area id and snack number (nobody can see or hand-place the loop, and changing the hash moves every area); and seeded draws, which ADR 0021 already rejected. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 355 | How big is a tadpole's home area (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #131, named default ND-2): the box around its loop, 320 to 480 pixels wide, about one screen, and at least 150 pixels tall, about 3/4 of the 202 pixels a one-screen-tall area can swim (y 94 to 296), so the loop reaches from near the surface to near the bed; a taller area aims for about 200. It fits inside one 480 x 360 box. Every turn point is inside the swim bounds and below the highest wave. Areas have no solid terrain, and the hard rules keep mats and currents out (open question 360). Not taken: a home rectangle stored as its own data with the loop inside it, two things to keep in step for no gain. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 356 | How often does a tadpole change direction (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #131, named default ND-3): each leg, turn point to turn point, takes 1.5 to 5 seconds at the area's speed, 60 to 200 pixels at 40 pixels per second and 105 to 350 at 70. A lap takes at most 40 seconds, so waiting in one place on its loop always catches the tadpole within a lap. An area's tadpoles start out of step, set by their offsets. Not taken: a fixed 2 seconds a leg, too regular to look like wandering. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 357 | How does a tadpole turn on screen (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #131, named default ND-4): drawing only. Its place stays exactly on its loop, so every proof uses straight legs. At each corner the sprite eases to the new leg's tilt over 0.25 seconds; the tilt is the leg's angle from level, held to 40 degrees each way, so a steep leg shows a steep tilt and never a sideways tadpole. It flips to face the new leg's sideways way at the corner. A near-vertical leg, whose sideways part is under a quarter of its length, keeps the facing it had. Not taken: rounded corners in the path itself, which make the proofs and the catch math harder. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 358 | How does a freshwater shrimp hop (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #131, named default ND-5; owner decision 3 on issue #131, 2026-10-24, that shrimp are always busy): a shrimp's home is a small loop of 3 to 6 hop spots, written as numbers in its area's layout file, in a box at most 200 x 160 pixels. When its rest ends and the head is farther than 120 pixels, twice its trigger radius, it hops toward its next hop spot: at most 2/3 of its flick distance, at 2/3 of its flick speed, landing on the spot when it is that close, then aiming at the next one. While the head is within 120 pixels it does not hop; it waits, ready to flick. After a hop it rests 3/4 of its rest time, and after a flick its full rest time. Hopping shrimp n of N first rests (n − 1) / N of its rest after a hop. A hop lands only where the shrimp's drift and bob pass the hard rules (open question 360), with each plastic worm where it is now; when its next spot is not clear it tries the one after, and when none is clear it rests again. A hop is always slower than its flick, so its top speed for the Snack magnet does not change. By area, from the ramp's shrimp values: rest after a hop 1.5, 1.275, 1.05, and 0.9 seconds; hops of 24, about 29, about 35, and 40 pixels at about 67, 77, 87, and 97 pixels per second; a hop about every 1.9, 1.7, 1.45, and 1.3 seconds, for Areas 2 to 5. Not taken: a separate hop distance, speed, and rest per area, three more values to tune when "every 1 to 2 seconds" already follows from each area's rest. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 359 | Does the flick still win over a hop (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #131, named default ND-6): yes. When its rest ends and the head is within its trigger radius, a shrimp flicks as before, with the same trigger radius, flick distance, flick speed, and catch limit. New: a hopping shrimp's flick lands only inside its home box grown by its flick distance, where its drift and bob pass the hard rules; otherwise it does not flick that moment, as when a landing is not clear, so a player cannot herd it across the area. After a flick it hops back toward its next hop spot. Needs owner confirmation. |
| 360 | Where may a mobile snack go, now that it wanders and hops (H4)? | Built, provisional (issue #131, named default ND-7; owner decision 6 on issue #131, 2026-10-24, that the keep-out rule is relaxed for tadpoles and shrimp alike; [ADR 0024](../adr/0024-mobile-snack-wander-loops-and-shrimp-hops.md)): this replaces open question 273's lane and spot checks. Why: that rule kept a whole lane farther than every predator's strike reach, and a home a screen wide that reaches nearly the full depth cannot meet it beside the danger bar's predators. Hard rules, for every tadpole turn point and leg box and every shrimp hop spot, hop leg box, and landing: inside the swim bounds and below the highest wave; out of the Home Spot ring, the safe area, every current, and every mat sweep; and at least 80 pixels from every plastic worm and every hazard's shape, a drifting ghost net by its whole drift path. A shrimp's whole way on every flick and hop, with its drift and bob box, passes the hard rules too, the plastic worms where they are now counting only at the landing. Threat rule, tadpoles: a predator or a moving hazard may reach part of the loop, but the loop's longest stretch out of every threat's reach is at least half the loop. Threat rule, shrimp: at least half of a shrimp's hop spots, with their drift and bob box, are out of every threat's reach. Reach for both is B10's S (`zoneThreats`): a predator's lunge range from its loop, with no leash, and a moving hazard's touch reach from its sweep. The threat rules are there because the exit needs every snack eaten (H2). Spit rule: a spat plastic worm never lands within 80 pixels of a tadpole leg's box or a shrimp hop leg's box; `SpitAvoid.keepClear` gets one box per leg, and a slanted leg's box is bigger than the leg, so a few more spits stay where they were. `src/logic/plasticWorms.ts` does not change. A shrimp with a 1-spot home keeps open question 273's check at each flick landing. Not taken: keeping the strict rule (a home a screen wide at every depth will not fit), or ignoring threats (that forces a risk to open the exit). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 361 | How is a tadpole proved catchable, and how fast does the Snack magnet pull (H4, A19)? | Built, provisional (issue #131, named default ND-8): 70 pixels per second is under the 72 cap, 0.8 x a one-leg axolotl's 90 pixels per second; two, three, and four legs swim at 112.5, 135, and 150. In issue #131 it runs on the dev area and on made-up layouts; each area's own issue runs it on every tadpole in that area. Shrimp: the catch limit stays, and no hop happens within 120 pixels of the head. The Snack magnet pull stays max(40, 1.2 x the fastest mobile snack's top speed), so 48, 60, 72, and 84 pixels per second for Areas 2 to 5, all under 86.4, and 84 in the dev area. Owner decision on issue #131 (2026-10-24): a shrimp's top speed stays the farthest it gets in a second over one rest and one flick, hops do not count, and the pull may stay below a shrimp's own hop and flick speeds, because inside the magnet's radius a shrimp never hops and the pull is at least 1.2 x that top speed ([ADR 0024](../adr/0024-mobile-snack-wander-loops-and-shrimp-hops.md)). Known gap: a queasy one-leg axolotl (54 pixels per second) or a snagged one (45) cannot catch a 70 pixel per second tadpole until it recovers, after 3 or 2 seconds; 60 pixel per second tadpoles already had the same kind of gap, and Tail Dash (330 pixels per second) always catches one. Kept as is, from ADR 0021: the Snack magnet moves a tadpole's whole loop with its stored place, a bounded residual, because the pull is always faster than the tadpole, so it is eaten soon ([ADR 0024](../adr/0024-mobile-snack-wander-loops-and-shrimp-hops.md)). A tadpole faster than 72 needs a new owner decision on the cap; later areas can get harder with more tadpoles and longer loops instead. Needs owner confirmation. |

| 423 | Are the wood block's pitches and the fireflies' range right (N6)? | Built, provisional (issue #153): the wood block plays A5 on the beat and D6 off the beat, the nearest semitones to the sketches' 880 Hz and 1.3 times that (about 3% higher off the beat than the sketch). Fireflies chime from the key's major pentatonic above its home note in octave 6, sometimes an octave higher, so they reach up to E8, above the C7 ceiling every other pitched note keeps; they are the softest notes in the mix (0.045). Needs owner confirmation. |
| 424 | Was frame time with music on checked in Areas 3 and 5 (M12, X11)? | Not measured on a real device (issue #153). The owner checks frame time with music on, and off, in Areas 3 and 5 on the target devices during the play-test. |

A save from before this change has no record of a broken pot, so a player who broke one but has no Legendary banked sees the tip once. It shows for 5 seconds of play. Needs confirmation in the play-test, with the wording. |
| 426 | Where do Area 2's plastic worms go, so they are not in a clump (N2, N3)? | Built, provisional (issue #145; owner decision on issue #145, 2026-10-26): spread out from beside the top Rare reeds to before `tilapia-2`, at (488, 100), (585, 294), (670, 96), (855, 96), (1080, 96), and (1300, 144), at least 180 pixels apart, over about 1.7 screens of x and 194 pixels of depth. | 445 | Which presses play the button tap (S15)? | Built, provisional (issue #164): every UiButton press, by touch or click (on press, or on release for most buttons) and by Enter or Space on the button with the focus ring, found by one key listener that runs before the scenes; plus the area map's markers, which are drawn, not buttons, by touch or key. It is made per trip, not once at start-up, because the snack ladder, the Dash edge and the exit sound's once-per-trip belong to one trip (open question 467), and the trip events reach only the swim scene. | 479 | How do sounds asked for at the same moment play (S3, S4, S13)? | Decided (issue #165, owner decision 2026-10-27): they play in turn. One sound queue gives every sound its turn on the audio clock, and a sound's notes are made only when its turn comes. Snacks eaten at the same moment sound 80 ms apart, in the order eaten, so the ladder climbs and each last-3 twinkle is heard. Every find waits its turn, two finds of the same rarity included, and is never dropped by the repeat limit. The 60 ms repeat limit (S13) now spaces a repeat out instead of dropping it: the same sound asked for again within 60 ms starts 60 ms after the last. For every sound but snacks and finds, at most one such repeat waits at a time and a further one plays nothing, so button taps never pile up. The trip's sound router owns its trip's sounds and drops those still waiting when the trip ends; turning sounds off or hiding the page drops every waiting sound. A sound that has started rings out. The exit's "ta-da" waits in the sound queue until the last snack's sound has ended on the audio clock (owner decision 2026-10-27), so it can play on the pause screen if the game is paused right after the last snack. This changes the last sentence of open question 468, so two finds of the same rarity within 60 ms both play, one after the other, and the regrowth part of open question 473, so two legs back at the same moment play the regrowth twice, 60 ms apart. A sound that has started runs on the audio clock, so if the audio pauses (the page hides, both switches turn off, or the device interrupts it) it finishes when the audio runs again (owner decision 2026-10-27). |
| 480 | Which sounds does the hazard and rescue slice add, and how loud are they (S9 to S11, S13)? | Built, provisional (issue #166, ND-1): 16 sounds, each ported note for note from the sketches: `ripple`, `shellBlock`, `lullaby`, `bleh`, `snag`, `wriggleFree`, `exitClosed`, `matsOpen`, `catchSnack`, `shrimpFlick`, `paintShell`, `dashReady`, `potWobble`, `dashPot`, `rescueMeet` and `rescueSafe`. Each level in its data is the sketches' measured one: the catch 5.5, the shrimp flick 25.5, the Paint shell 3, Dash ready 7.5, the pot wobble 17, the Dash pot 12.5, the ripple 14, the Shell block 9, the lullaby 1.5, the "bleh" 18, the snag 7.5, the wriggle free 10, the closed exit 7.5, the mats opening 23.5, the rescue meeting 9 and the rescue's safe sound −1.5. Only the shrimp flick is a touch sound (S13). |

### Tuning values (starting points)

| Value | Starting point |
|---|---|
| Regrowth time | About 40 seconds per leg (was 20 until 0.47.2: legs grew back too fast) |
| Protected time | About 2 seconds |
| Telegraph wind-up | At least 0.8 seconds |
| Lunge cooldown | About 2.5 seconds (2.2 for Area 4's tilapia, and 2 for Area 5's tilapia and the garter snake) |
| Top speed by legs (4 / 3 / 2 / 1) | 100% / 90% / 75% / 60%. One leg must still beat the strongest current. |
| Tilapia in the canal | At least 3 |
| Tilapia in the pond | 4, with the closest routes about 465 pixels apart, `tilapia-1` and `tilapia-2`, since issue #132; they were 290 pixels apart before it (open question 373) |
| Carp in the pond | 2 since issue #132, `carp-0` and `carp-1`, playing `POND_CARP`: `CARP` with a chase of sight 220 pixels, leash 60, and speed 115 pixels per second. Every carp loop is at least 210 pixels from every tilapia route, the spacing chosen inside about 210 to 220 pixels and never 200 or less (owner decision on issue #132, 2026-10-25). Carp to carp is more than 240 pixels, about 1111 (open question 374). |
| Area 3 size | 6 x 1 screens, 2880 x 360 world pixels |
| Tilapia in Area 3 | 4, all before the herons' stretches, in two pairs of taller loops since issue #133, each loop reaching into two depth bands (open question 409): `tilapia-0` x 836 to 876 and y 110 to 190, `tilapia-1` x 750 to 790 and y 204 to 296, `tilapia-2` x 1486 to 1526 and y 110 to 190, and `tilapia-3` x 1390 to 1440 and y 204 to 296. The loops of each pair are 48.1 pixels apart and the pairs at least 514.2 pixels apart, because the owner's option A on issue #133 drops the tilapia-to-tilapia spacing for Area 3; before it, the closest routes were 290 pixels apart. |
| Carp in Area 3 | 2 (issue #133), `carp-0` and `carp-1`, after the herons in the predator list, playing `POND_2_CARP`: the common carp tuning with a chase of sight 220 pixels, leash 80, and speed 115 pixels per second (open question 410). `carp-0` on a floor loop under both heron stretches, through (2100, 200), (2200, 262), (2480, 262), (2480, 296), and (2100, 296), more than a heron's reach, 110 pixels, from every stretch; `carp-1` near the surface just past the second tilapia pair, x 1586 to 1614 and y 110 to 190, more than 230 pixels from every stretch. The carp loops are more than 240 pixels apart. |
| Area 4 size | 6 x 2 screens, 2880 x 720 world pixels, bed top at y 678. The Home Spot at (120, 200), its safe area x 40 to 300 from the surface to the bed; the exit at (2780, 625), radius 40 (open question 191). |

| Rarity chance per slot (Common / Rare / Epic / Legendary) | 65% / 27% / 6% / 2%. A slot whose rarity has none left takes a more common rarity with one left, nearest first, then only the next rarer one, else it stays empty (B4, issue #111). |
| Hiding spot weighting | 1 for the spot nearest the main swim path to 3 for the farthest, linear within each treasure's pool |
| Risk bands: route distance (Common / Rare / Epic / Legendary) | More than 200 / more than 140 and at most 200 / more than 70 and at most 140 / at most 70 pixels from the nearest tilapia route (B10). In Area 5, whose tilapia have their own lunge range of 150 pixels: more than 210 / more than 150 and at most 210 / more than 75 and at most 150 / at most 75 pixels; from a garter snake's stretch, with S = 110: more than 170 / more than 110 and at most 170 / more than 55 and at most 110 / at most 55 pixels; since issue #135, from a carp's loop, with S = 125: more than 185 / more than 125 and at most 185 / more than 62.5 and at most 125 / at most 62.5 pixels. |
| Turn clearance | Every hiding spot at least 60 pixels from every tilapia route waypoint (B10). Any predator turn uses the same 60 pixels under the generic rule in B10, such as both ends of a heron's or a garter snake's stretch; the tilapia's value is unchanged. |
| Risk weight | 1 to 2, linear in route distance within each treasure's pool, 2 at the rarity's risky side (nearer for Rare, Epic, and Legendary; farther for Common). Multiplied by the hiding spot weighting (B10). |
| Per-area tuning | Every area uses these treasure slot counts and chances unless it sets its own, keyed by area id |
| Stranded axolotl chance | 40% of trips, about 4 in 10, the default for every area that sets no chance of its own, unless one is already waiting (C2, C5; issue #111) |
| Snack regrowth speed-up | Each snack adds 10% of one leg's regrowth to the leg that is regrowing (about 4 seconds at the base time; it was 20% of a 20 second leg until 0.47.2). Never past a full leg. |
| Snack eat reach | A circle of 36 pixels radius, centred 25 pixels ahead of the axolotl's middle along its body, turning with its tilt. While the body squashes in a turn, the centre moves in with it. The same at every size. It covers the whole head of the largest size; smaller sizes reach a little in front of the snout. |
| Treasure hint range (Common / Rare / Epic / Legendary) | 150 / 115 / 85 / 60 pixels. Rarer is shorter. |
| Dash pot touch distance | 30 pixels |
| Dream colours by rarity | Common: Mint, Sky blue, Lavender (solid). About 3 times the fastest Area 1 snack drift, at least twice the Area 2, Area 3, Area 4, and Area 5 drift, and well under the slowest swim (90 pixels per second with one leg). In an area with mobile snacks (H4), max(40, 1.2 x the top speed of its fastest mobile snack), worked out once per trip: a tadpole's top speed is its speed, and a shrimp's is flick distance / (rest + flick distance / flick speed). The perk's 40 does not change. With the 72 pixel per second tadpole limit, every pull is at most 86.4 pixels per second, under one leg's 90. Phase 2 values in the H4 snack ramp table; the dev area's is 84 (A19; open questions 275 and 361). |
